  "short_description": {
    "id": "Rübezahl adalah roh gunung dari cerita rakyat Jerman, Polandia, dan Ceko yang menguasai cuaca Pegunungan Raksasa dan bisa berganti rupa.",
    "en": "Rübezahl is a mountain spirit of German, Polish and Czech folklore who rules the weather of the Giant Mountains and can change his shape.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "rubezahl-c01",
      "rubezahl-c09",
      "rubezahl-c11"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Rübezahl, dalam bahasa Polandia Liczyrzepa dan dalam bahasa Ceko Krakonoš, adalah roh gunung Pegunungan Raksasa di perbatasan Republik Ceko dan Polandia. Dalam legenda ia tampil sebagai raksasa, gnom, atau roh gunung yang suasana hatinya berubah-ubah: ramah kepada orang baik, mengajari mereka pengobatan dan memberi hadiah, tetapi membalas dengan keras siapa pun yang mengejeknya.",
      "en": "Rübezahl, Liczyrzepa in Polish and Krakonoš in Czech, is the mountain spirit of the Giant Mountains on the border between the Czech Republic and Poland. In legend he appears as a capricious giant, gnome or mountain spirit: friendly to good people, teaching them medicine and giving gifts, but taking severe revenge on anyone who mocks him.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "rubezahl-c01",
        "rubezahl-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dianggap penguasa cuaca pegunungan yang tiba-tiba mengirim petir, kabut, hujan, dan salju walau matahari bersinar. Wujudnya beragam: biarawan berjubah kelabu, penambang, tukang, hewan, atau benda seperti tunggul pohon, batu, dan awan. Ia juga kadang menyesatkan pengembara dan memiliki kebun tanaman ajaib yang dijaganya.",
      "en": "He is held to be the weather lord of the mountains, sending sudden lightning, fog, rain and snow even in sunshine. His shapes vary: a monk in a grey habit, a miner, a craftsman, an animal, or objects such as a tree stump, a stone or a cloud. He also sometimes leads walkers astray and owns a garden of wondrous herbs that he defends.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "rubezahl-c07",
        "rubezahl-c09",
        "rubezahl-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Asal namanya tidak jelas. Johann Karl August Musäus mengisahkan Rübezahl menculik seorang putri yang memintanya menghitung lobak (Rüben) di ladang, lalu kabur selagi ia menghitung; tafsiran lain mengaitkannya dengan kata Jerman Hulu Pertengahan zagel, \"ekor\". \"Rübezahl\" adalah julukan ejekan yang membangkitkan amarahnya; sebutan hormatnya antara lain \"Herr der Berge\".",
      "en": "The origin of his name is unclear. Johann Karl August Musäus told how Rübezahl abducted a princess who asked him to count the turnips (Rüben) in a field and fled while he counted; another reading links it to Middle High German zagel, 'tail'. 'Rübezahl' is a mocking nickname that rouses his anger; respectful names include 'Herr der Berge'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "rubezahl-c02",
        "rubezahl-c05",
        "rubezahl-c14"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam dongeng Ceko, Krakonoš dipandang sebagai penjaga Pegunungan Raksasa yang memberi orang ragi asam dan menciptakan sup kyselo; dari setan pembawa badai ia berkembang menjadi pelindung orang miskin di pegunungannya. Ia pertama kali digambarkan pada peta Martin Helwig tahun 1561, sedangkan kisahnya pertama dibukukan oleh Johannes Praetorius pada 1662.",
      "en": "In Czech tales Krakonoš is the guardian of the Giant Mountains who gave people sourdough and invented kyselo soup; from a storm-bringing demon he grew into a protector of the poor in his mountains. He was first depicted on a 1561 map by Martin Helwig, while his story was first written down by Johannes Praetorius in 1662.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "rubezahl-c01",
      "source_id": "rubezahl-s1",
      "quote": "is a folkloric mountain spirit (woodwose) of the Giant Mountains (Czech: Krkonoše , Polish: Karkonosze , hence his name in Czech and Polish), a mountain range along the border between the Czech Republic and Poland. He is the subject of many legends and fairy tales in German, Polish, and Czech folklore.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rübezahl (Polandia: Liczyrzepa, Duch Gór; Ceko: Krakonoš) adalah roh gunung dalam cerita rakyat dari Pegunungan Raksasa (Krkonoše, Karkonosze) di perbatasan Republik Ceko dan Polandia, tokoh banyak legenda Jerman, Polandia, dan Ceko.",
        "en": "Rübezahl (Polish Liczyrzepa, Duch Gór; Czech Krakonoš) is a folkloric mountain spirit of the Giant Mountains (Krkonoše, Karkonosze) on the Czech–Polish border, subject of many German, Polish and Czech legends."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rubezahl-c02",
      "source_id": "rubezahl-s1",
      "quote": "One interpretation is from the story How Rübezahl Got his Name by Johann Karl August Musäus, which recounts how Rübezahl abducted a princess who liked turnips (German: Rüben , singular Rübe). ... The princess asks him to count (zählen) the turnips in the field. While he counted, she escaped.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu tafsiran nama berasal dari kisah Johann Karl August Musäus: Rübezahl menculik putri penyuka lobak (Rüben), sang putri memintanya menghitung (zählen) lobak di ladang, dan kabur selagi ia menghitung.",
        "en": "One reading of the name comes from Johann Karl August Musäus's story: Rübezahl abducted a turnip-loving princess (Rüben), who asked him to count (zählen) the turnips in the field and escaped while he counted."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rubezahl-c03",
      "source_id": "rubezahl-s1",
      "quote": "The Polish name Liczyrzepa is a direct translation of the German name, introduced by Stanisław Bełza in 1898.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Polandia Liczyrzepa adalah terjemahan langsung nama Jerman, diperkenalkan Stanisław Bełza pada 1898.",
        "en": "The Polish name Liczyrzepa is a direct translation of the German name, introduced by Stanisław Bełza in 1898."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rubezahl-c04",
      "source_id": "rubezahl-s1",
      "quote": "In Czech fairytales, Rübezahl (Czech: Krakonoš ) gave sourdough to people and invented the traditional regional soup kyselo. In the Giant Mountains is a mountain named Kotel, which means cauldron. When fog rises from the valley at the bottom of the Kotel, people say that Krakonoš is cooking kyselo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam dongeng Ceko, Krakonoš memberi orang ragi asam dan menciptakan sup kyselo; bila kabut naik dari dasar lembah Kotel, orang berkata Krakonoš sedang memasak kyselo.",
        "en": "In Czech tales Krakonoš gave people sourdough and invented kyselo soup; when fog rises from the bottom of the Kotel, people say Krakonoš is cooking kyselo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rubezahl-c05",
      "source_id": "rubezahl-s1",
      "quote": "Rübezahl is a name of ridicule, the use of which provokes his anger. In fact calling him by this nickname was the one crime he would not tolerate. Respectful names are \"Lord of the Mountain(s)\" (Herr vom Berge, Herr der Berge)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rübezahl adalah nama ejekan yang membangkitkan amarahnya; sebutan hormatnya antara lain \"Penguasa Gunung\" (Herr vom Berge, Herr der Berge).",
        "en": "Rübezahl is a mocking name that provokes his anger; respectful names include 'Lord of the Mountains' (Herr vom Berge, Herr der Berge)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rubezahl-c06",
      "source_id": "rubezahl-s1",
      "quote": "In legends, Rübezahl appears as a capricious giant, gnome, or mountain spirit. With good people he is friendly, teaching them medicine and giving them presents. If someone derides him, however, he exacts a severe revenge. He sometimes plays the role of a trickster in folk tales.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam legenda Rübezahl tampil sebagai raksasa, gnom, atau roh gunung yang berubah-ubah; ramah kepada orang baik, mengajari pengobatan dan memberi hadiah, tetapi membalas keras orang yang mengejeknya, dan kadang berperan sebagai penipu.",
        "en": "In legend Rübezahl appears as a capricious giant, gnome or mountain spirit; friendly to good people, teaching medicine and giving presents, but taking severe revenge on those who deride him, and sometimes a trickster."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rubezahl-c07",
      "source_id": "rubezahl-s1",
      "quote": "Rübezahl is the fantastic lord of weather of the mountains and is similar to the Wild Hunt. Unexpectedly or playfully, he sends lightning and thunder, fog, rain and snow from the mountain above, even while the sun is shining.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rübezahl adalah penguasa cuaca pegunungan, mirip Perburuan Liar, yang tiba-tiba mengirim petir, guntur, kabut, hujan, dan salju walau matahari bersinar.",
        "en": "Rübezahl is the lord of weather of the mountains, similar to the Wild Hunt, sending lightning, thunder, fog, rain and snow even while the sun shines."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rubezahl-c08",
      "source_id": "rubezahl-s3",
      "quote": "Représenté pour la première fois en 1561 sur une carte du géographe Martin Helwig, il n'est pas antérieur au XVe siècle. Sa légende naquit probablement parmi les mineurs immigrants dans la région.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rübezahl pertama kali digambarkan pada 1561 di peta ahli geografi Martin Helwig, tidak lebih tua dari abad ke-15, dan legendanya mungkin lahir di kalangan penambang pendatang.",
        "en": "Rübezahl was first depicted in 1561 on a map by the geographer Martin Helwig, is no older than the 15th century, and his legend probably arose among immigrant miners."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rubezahl-c09",
      "source_id": "rubezahl-s2",
      "quote": "Insbesondere zeigt er sich als Mönch in aschgrauer Kutte (vergleichbar Wodan im Wolkenmantel), aber auch als Bergmann, Junker, Handwerker und in ähnlicher Gestalt und Verkleidung, aber auch in Tiergestalt oder als Gegenstand (Baumstumpf, Stein, Wolke).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menampakkan diri terutama sebagai biarawan berjubah kelabu abu, tetapi juga sebagai penambang, bangsawan muda, tukang, dalam wujud hewan, atau sebagai benda (tunggul pohon, batu, awan).",
        "en": "He appears especially as a monk in an ash-grey habit, but also as a miner, squire or craftsman, in animal form, or as an object (tree stump, stone, cloud)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rubezahl-c10",
      "source_id": "rubezahl-s2",
      "quote": "Bisweilen werden Wanderer von ihm in die Irre geleitet. Er soll einen Garten mit Wunderkräutern besitzen, den er gegen Eindringlinge verteidigt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang ia menyesatkan pengembara, dan konon ia memiliki kebun tanaman ajaib yang dibelanya dari penyusup.",
        "en": "He sometimes leads walkers astray and is said to own a garden of wondrous herbs that he defends against intruders."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rubezahl-c11",
      "source_id": "rubezahl-s3",
      "quote": "La légende veut que ce géant capricieux vive au cœur des monts des Géants, en Silésie, et puisse prendre des apparences différentes, dont celle d'un moine, d'un mineur, d'un chasseur sauvage ou d'une souche d'arbre.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, raksasa yang berubah-ubah ini tinggal di jantung Pegunungan Raksasa di Silesia dan bisa mengambil berbagai rupa, seperti biarawan, penambang, pemburu liar, atau tunggul pohon.",
        "en": "By legend this capricious giant lives in the heart of the Giant Mountains in Silesia and can take various forms, including a monk, a miner, a wild hunter or a tree stump."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rubezahl-c12",
      "source_id": "rubezahl-s1",
      "quote": "Rübezahl is seen to be the guardian of the Giant Mountains. ... Historically, his character has kept on expanding; from a bad demon causing storms and heavy snow, he evolved into a guardian of the poor people living in his mountains.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rübezahl dipandang sebagai penjaga Pegunungan Raksasa; dari setan jahat pembawa badai dan salju lebat, ia berkembang menjadi pelindung orang miskin di pegunungannya.",
        "en": "Rübezahl is seen as guardian of the Giant Mountains; from a bad demon causing storms and heavy snow he evolved into a guardian of the poor in his mountains."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rubezahl-c13",
      "source_id": "rubezahl-s1",
      "quote": "The Rübezahl story was first collected and written down by Johannes Praetorius in the Daemonologia Rubinzalii Silesii (1662). The character later appeared in Johann Karl August Musäus's \"Legenden von Rübezahl \" (1783)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Rübezahl pertama dikumpulkan dan dituliskan Johannes Praetorius dalam Daemonologia Rubinzalii Silesii (1662), lalu muncul dalam Legenden von Rübezahl karya Musäus (1783).",
        "en": "The Rübezahl story was first collected by Johannes Praetorius in Daemonologia Rubinzalii Silesii (1662) and later appeared in Musäus's Legenden von Rübezahl (1783)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rubezahl-c14",
      "source_id": "rubezahl-s1",
      "quote": "Another proposed etymology is Riebezagel, from a combination of the personal name Riebe and the Middle High German zagel, meaning \"tail\", from his pictorial representation as a tailed demon.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Etimologi lain yang diajukan adalah Riebezagel, gabungan nama diri Riebe dan kata Jerman Hulu Pertengahan zagel, \"ekor\", dari penggambarannya sebagai setan berekor.",
        "en": "Another proposed etymology is Riebezagel, combining the personal name Riebe with Middle High German zagel, 'tail', from his depiction as a tailed demon."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Vellamo adalah dewi air, danau, dan laut dalam mitologi Finlandia, istri dewa laut Ahti, yang dihormati para nelayan.",
    "en": "Vellamo is the goddess of water, lakes and seas in Finnish mythology, wife of the sea god Ahti and revered by fishermen.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "vellamo-c01",
      "vellamo-c02",
      "vellamo-c06"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Vellamo, juga dieja Wellamo, adalah dewi air, danau, dan laut dalam mitologi Finlandia yang dijuluki Veen emäntä, \"Nyonya Air\". Suaminya ialah dewa laut Ahti, dan dalam mantra memancing Finlandia keduanya diminta menggiring ternak mereka, yaitu ikan, ke dalam jaring.",
      "en": "Vellamo, whose name is also written Wellamo, is the Finnish mythological goddess of seas, lakes and water in general, called Veen emäntä, 'Mistress of Water'. Her husband is the sea god Ahti, and Finnish fishing spells ask the two of them to drive their cattle, meaning fish, into the nets.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vellamo-c01",
        "vellamo-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam lagu-lagu runik, Nyonya Air memiliki sapi-sapi yang kadang diberikannya kepada orang miskin; saat sapi-sapinya merumput di pantai, ia duduk agak jauh di atas batu sambil menyisir rambut. Ia dihormati khususnya oleh para nelayan, karena melihatnya walau dari jauh menjamin tangkapan melimpah. Ia juga digambarkan tinggal bersama Ahti di istana bawah laut Ahtola dan menggiring sapi ajaibnya ke permukaan saat kabut pagi untuk memakan buih laut.",
      "en": "In runic songs the Mistress of Water owns cows that she sometimes gives to poor people; while they graze on the beach she sits a little way off on a rock combing her hair. Fishermen especially revered her, since seeing her even from afar guaranteed a rich catch. She is also described as living with Ahti in the undersea palace of Ahtola and leading her magic cows to the surface in the morning mist to eat sea foam.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vellamo-c03",
        "vellamo-c04",
        "vellamo-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama Vellamo diduga berasal dari kata Finlandia velloa, \"mengaduk\", yang menggambarkan gerak air dan ombak; Kaarle Krohn dan Uno Harva menganggapnya nama yang terbentuk belakangan. Ia digambarkan tinggi dan cantik, bergaun biru dari lumut laut, kadang berekor ikan seperti putri duyung, sebagaimana pada lambang Päijät-Häme. Sebuah lagu runik Karelia Selatan menyebut Näkki sebagai keturunan Vellamo dan Ahti.",
      "en": "The name Vellamo is thought to come from Finnish velloa, 'to churn', which evokes moving water and waves; Kaarle Krohn and Uno Harva considered it a later-formed name. She is described as tall and beautiful in a blue dress of sea moss, sometimes with a fish tail like a mermaid, as on the arms of Päijät-Häme.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "vellamo-c01",
      "source_id": "vellamo-s1",
      "quote": "is the goddess of water, lakes and seas in Finnish mythology. She is called Veen emäntä 'Mistress of Water'.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vellamo (juga Wellamo) adalah dewi air, danau, dan laut dalam mitologi Finlandia, dijuluki Veen emäntä, \"Nyonya Air\".",
        "en": "Vellamo (also Wellamo) is the goddess of water, lakes and seas in Finnish mythology, called Veen emäntä, 'Mistress of Water'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vellamo-c02",
      "source_id": "vellamo-s1",
      "quote": "Vellamo's husband is the Finnish sea god Ahti. In Finnish fishing spells, Vellamo and Ahti are asked to bring their cattle (fish) into fishing nets.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Suami Vellamo adalah dewa laut Finlandia Ahti; dalam mantra memancing Finlandia, Vellamo dan Ahti diminta membawa ternak mereka (ikan) ke jaring.",
        "en": "Vellamo's husband is the Finnish sea god Ahti; in Finnish fishing spells Vellamo and Ahti are asked to bring their cattle (fish) into the nets."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vellamo-c03",
      "source_id": "vellamo-s1",
      "quote": "The Mistress of Water also had cows. She often helped poor people by sending them cattle. ... When the Mistress's cows were on the beach eating, she herself is sitting a bit further away on a rock, combing her hair.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nyonya Air memiliki sapi dan sering menolong orang miskin dengan mengirimi mereka ternak; saat sapinya makan di pantai, ia duduk agak jauh di atas batu sambil menyisir rambut.",
        "en": "The Mistress of Water had cows and often helped poor people by sending them cattle; while her cows ate on the beach she sat a little way off on a rock combing her hair."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vellamo-c04",
      "source_id": "vellamo-s1",
      "quote": "She was especially respected by fishermen; if you saw her, even far away, while seining, you were guaranteed to get plenty of fish.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia sangat dihormati nelayan; siapa yang melihatnya, walau dari jauh, saat menjaring dijamin mendapat banyak ikan.",
        "en": "She was especially respected by fishermen; seeing her even far away while seining guaranteed plenty of fish."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vellamo-c05",
      "source_id": "vellamo-s1",
      "quote": "The name \"Vellamo\" has been theorized to come from the Finnish word velloa , which means 'to churn', describing the movement of water and waves. Kaarle Krohn and Uno Harva considered the name Vellamo to be a later-formed name",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Vellamo diduga berasal dari kata Finlandia velloa, \"mengaduk\", yang menggambarkan gerak air dan ombak; Kaarle Krohn dan Uno Harva menganggapnya nama bentukan belakangan.",
        "en": "The name Vellamo is thought to come from Finnish velloa, 'to churn', describing the movement of water and waves; Kaarle Krohn and Uno Harva considered it a later-formed name."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vellamo-c06",
      "source_id": "vellamo-s2",
      "quote": "Vellamo est très respectée des pêcheurs, qui prient pour elle dans l'espoir de pêches miraculeuses. Vellamo peut également contrôler les vents pour aider les marins, elle contrôle les tempêtes et les vagues.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vellamo sangat dihormati nelayan yang berdoa kepadanya demi tangkapan besar; ia juga dapat mengendalikan angin untuk membantu pelaut serta mengendalikan badai dan ombak.",
        "en": "Vellamo is much respected by fishermen, who pray to her for miraculous catches; she can also control the winds to help sailors and controls storms and waves."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vellamo-c07",
      "source_id": "vellamo-s2",
      "quote": "Vellamo est décrite grande et belle, et porte une robe bleue faite de mousse de mer. Elle est parfois représentée avec une queue de poisson, comme une sirène",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vellamo digambarkan tinggi dan cantik, mengenakan gaun biru dari lumut laut, dan kadang ditampilkan berekor ikan seperti putri duyung.",
        "en": "Vellamo is described as tall and beautiful, wearing a blue dress of sea moss, and is sometimes shown with a fish tail like a mermaid."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vellamo-c08",
      "source_id": "vellamo-s2",
      "quote": "Vellamo vit avec son époux Ahti dans le palais sous-marin de Ahtola. Elle est la maîtresse de vaches magiques qui vivent sur des champs sous-marins. Parfois, lors des périodes de brumes matinales, elle mène ses vaches au-dessus de la surface pour manger l'écume des mers",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vellamo tinggal bersama suaminya Ahti di istana bawah laut Ahtola; ia pemilik sapi ajaib yang hidup di padang bawah laut dan kadang, saat kabut pagi, menggiring sapinya ke permukaan untuk makan buih laut.",
        "en": "Vellamo lives with her husband Ahti in the undersea palace of Ahtola; she owns magic cows living on undersea fields and sometimes, in morning mist, leads them to the surface to eat sea foam."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vellamo-c09",
      "source_id": "vellamo-s1",
      "quote": "Vellamo pictured as a mermaid in the coat of arms of Päijät-Häme.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vellamo digambarkan sebagai putri duyung pada lambang Päijät-Häme.",
        "en": "Vellamo is pictured as a mermaid on the coat of arms of Päijät-Häme."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vellamo-c10",
      "source_id": "vellamo-s1",
      "quote": "A South Karelian runic song calls the Nixie (Näkki ) the offspring of Vellamo and Ahti.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebuah lagu runik Karelia Selatan menyebut Nixie (Näkki) keturunan Vellamo dan Ahti.",
        "en": "A South Karelian runic song calls the Nixie (Näkki) the offspring of Vellamo and Ahti."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vellamo-c11",
      "source_id": "vellamo-s1",
      "quote": "Seeing the water goddess as a \"Water Mother\" lines with many other Finno-Ugric people's views, such as the Mordvin Ved-ava.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pandangan dewi air sebagai \"Ibu Air\" sejalan dengan pandangan banyak bangsa Finno-Ugria lain, seperti Ved-ava bangsa Mordvin.",
        "en": "Seeing the water goddess as a 'Water Mother' matches many other Finno-Ugric peoples' views, such as the Mordvin Ved-ava."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vellamo-c12",
      "source_id": "vellamo-s1",
      "quote": "A Karelian runic song describes a hero, such as Väinämöinen, catching a fish he tries to cut and eat. The fish jumps away, revealing herself to be a \"watery maiden of Vellamo\" and \"Ahti's only child\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebuah lagu runik Karelia mengisahkan pahlawan seperti Väinämöinen menangkap ikan yang hendak dipotong dan dimakannya; ikan itu melompat pergi dan mengaku \"gadis air Vellamo\" serta \"anak tunggal Ahti\".",
        "en": "A Karelian runic song tells of a hero such as Väinämöinen catching a fish he tries to cut and eat; it leaps away, revealing herself as the 'watery maiden of Vellamo' and 'Ahti's only child'."
      }
    }
  ],

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Alp adalah makhluk gaib dalam cerita rakyat Jerman yang kadang disamakan dengan vampir, tetapi perilakunya lebih mirip inkubus. Kata Alp adalah bentuk Jerman dari kata yang dalam bahasa Inggris menjadi elf; ia juga dikenal sebagai trud, mar, mahr, schrat, dan walrider. Artikel bahasa Jerman menyebutnya Nachtalb, sebutan belakangan untuk makhluk yang semula bernama Mahr, yang menindih manusia pada malam hari dan menebar kengerian.",
      "en": "The Alp is a supernatural being of German folklore sometimes likened to a vampire, though its behaviour is closer to an incubus. Alp is the German form of the word that became English elf; it is also known as trud, mar, mahr, schrat and walrider. The German article calls it Nachtalb, a late name for a being originally called Mahr that weighs on people at night and fills them with dread.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "alp-folklore-c01",
        "alp-folklore-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Alp biasanya jantan dan menyerang korban, kerap perempuan, pada malam hari dengan mengendalikan mimpi mereka. Serangannya disebut Alpdruck: ia duduk di dada orang tidur dan makin berat hingga korban terbangun ketakutan, sesak napas, dan tak bisa bergerak. Alp juga dikatakan mengasamkan susu, mengusutkan rambut menjadi \"simpul elf\", dan menunggangi kuda sampai kelelahan.",
      "en": "The Alp is usually male and attacks its victims, often women, at night by controlling their dreams. Its attack is called Alpdruck: it sits on the sleeper's chest and grows heavier until the victim wakes terrified, breathless and unable to move. Alpe are also said to sour milk, tangle hair into 'elf-knots' and ride horses to exhaustion.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "alp-folklore-c02",
        "alp-folklore-c03",
        "alp-folklore-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Alp terkenal karena kemampuannya berubah menjadi kucing, babi, anjing, ular, atau kupu-kupu putih kecil. Ia selalu memakai topi yang disebut Tarnkappe, yang memberinya kekuatan gaib dan membuatnya tak terlihat; Alp yang kehilangan topi itu akan menawarkan imbalan besar untuk pengembaliannya. Perlindungan terhadapnya antara lain sapu di bawah bantal, ladam besi di tiang ranjang, cermin di dada, baja, dan salib, serta menyumbat lubang kunci sebelum ia datang.",
      "en": "The Alp is known for turning into a cat, pig, dog, snake or small white butterfly. It always wears a hat called the Tarnkappe that gives it magic powers and invisibility; an Alp that loses the hat will offer a great reward for its return. Protections include a broomstick under the pillow, iron horseshoes on the bedpost, a mirror on the chest, steel and crosses, and stopping up keyholes before it comes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "alp-folklore-c05",
        "alp-folklore-c06",
        "alp-folklore-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Seorang anak bisa menjadi Alp bila ibunya menggigit kekang kuda untuk meredakan sakit saat persalinan yang sangat lama, atau bila ia lahir berselaput atau berambut di telapak tangan; bayi lahir mati juga diduga kembali sebagai Alp. Menurut artikel bahasa Jerman, Alp biasanya makhluk kecil hitam yang masuk lewat lubang kunci atau lubang dahan.",
      "en": "A child may become an Alp if its mother bites a horse collar to ease an extremely long childbirth, or if it is born with a caul or hair on its palms; stillborn infants are also suspected of returning as Alpe. According to the German article, the Alp is usually a small black being that enters through keyholes or knot-holes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "alp-folklore-c09",
        "alp-folklore-c10"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "alp-folklore-c01",
      "source_id": "alp-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "Alp is sometimes likened to a vampire, but its behavior is more akin to that of the incubus. ... The word Alp is the German form of the word that comes into English as elf, both descended from Common Germanic. It is also known by the following names: trud, mar, mart, mahr, schrat, and walrider.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Alp, makhluk dalam cerita rakyat Jerman, kadang disamakan dengan vampir tetapi perilakunya lebih mirip inkubus; kata Alp adalah bentuk Jerman dari kata yang menjadi elf dalam bahasa Inggris, dan ia juga disebut trud, mar, mart, mahr, schrat, dan walrider.",
        "en": "The Alp of German folklore is sometimes likened to a vampire but behaves more like an incubus; Alp is the German form of the word that became English elf, and it is also called trud, mar, mart, mahr, schrat and walrider."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alp-folklore-c02",
      "source_id": "alp-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "An Alp is typically male, while the mara and mart appear to be more feminine versions of the same creature. Its victims are often females, whom it attacks during the night, controlling their dreams and creating horrible nightmares (hence the German word Alptraum [\"elf-dream\"], meaning a nightmare).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Alp biasanya jantan, sedangkan mara dan mart tampaknya versi lebih feminin; korbannya sering perempuan yang diserangnya pada malam hari dengan mengendalikan mimpi mereka, dari situ kata Jerman Alptraum (\"mimpi elf\"), yaitu mimpi buruk.",
        "en": "The Alp is typically male, the mara and mart appearing more feminine versions; its victims are often women whom it attacks at night by controlling their dreams, hence the German Alptraum ('elf-dream'), a nightmare."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alp-folklore-c03",
      "source_id": "alp-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "Alpdruck is when an Alp sits astride a sleeper's chest and becomes heavier until the crushing weight awakens the terrified and breathless dreamer. The victim awakes unable to move under the Alp's weight.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Alpdruck terjadi saat Alp duduk mengangkangi dada orang tidur dan makin berat hingga korban terbangun ketakutan dan sesak napas, tak bisa bergerak di bawah bebannya.",
        "en": "Alpdruck is when an Alp sits astride a sleeper's chest and grows heavier until the terrified, breathless dreamer wakes, unable to move under its weight."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alp-folklore-c04",
      "source_id": "alp-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "Alpe also exhibit a tendency for mischief similar to elves, like souring milk and re-diapering a baby ... They also enjoy tangling hair into \"elfknots\" or chewing and twisting horse's tails. They will ride a horse to exhaustion during the night",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Alpe juga suka berbuat iseng seperti elf, misalnya mengasamkan susu, mengusutkan rambut menjadi \"simpul elf\", dan menunggangi kuda sampai kelelahan pada malam hari.",
        "en": "Alpe also show elf-like mischief, such as souring milk, tangling hair into 'elfknots' and riding horses to exhaustion at night."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alp-folklore-c05",
      "source_id": "alp-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "The Alp is best known for its shapeshifting abilities, similar to the creatures from werewolf lore. It may change into a cat, pig, dog, snake or a small white butterfly. It has also been said that it can fly like a bird and ride a horse.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Alp paling dikenal karena kemampuan berganti rupa, mirip makhluk dalam kisah manusia serigala; ia dapat berubah menjadi kucing, babi, anjing, ular, atau kupu-kupu putih kecil, dan konon bisa terbang seperti burung.",
        "en": "The Alp is best known for shapeshifting, like werewolf-lore creatures; it can become a cat, pig, dog, snake or small white butterfly, and is said to fly like a bird."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alp-folklore-c06",
      "source_id": "alp-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "The hat is known as a Tarnkappe (the literal translation being \"camouflage cap\" or \"cap of concealment\") which is simply a hat (or less commonly a veil) that gives the Alp magic powers and the ability to turn invisible while worn ... An Alp who has lost this hat will offer a great reward for its safe return.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Topi Alp disebut Tarnkappe (\"topi penyamaran\"), yang memberinya kekuatan gaib dan kemampuan tak terlihat selama dipakai; Alp yang kehilangan topi ini akan menawarkan imbalan besar untuk pengembaliannya.",
        "en": "Its hat is called the Tarnkappe ('cap of concealment'), giving the Alp magic powers and invisibility while worn; an Alp that loses it will offer a great reward for its return."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alp-folklore-c07",
      "source_id": "alp-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "Protections against an Alp include laying a broomstick under a pillow, iron horseshoes hung from the bedpost, placing shoes against the bed with the toes pointing toward the door, or placing a mirror on the chest. Steel and crosses are also used. ... Plugging up any holes, specifically keyholes, before a visitation will keep the Alp out.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penangkal Alp antara lain sapu di bawah bantal, ladam besi di tiang ranjang, sepatu menghadap pintu, cermin di dada, baja, dan salib; menyumbat lubang, terutama lubang kunci, sebelum ia datang akan mencegahnya masuk.",
        "en": "Protections include a broomstick under the pillow, iron horseshoes on the bedpost, shoes pointing to the door, a mirror on the chest, steel and crosses; plugging holes, especially keyholes, before a visit keeps the Alp out."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alp-folklore-c08",
      "source_id": "alp-folklore-s2",
      "quote": "Nachtalb ist eine späte Bezeichnung für ein Fantasie- und Sagenwesen, das ursprünglich „Mahr“ hieß und in der Nacht auf Menschen lastet (vgl. Albtraum) und ihnen Grauen einflößt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nachtalb adalah sebutan belakangan untuk makhluk khayalan dan legenda yang semula bernama Mahr, yang menindih manusia pada malam hari dan membuat mereka ngeri.",
        "en": "Nachtalb is a late name for a fantasy and legendary being originally called Mahr that weighs on people at night and fills them with dread."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alp-folklore-c09",
      "source_id": "alp-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "Children may become an Alp if a woman bites a horse collar to ease the pain during an extremely long and tortuous childbirth. Also, a child born with a caul} or hair on the palms may become an Alp. ... Stillborn infants are also suspected to return from the grave as Alpe and torment their family.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anak bisa menjadi Alp bila ibunya menggigit kekang kuda untuk meredakan sakit persalinan yang sangat lama, atau bila lahir berselaput atau berambut di telapak tangan; bayi lahir mati juga diduga kembali dari kubur sebagai Alp.",
        "en": "A child may become an Alp if the mother bites a horse collar during an extremely long childbirth, or if born with a caul or hair on the palms; stillborn infants are suspected of returning from the grave as Alpe."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alp-folklore-c10",
      "source_id": "alp-folklore-s2",
      "quote": "Es handelt sich gewöhnlich um ein kleines, schwarzes Wesen, das schlafende Menschen und Haustiere anfällt, selten auch Gegenstände. Es dringt durch Schlüssel- oder Astlöcher ein.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makhluk ini biasanya kecil dan hitam, menyerang manusia dan hewan peliharaan yang tidur, jarang juga benda, dan masuk lewat lubang kunci atau lubang dahan.",
        "en": "It is usually a small black being that attacks sleeping people and domestic animals, rarely objects, and enters through keyholes or knot-holes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alp-folklore-c11",
      "source_id": "alp-folklore-s2",
      "quote": "Dem germanischen Volksglauben nach steht ein „Alb“ für einen unterirdischen Naturgeist mit niederem Rang. Alben sind elbische Wesen, die den Kobolden und Zwergen nahe stehen",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut kepercayaan rakyat Jermanik, Alb adalah roh alam bawah tanah berderajat rendah; Alben adalah makhluk sejenis elf yang dekat dengan kobold dan kurcaci.",
        "en": "In Germanic folk belief an Alb is a low-ranking subterranean nature spirit; Alben are elf-like beings close to kobolds and dwarves."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alp-folklore-c12",
      "source_id": "alp-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "The Alp, in many cases, is considered a demon, but there have been some instances in which the Alp is created from the spirits of recently dead relatives, more akin to a spirit or ghost.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Alp dalam banyak kasus dianggap setan, tetapi ada pula yang tercipta dari roh kerabat yang baru meninggal, lebih mirip roh atau hantu.",
        "en": "The Alp is in many cases considered a demon, but in some instances it arises from the spirits of recently dead relatives, closer to a spirit or ghost."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Gandalf adalah kurcaci (dvergr) dalam mitologi Nordik yang disebut dalam daftar kurcaci puisi Völuspá dan dalam Edda Prosa.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gandalf, dalam bahasa Nordik Kuno Gandálfr, adalah seorang dvergr atau kurcaci dalam mitologi Nordik. Namanya muncul dalam apa yang disebut \"Daftar Para Kurcaci\" di puisi Völuspá dari Edda Puisi, serta dalam Edda Prosa.",
      "en": "Gandalf, Old Norse Gandálfr, is a dvergr or dwarf in Norse mythology. His name appears in the so-called 'Tally of the Dwarves' in the poem Völuspá of the Poetic Edda, and in the Prose Edda.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gandalf-mythology-c01",
        "gandalf-mythology-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Namanya berasal dari kata Nordik Kuno gandr, entitas gaib seperti tongkat sihir, dan álfr, \"elf\", sehingga dapat dimaknai roh pelindung yang memegang tongkat sihir. Nama yang sama juga dipakai untuk seorang raja Nordik dalam Heimskringla.",
      "en": "His name comes from Old Norse gandr, a magical entity such as a wand, and álfr, 'elf', so it can be read as a protective spirit wielding a magic wand. The same name was also borne by a Norse king in the Heimskringla.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gandalf-mythology-c02",
        "gandalf-mythology-c03",
        "gandalf-mythology-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "J. R. R. Tolkien kemudian menamai tokoh penyihirnya Gandalf menurut kurcaci ini, setelah semula memakai nama itu untuk pemimpin rombongan kurcaci yang akhirnya bernama Thorin Oakenshield.",
      "en": "J. R. R. Tolkien later named his wizard Gandalf after this dwarf, having first used the name for the leader of the dwarf party who was finally called Thorin Oakenshield.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gandalf-mythology-c06"
      ]

Tolkien's Middle-earth books, yet their roots lie in Norse mythology.",
      "claim_ids": [

    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, setengah-elf adalah keturunan elf dan manusia; contoh utamanya Skuld dan Högni. Skuld adalah putri Denmark dalam saga Hrólf Kraki, anak Raja Helgi dengan seorang perempuan elf yang ia bebaskan dari kutukan; nama Skuld berarti \"apa yang harus kaulakukan\".",
      "en": "In Norse mythology a half-elf is the child of an elf and a human; the main examples are Skuld and Högni.       "id": "half-elf-c02",
      "source_id": "half-elf-s1",
      "quote": "Half-elves became known in modern times mainly through J. R. R. Tolkien's Middle-earth writings but have origins in Norse mythology. A half-elf appeared in Lord Dunsany's 1924 book The King of Elfland's Daughter.",

    {
      "id": "half-elf-c03",
      "source_id": "half-elf-s1",
      "quote": "In Norse mythology, a half-elf is the offspring of an elf and a human. Major examples include Skuld and Högni. Högni was a hero in Thidreks saga, born to a human queen when an elf visited her while the king was away. Skuld was a Danish princess, as told in Hrólf Kraki's saga.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, setengah-elf adalah keturunan elf dan manusia; contoh utamanya Skuld dan Högni. Högni adalah pahlawan Thidreks saga yang lahir dari ratu manusia setelah elf mendatanginya saat raja pergi, dan Skuld adalah putri Denmark dalam saga Hrólf Kraki.",
        "en": "In Norse mythology a half-elf is the offspring of an elf and a human; major examples are Skuld and Högni. Högni was a Thidreks saga hero born to a human queen visited by an elf while the king was away, and Skuld a Danish princess in Hrólf Kraki's saga."

    {
      "id": "half-elf-c04",
      "source_id": "half-elf-s1",
      "quote": "King Helgi, sleeping alone as he had not been invited to King Adils's wedding, let in a ragged person on a midwinter night. ...       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Raja Helgi, yang tidur sendirian, membukakan pintu bagi sosok berpakaian compang-camping pada malam pertengahan musim dingin; perempuan itu berkata ia telah dibebaskan dari kutukan, dan tiga tahun kemudian perempuan elf itu kembali dan meninggalkan seorang putri di pintu raja bernama Skuld, yang berarti \"apa yang harus kaulakukan\".",
        "en": "King Helgi, sleeping alone, let in a ragged person on a midwinter night; she said he had freed her from a curse, and three years later the elf-woman returned and left a daughter at his door named Skuld, meaning 'what you should do'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "half-elf-c05",
      "source_id": "half-elf-s1",
      "quote": "She said that the King would gain the reward for breaking the curse, but that the King's people would suffer as the King had not done as she had asked. She never came back, but Skuld was always angry.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berkata raja akan mendapat imbalan karena mematahkan kutukan, tetapi rakyatnya akan menderita karena raja tidak menuruti permintaannya; ia tak pernah kembali, dan Skuld selalu marah.",

      "id": "half-elf-c06",
      "source_id": "half-elf-s1",
      "quote": "The scholar Hilda Ellis describes Skuld as evil, recalling that in the saga, Skuld used magic to raise an army against Hrólf, her half-brother. As quickly as Hrólf's warriors kill Skuld's men, they spring up, fighting more strongly than ever.",
      "locator": null,

      "statement": {
        "id": "Sarjana Hilda Ellis menggambarkan Skuld sebagai jahat: dalam saga ia memakai sihir untuk membangkitkan pasukan melawan Hrólf, saudara tirinya, dan prajuritnya yang dibunuh segera bangkit dan bertarung lebih kuat.",
        "en": "The scholar Hilda Ellis describes Skuld as evil: in the saga she used magic to raise an army against her half-brother Hrólf, and her men sprang up again as fast as they were killed, fighting harder."

      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Dunia Tengah, setengah-elf adalah anak Elf dan Manusia yang dapat memilih keabadian Elf atau hidup fana Manusia; sejak dipopulerkan Tolkien mereka dikenal luas dalam permainan peran, gim video, dan film, dan Dungeons & Dragons punya ras setengah-elf sendiri.",
        "en": "In Middle-earth half-elves are children of Elves and Men who can choose Elvish immortality or mortal life; since Tolkien popularised them they have spread through role-playing games, video games and films, and Dungeons & Dragons has its own half-elf race."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "half-elf-c09",
      "source_id": "half-elf-s2",
      "quote": "un semi-elfe ou demi-elfe est le fruit de l'union d'un elfe et d'une autre créature humanoïde, le plus souvent humaine.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam karya fiksi, terutama fantasi heroik dan fantasi tinggi, semi-elfe atau demi-elfe adalah hasil persatuan elf dengan makhluk humanoid lain, paling sering manusia.",
        "en": "In fiction, especially heroic and high fantasy, a semi-elfe or demi-elfe is the product of an elf's union with another humanoid creature, most often a human."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Landvættir adalah roh tanah dalam agama Nordik Kuno, kepercayaan rakyat kemudian, dan Heathenry modern, yang terikat pada tempat tertentu dan menentukan kesuburan tanahnya.",
    "en": "Landvættir are land spirits of Old Nordic religion, later folk belief and modern Heathenry, bound to particular places and needed for the land to be fruitful.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "landv-ttir-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Landvættir, \"roh tanah\", dikenal dalam agama Nordik Kuno, kepercayaan rakyat sesudahnya, dan Heathenry modern. Mereka terikat erat pada tempat tertentu, dan kesejahteraan mereka dianggap syarat agar tanah yang mereka huni subur. Dalam sumber Nordik Kuno mereka bisa berbahaya, sanggup mengusir orang yang tak diinginkan, tetapi juga dapat ditakut-takuti, misalnya dengan hiasan kepala kapal atau níðstöng.",
      "en": "Landvættir, 'land spirits', figure in Old Nordic religion, in the folk belief that followed it and in present-day Heathenry. Each is bound to a particular place, and the fruitfulness of that land was thought to depend on their wellbeing. Old Norse sources show them as potentially dangerous, able to drive away unwanted people, yet capable of being frightened by things like carved ship figureheads or a níðstöng.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "landv-ttir-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Hukum kafir lama Islandia melarang kapal mendekati daratan dengan kepala berukir yang menganga agar roh tanah tidak takut. Landnámabók menceritakan tak seorang pun berani tinggal di tempat Hjörleif menetap karena landvættir, dan para perempuan membawa makanan ke tumpukan batu agar landvættir membuat mereka makmur. Hukum Gulating Norwegia abad ke-13 melarang kepercayaan bahwa gundukan kubur, hutan, dan air terjun dihuni landvættir sebagai bidah.",
      "en": "The old heathen law of Iceland forbade ships to approach land with gaping carved heads lest the land spirits be scared. Landnámabók says that fear of the landvættir kept anyone from living on Hjörleif's former land, and women took food to stone piles hoping the landvættir would make them prosperous. Norway's 13th-century Gulating laws banned the belief that howes, woods and waterfalls were inhabited by landvættir as heresy.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "landv-ttir-c02",
        "landv-ttir-c03",
        "landv-ttir-c04",
        "landv-ttir-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam sebuah kisah saga, seorang penyihir utusan Raja Harald berwujud paus melihat semua gunung dan bukit Islandia penuh roh penjaga, lalu diusir di tiap sisi pulau oleh naga, burung besar, banteng, dan raksasa gunung (bergrisi). Keempat sosok ini kini dipandang sebagai pelindung empat wilayah Islandia dan tampil pada lambang negara serta koin króna.",
      "en": "In a saga account a warlock sent by King Harald in the shape of a whale saw all the mountains and hills of Iceland full of guardian spirits, and was driven off on each side of the island by a dragon, a great bird, a bull and a mountain giant (bergrisi). These four figures are now regarded as protectors of Iceland's four quarters and appear on the coat of arms and the króna coins.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "landv-ttir-c06",
        "landv-ttir-c07",
        "landv-ttir-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebagian sarjana menganggap landvættir roh chthonic atau arwah orang mati, sedangkan yang lain memandangnya roh alam karena kadang mereka menghuni tanah yang tak pernah didiami manusia. Mereka juga dikaitkan dengan elf, kurcaci, dan landdísir.",
      "en": "Some scholars consider landvættir chthonic spirits of the dead, while others see them as nature spirits because they sometimes live in land never settled by people. They have also been linked with elves, dwarfs and landdísir.",

    {
      "id": "landv-ttir-c01",
      "source_id": "landv-ttir-s1",
      "quote": "Landvættir (\"land spirits\" or \"land wights\") are spirits of the land in Old Nordic religion, later folk belief and modern Heathenry. They are closely associated with specific locations and their wellbeing is presented as being required for the land they inhabit to be fruitful. In Old Norse sources, they are depicted as being potentially harmful and capable of driving away unwanted individuals",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Landvættir (\"roh tanah\") adalah roh tanah dalam agama Nordik Kuno, kepercayaan rakyat sesudahnya, dan Heathenry modern; mereka terikat pada tempat tertentu, kesejahteraannya syarat kesuburan tanah, dan dalam sumber Nordik Kuno mereka bisa berbahaya serta mampu mengusir orang yang tak diinginkan.",
        "en": "Landvættir ('land spirits') are spirits of the land in Old Nordic religion, later folk belief and modern Heathenry; tied to specific places, their wellbeing is needed for the land to be fruitful, and Old Norse sources show them as potentially harmful and able to drive away unwanted people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "landv-ttir-c02",
      "source_id": "landv-ttir-s1",
      "quote": "Translation: It was the beginning of the preamble of the heathen laws that men should not take ships to sea with carved figure heads upon their sterns, but if they did, they should take them off before they came in sight of land",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pembukaan hukum kafir menyatakan orang tidak boleh berlayar dengan kepala berukir di kapalnya, dan bila ada, harus melepasnya sebelum daratan terlihat.",
        "en": "The preamble of the heathen laws said men should not sail with carved figureheads, and if they did, should remove them before land came in sight."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "landv-ttir-c03",
      "source_id": "landv-ttir-s1",
      "quote": "Later, the book states that no one dared live where Hjörleif had settled because of the landvættir.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Landnámabók kemudian menyatakan tak seorang pun berani tinggal di tempat Hjörleif menetap karena landvættir.",
        "en": "The book later states no one dared live where Hjörleif had settled because of the landvættir."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "landv-ttir-c04",
      "source_id": "landv-ttir-s1",
      "quote": "In this account, the women take food to stone piles or flat stones, hoping that in return the landvættir would make them prosperous.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam catatan itu, para perempuan membawa makanan ke tumpukan batu atau batu datar dengan harapan landvættir membuat mereka makmur.",
        "en": "In this account, women take food to stone piles or flat stones hoping the landvættir will make them prosperous."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "landv-ttir-c05",
      "source_id": "landv-ttir-s1",
      "quote": "The Norwegian Gulating laws, written in the latter half of the 13th century, made illegal the belief that howes, wooded areas and waterfalls, were inhabited by landvættir, considering it a heresy and belonging to the heathen religion.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hukum Gulating Norwegia dari paruh kedua abad ke-13 melarang kepercayaan bahwa gundukan kubur, hutan, dan air terjun dihuni landvættir, karena dianggap bidah dan bagian agama kafir.",
        "en": "Norway's Gulating laws of the later 13th century outlawed the belief that howes, woods and waterfalls were inhabited by landvættir, deeming it heresy and heathen."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "landv-ttir-c06",
      "source_id": "landv-ttir-s1",
      "quote": "King Harald told a warlock to hie to Iceland in some altered shape, and to try what he could learn there to tell him: and he set out in the shape of a whale. ... where he saw all the mountains and hills full of guardian-spirits, some great, some small.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Raja Harald menyuruh seorang penyihir pergi ke Islandia dalam wujud lain; ia berangkat sebagai paus dan melihat semua gunung dan bukit penuh roh penjaga, besar dan kecil.",
        "en": "King Harald sent a warlock to Iceland in altered shape; he went as a whale and saw all the mountains and hills full of guardian spirits, great and small."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "landv-ttir-c07",
      "source_id": "landv-ttir-s1",
      "quote": "First on the East side by a large dragon followed by snakes, toads and lizards who blew atter, or poison, at him, then on the North side by a great bird with many smaller birds, next in the West a large bull followed by beasts and other landvættir, and finally a huge bergrisi with an iron staff in the South",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia diusir di sisi timur oleh naga besar yang diikuti ular, kodok, dan kadal penyembur racun, di utara oleh burung besar, di barat oleh banteng besar beserta landvættir lain, dan di selatan oleh bergrisi raksasa bertongkat besi.",
        "en": "He was driven off in the east by a great dragon with poison-blowing snakes, toads and lizards, in the north by a great bird, in the west by a great bull with other landvættir, and in the south by a huge bergrisi with an iron staff."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "landv-ttir-c08",
      "source_id": "landv-ttir-s2",
      "quote": "Les quatre Landvættir sont aujourd'hui considérés comme les protecteurs des quatre quartiers d'Islande : le dragon (Dreki) dans le Nord-Est, l'aigle ou griffon (Gammur) dans de Nord-Ouest, le taureau (Griðungur) dans le Sud-Ouest, et le géant (Bergrisi) dans le Sud-Est. Les quatre Landvættir de l'Islande sont représentés sur les Armoiries de l'Islande et sur l'envers des pièces",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Empat landvættir kini dianggap pelindung empat wilayah Islandia: naga (Dreki), elang atau griffin (Gammur), banteng (Griðungur), dan raksasa (Bergrisi); keempatnya tampil pada lambang Islandia dan sisi belakang koin króna.",
        "en": "The four landvættir are now regarded as protectors of Iceland's four quarters: the dragon (Dreki), eagle or griffin (Gammur), bull (Griðungur) and giant (Bergrisi); they appear on the Icelandic arms and the reverse of króna coins."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "landv-ttir-c09",
      "source_id": "landv-ttir-s1",
      "quote": "Some scholars have suggested that landvættir are chthonic and spirits of the dead, but others have interpreted them as nature spirits, since they sometimes live in land that has never been populated.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian sarjana menduga landvættir adalah roh chthonic dan arwah orang mati, sedangkan yang lain menafsirkannya sebagai roh alam karena kadang menghuni tanah yang tak pernah didiami.",
        "en": "Some scholars suggest landvættir are chthonic spirits of the dead, while others interpret them as nature spirits because they sometimes live in never-populated land."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "landv-ttir-c10",
      "source_id": "landv-ttir-s1",
      "quote": "Landvættir have been variously connected by scholars to other beings believed to inhabit the land such as elves, dwarfs and landdísir, with which they were potentially identified at different points in history.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para sarjana mengaitkan landvættir dengan makhluk lain penghuni tanah seperti elf, kurcaci, dan landdísir, yang mungkin pernah disamakan dengannya pada masa berbeda.",
        "en": "Scholars have linked landvættir to other land-dwelling beings such as elves, dwarfs and landdísir, with which they may have been identified at different times."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "landv-ttir-c11",
      "source_id": "landv-ttir-s1",
      "quote": "As a compound, land-vættir has been variously translated as \"guardian spirits of a country\", \"land-spirits\", \"landwights\" or \"nature spirits\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai kata majemuk, land-vættir diterjemahkan antara lain sebagai \"roh penjaga negeri\", \"roh tanah\", atau \"roh alam\".",
        "en": "As a compound, land-vættir has been rendered 'guardian spirits of a country', 'land-spirits', 'landwights' or 'nature spirits'."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Helhest adalah kuda berkaki tiga dalam cerita rakyat Denmark yang dikaitkan dengan Hel, kematian, dan penyakit, dan konon berkeliaran di halaman gereja.",
    "en": "The helhest is a three-legged horse of Danish folklore associated with Hel, death and illness, said to roam churchyards.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Denmark, helhest (\"kuda Hel\") adalah kuda berkaki tiga yang dikaitkan dengan Hel. Ia berhubungan dengan kematian dan penyakit, dan menurut cerita rakyat pernah terlihat di berbagai tempat di Denmark. Ia juga diyakini muncul pada malam hari di pemakaman dan menandakan kematian siapa pun yang melihatnya.",
      "en": "In Danish folklore the helhest ('Hel horse') is a three-legged horse associated with Hel. It is connected with death and illness, and folklore records sightings of it in various places in Denmark. It was also believed to appear at night in cemeteries and foretold the death of anyone who saw it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "helhest-c01",
        "helhest-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut Jacob Grimm, helhest kadang digambarkan berjalan mengitari pemakaman dengan tiga kaki sambil membawa kematian, dan dari Schleswig tercatat ungkapan bahwa saat wabah, Hel berkeliling di atas kuda berkaki tiga membinasakan manusia. Grimm juga menduga bahwa sebelum kristenisasi, helhest semula tunggangan dewi Hel.",
      "en": "According to Jacob Grimm, the helhest was sometimes described as walking around the graveyard on three legs causing death, and from Schleswig comes a saying that in time of plague Hel rides about on a three-legged horse destroying men. Grimm also theorised that before Christianisation the helhest was originally the steed of the goddess Hel.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "helhest-c02",
        "helhest-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebuah legenda menyebut bahwa dahulu, sebelum jenazah manusia dimakamkan di suatu halaman gereja, seekor kuda hidup dikubur lebih dulu, dan kuda itu muncul kembali sebagai helhest. Di Katedral Roskilde orang dahulu meludahi sebuah batu sempit tempat helhest konon dikuburkan, dan di halaman Katedral Aarhus seorang lelaki yang melihat kuda itu dari jendelanya jatuh sakit lalu meninggal.",
      "en": "A legend holds that formerly a living horse was buried in each churchyard before any human body, and this horse reappears as the helhest. At Roskilde Cathedral visitors once spat on a narrow stone said to cover a buried helhest, and at the Aarhus Cathedral yard a man who saw the horse from his window fell ill and died.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "helhest-c03",
        "helhest-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Beberapa ungkapan Denmark abad ke-19 merujuk kuda ini, misalnya \"berjalan seperti helhest\" untuk orang yang melangkah berat dan berisik. Benjamin Thorpe mengaitkan ungkapan \"ia memberi kematian sekarung gandum\", yang dipakai bila seseorang selamat dari penyakit mematikan, dengan gagasan tentang helhest.",
      "en": "Several 19th-century Danish phrases refer to the horse, such as 'to walk like a Hel-horse' for someone who steps heavily and noisily. Benjamin Thorpe linked the saying 'he gave death a pack of oats', used when someone survives a near-fatal disease, to ideas of the helhest.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "helhest-c07"
      ]
    }

      "quote": "In Danish folklore, a helhest (Danish \"Hel horse\") is a three-legged horse associated with Hel. Various Danish phrases are recorded that refer to the horse.       "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Denmark, helhest (\"kuda Hel\") adalah kuda berkaki tiga yang dikaitkan dengan Hel, kematian, dan penyakit, dan disebut pernah terlihat di berbagai tempat di Denmark.",
        "en": "In Danish folklore the helhest ('Hel horse') is a three-legged horse associated with Hel, death and illness, said to have been seen in various places in Denmark."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "helhest-c02",
      "source_id": "helhest-s1",
      "quote": "According to Jacob Grimm, the helhest was sometimes described as going \"around the graveyard on three legs, causing death\", and from Schleswig, a phrase is recorded that, in time of plague, \"die (corrected by Grimm from der) Hel rides about on a three-legged horse, destroying men\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Jacob Grimm, helhest kadang digambarkan berjalan \"mengitari pemakaman dengan tiga kaki, membawa kematian\", dan dari Schleswig tercatat ungkapan bahwa saat wabah \"Hel berkeliling di atas kuda berkaki tiga, membinasakan manusia\".",
        "en": "According to Jacob Grimm the helhest was sometimes described as going 'around the graveyard on three legs, causing death', and from Schleswig comes the phrase that in plague time 'Hel rides about on a three-legged horse, destroying men'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "helhest-c03",
      "source_id": "helhest-s1",
      "quote": "Legend dictates that \"in every churchyard in former days, before any human body was buried in it, a living horse was interred. This horse re-appears and is known by the name of 'Hel-horse.'\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, dahulu di setiap halaman gereja seekor kuda hidup dikubur sebelum jenazah manusia mana pun, dan kuda ini muncul kembali dengan nama \"kuda Hel\".",
        "en": "Legend says that formerly in every churchyard a living horse was buried before any human body, and this horse reappears as the 'Hel-horse'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "helhest-c04",
      "source_id": "helhest-s1",
      "quote": "19th century scholar Jacob Grimm theorizes that, prior to Christianization, the helhest was originally the steed of the goddess Hel.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sarjana abad ke-19 Jacob Grimm berteori bahwa sebelum kristenisasi, helhest semula tunggangan dewi Hel.",
        "en": "The 19th-century scholar Jacob Grimm theorised that before Christianisation the helhest was originally the steed of the goddess Hel."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "helhest-c05",
      "source_id": "helhest-s1",
      "quote": "According to folklore, the Aarhus Cathedral yard at times features the Hel-horse. ... upon looking out the window he grew deathly pale, but would not detail afterward what he had seen. Soon thereafter he grew sick and died. At the Roskilde Cathedral, people in former times would spit on a narrow stone where a Helhest was said to be buried.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat, kuda Hel kadang muncul di halaman Katedral Aarhus; seorang lelaki yang melihatnya dari jendela menjadi pucat pasi, lalu sakit dan meninggal. Di Katedral Roskilde, orang dahulu meludahi batu sempit tempat helhest konon dikuburkan.",
        "en": "By folklore the Hel-horse at times appears in the Aarhus Cathedral yard; a man who looked at it from his window turned deathly pale, then fell sick and died. At Roskilde Cathedral people once spat on a narrow stone under which a helhest was said to be buried."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "helhest-c06",
      "source_id": "helhest-s2",
      "quote": "Le Helhest du folklore était censé apparaître la nuit dans les cimetières, et annoncer la mort de toute personne qui le voyait. C'est une créature surnaturelle en forme de cheval à trois jambes, mais parfois aussi de cheval sans tête.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Helhest dalam cerita rakyat diyakini muncul pada malam hari di pemakaman dan menandakan kematian siapa pun yang melihatnya; wujudnya kuda berkaki tiga, kadang juga kuda tanpa kepala.",
        "en": "The helhest of folklore was believed to appear at night in cemeteries and announce the death of anyone who saw it; it was a three-legged horse, sometimes also a headless one."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "helhest-c07",
      "source_id": "helhest-s1",
      "quote": "A number of Danish phrases connected to the horse were recorded in the 19th century, such as \"at gå eller træde som en Helhest\" (\"to walk or step like a Hel-horse\") for someone who takes strong or heavy steps and thus causes noise. ... Benjamin Thorpe connects the Danish phrase \"he gave death a pack of oats\" when an individual survives a near-fatal disease to notions of the Helhest",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejumlah ungkapan Denmark terkait kuda ini tercatat pada abad ke-19, seperti \"berjalan seperti helhest\" untuk orang yang melangkah berat dan berisik; sarjana abad ke-19 Benjamin Thorpe mengaitkan ungkapan \"ia memberi kematian sekarung gandum\" bagi orang yang selamat dari penyakit mematikan dengan helhest.",
        "en": "Several Danish phrases about the horse were recorded in the 19th century, such as 'to walk like a Hel-horse' for someone stepping heavily and noisily; the 19th-century scholar Benjamin Thorpe linked the phrase 'he gave death a pack of oats', said of a survivor of near-fatal illness, to the helhest."
      }

    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, menurut Gylfaginning, Annar (Nordik Kuno Annarr, \"yang kedua, yang lain\") adalah ayah Jörð, Ibu Bumi, dari Nótt, sang Malam.       "statement": {
        "id": "Snorri mungkin memakai sumber yang memaksudkan annar (\"yang kedua, yang lain\") sebagai Odin, karena ia sendiri baru menulis tentang Odin: \"Bumi adalah putrinya dan istrinya\".",
        "en": "Snorri may have used a source in which annar ('second, another') meant Odin, for he had just written of Odin: 'The earth was his daughter and his wife'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "annar-c05",
      "source_id": "annar-s1",
      "quote": "But in the Skáldskaparmál Snorri uses the form Ónar instead, giving \"daughter of Ónar\" as one of the kennings for Jörð.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Skáldskaparmál Snorri justru memakai bentuk Ónar dan memberi \"putri Ónar\" sebagai salah satu kenning untuk Jörð.",
        "en": "In the Skáldskaparmál Snorri instead uses the form Ónar, giving 'daughter of Ónar' as a kenning for Jörð."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "annar-c06",
      "source_id": "annar-s2",
      "quote": "Poiché Annarr è anche uno degli epiteti di Odino, e Snorri Sturluson afferma che Jǫrð fosse insieme figlia e sposa di quest'ultimo, è possibile che Annarr vada identificato con lo stesso Odino, ma è allo stesso tempo improbabile",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena Annarr juga salah satu julukan Odin dan Snorri Sturluson menyebut Jǫrð putri sekaligus istri Odin, Annarr mungkin identik dengan Odin, tetapi hal itu juga dinilai tidak mungkin.",
        "en": "Since Annarr is also an epithet of Odin and Snorri Sturluson says Jǫrð was both his daughter and wife, Annarr may be Odin himself, though this is also judged unlikely."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "annar-c07",
      "source_id": "annar-s1",
      "quote": "In the pseudo-historical genealogy of Odin's ancestors in the introduction to Snorri Sturluson's Prose Edda, a certain Athra is said to be he \"whom we call Annar\". What this refers to is unknown.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam silsilah semu-sejarah leluhur Odin di pengantar Edda Prosa karya Snorri Sturluson, seorang Athra disebut \"yang kita sebut Annar\"; maksudnya tidak diketahui.",
        "en": "In the pseudo-historical genealogy of Odin's ancestors in the introduction to Snorri Sturluson's Prose Edda, one Athra is said to be 'whom we call Annar'; what this means is unknown."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Identitas Annar",
        "en": "Annar's identity"
      },

      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Blóðughófi (dalam bahasa Nordik Kuno berarti \"Kuku Berdarah\") adalah kuda milik Freyr yang terkenal karena dapat melewati api dan kegelapan tanpa celaka. Namanya tercatat dalam beberapa þulur, yaitu daftar nama puitis untuk kuda.",
      "en": "In Norse mythology Blóðughófi (Old Norse for 'Bloody Hoof') is Freyr's horse, famed for crossing fire and darkness unharmed. His name is recorded in several þulur, the poetic name-lists of horses.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "blo-ughofi-c01",

    {
      "id": "Dalam Kálfsvísa, ia disebut di antara kuda-kuda lain sebagai tunggangan \"Pembunuh Beli\", julukan Freyr. Dalam Þorgrímsþula ia ditunggangi \"öflgan Atriða\", yang kadang ditafsirkan sebagai nama Freyr, walau ejaan yang mirip juga dipakai untuk Odin.       "en": "In the Kálfsvísa he is listed among other horses as the mount of the 'Slayer of Beli', a name for Freyr.       "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Durinn atau Durin adalah kurcaci yang disebut dalam bait ke-10 puisi Völuspá dari Edda Puisi, dan disebut lagi dalam Gylfaginning dari Edda Prosa. Ia kurcaci kedua yang diciptakan, setelah Mótsognir, kurcaci pertama dan terkemuka. Terjemahan Völuspá oleh Karl Simrock menyebut para dewa bermusyawarah tentang siapa yang akan menciptakan bangsa kurcaci dari darah Brimir.",
      "en": "In Norse mythology Durinn or Durin is a dwarf named in stanza 10 of the Völuspá in the Poetic Edda and again in the Gylfaginning of the Prose Edda. Karl Simrock's translation of the Völuspá tells of the gods taking counsel over who should make the race of dwarfs from Brimir's blood.",

      "en": "In Hervarar saga he and the dwarf Dvalin together forged the magic sword Tyrfing; in variant texts of the saga he is called Dulinn.       "id": "durinn-c01",
      "source_id": "durinn-s1",
      "quote": "is a dwarf according to stanza 10 of the poem Völuspá from the Poetic Edda, and repeated in Gylfaginning from the Prose Edda. He was the second created after the first and foremost dwarf Mótsognir.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Durinn (atau Durin) adalah kurcaci menurut bait ke-10 puisi Völuspá dari Edda Puisi, diulang dalam Gylfaginning dari Edda Prosa; ia yang kedua diciptakan setelah kurcaci pertama dan terkemuka, Mótsognir.",
        "en": "Durinn (or Durin) is a dwarf according to stanza 10 of the Völuspá in the Poetic Edda, repeated in the Gylfaginning of the Prose Edda; he was the second created after the first and foremost dwarf, Mótsognir."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "durinn-c02",
      "source_id": "durinn-s1",
      "quote": "He is also attested in Hervarar saga, where he forged the magic sword Tyrfing with the help of the dwarf Dvalin. In variant texts of the saga Durinn is known as Dulinn.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga tercatat dalam Hervarar saga, menempa pedang ajaib Tyrfing dibantu kurcaci Dvalin; dalam varian teks saga itu ia disebut Dulinn.",
        "en": "He is also attested in Hervarar saga, forging the magic sword Tyrfing with the dwarf Dvalin; in variant texts of the saga he is called Dulinn."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "durinn-c03",
      "source_id": "durinn-s2",
      "quote": "Durin, auch Durinn (altnordisch), war der zweite Zwerg, der von den Göttern in der nordischen Mythologie erschaffen wurde.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Durin, juga Durinn (Nordik Kuno), adalah kurcaci kedua yang diciptakan para dewa dalam mitologi Nordik.",
        "en": "Durin, also Durinn (Old Norse), was the second dwarf created by the gods in Norse mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "durinn-c04",
      "source_id": "durinn-s2",
      "quote": "Hochheilge Götter hielten Rath, Wer schaffen sollte der Zwerge Geschlecht Aus Brimirs Blut und blauen Gliedern. Da ward Modsognir der mächtigste Dieser Zwerge und Durin nach ihm.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam terjemahan Völuspá oleh Karl Simrock, para dewa bermusyawarah siapa yang akan menciptakan bangsa kurcaci dari darah dan anggota tubuh Brimir; Modsognir menjadi yang terkuat dan Durin sesudahnya.",
        "en": "In Karl Simrock's translation of the Völuspá the gods take counsel over who should make the race of dwarfs from Brimir's blood and limbs; Modsognir became the mightiest and Durin after him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "durinn-c05",
      "source_id": "durinn-s3",
      "quote": "Durin (el durmiente) y Mótsognir (de feroz rugido) se formaron de forma espontánea cuando Odín y sus hermanos Vili y Ve crearon el mundo a partir del cuerpo del gigante cósmico Ymir. ... Los dioses les concedieron la inteligencia y una apariencia humana.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Durin dan Mótsognir terbentuk dengan sendirinya ketika Odin serta saudaranya Vili dan Ve menciptakan dunia dari tubuh raksasa kosmis Ymir; para dewa lalu memberi mereka akal dan rupa manusia.",
        "en": "Durin and Mótsognir formed spontaneously when Odin and his brothers Vili and Ve made the world from the body of the cosmic giant Ymir; the gods then gave them intelligence and human appearance."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "durinn-c06",
      "source_id": "durinn-s1",
      "quote": "In the legendarium of J. R. R. Tolkien, Durin is the name given to several kings of the Dwarves including Durin the Deathless, the eldest of the Seven Fathers of their race.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam legendarium J. R. R. Tolkien, Durin adalah nama beberapa raja kurcaci, termasuk Durin yang Abadi, yang tertua dari Tujuh Leluhur bangsa mereka.",
        "en": "In J. R. R. Tolkien's legendarium Durin is the name of several Dwarf kings, including Durin the Deathless, eldest of the Seven Fathers of their race."
      }
    }

    "en": "Hafgufa is a giant sea creature of Norse literature said to inhabit Iceland's waters, so huge that it looked more like an island than a living thing.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Hafgufa (dari kata Nordik Kuno haf, \"laut\", dan gufa, \"uap\") adalah makhluk laut yang konon menghuni perairan Islandia, yaitu Laut Greenland, hingga ke selatan menuju Helluland. Ia disebut dalam risalah Norwegia abad ke-13 Konungs skuggsjá (\"Cermin Raja\") dan dalam daftar paus (þulur) di Snorra Edda.",
      "en": "Hafgufa (from Old Norse haf, 'sea', and gufa, 'steam') is a sea creature said to live in Iceland's waters, the Greenland Sea, and southward toward Helluland. It is mentioned in the 13th-century Norwegian tract Konungs skuggsjá ('King's Mirror') and in the list of whales (þulur) in the Snorra Edda.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "hafgufa-c02",

    {
      "id": "Dalam Konungs skuggsjá, sang raja menggambarkannya sebagai ikan raksasa yang lebih mirip pulau, jarang terlihat dan selalu di dua tempat yang sama, sehingga ia menduga hanya ada dua ekor yang mandul.       "en": "In the Konungs skuggsjá the king describes it as a huge fish more like an island, rarely seen and always in the same two places, so he guessed there were only two and that they were barren. It would belch up food that drew in fish, then shut its mouth and swallow them all at once.",

}

    "en": "Víðópnir is a bird of Norse mythology, described as a rooster perched at the top of the world tree Mímameiðr or Yggdrasil.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Víðópnir adalah burung yang menghuni puncak pohon dunia Yggdrasil, kadang ditempatkan di kening burung kosmis lain. Menurut puisi Edda Fjölsvinnsmál, Víðópnir atau Víðófnir adalah ayam jantan di mahkota pohon dunia, kadang digambarkan sebagai elang alap, yang duduk di antara kedua mata elang kosmis Hræsvelgr di puncak Mímameiðr, pohon yang dianggap sama dengan Yggdrasil.",
      "en": "In Norse mythology Víðópnir is a bird living at the top of the world tree Yggdrasil, sometimes placed on the brow of another cosmic bird. According to the eddic poem Fjölsvinnsmál, Víðópnir or Víðófnir is a rooster in the crown of the world tree, at times pictured as a falcon, perched between the eyes of Hræsvelgr, the cosmic eagle, on top of Mímameiðr, a tree taken to be the same as Yggdrasil.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vi-opnir-c01",
        "vi-opnir-c02"
      ]
