    {
      "id": "Ia berwarna keemasan dan bersinar seperti badai petir, menjaga keutuhan pohon dunia dan menjadi momok bagi raksasa Surt dan raksasa perempuan Sinmara. Ia hanya dapat dibunuh dengan senjata Lævateinn yang dijaga Sinmara, tetapi Sinmara hanya meminjamkannya kepada orang yang membawakan bulu ekor Víðópnir, sebuah tugas yang tampaknya mustahil.",
      "en": "He is golden and shines like a thunderstorm, guarding the integrity of the world tree and terrifying the giant Surt and the giantess Sinmara. He can only be killed with the weapon Lævateinn, which Sinmara keeps, yet she lends it only to someone who first brings her a tail feather of Víðópnir, a seemingly impossible task.",

      "id": "Karl Joseph Simrock menafsirkan namanya sebagai Windofnir, \"penenun angin\". Jacob Grimm membandingkan ayam di pohon ini dengan ayam di tiang Mei dan ayam angin di menara gereja, dan menduga para misionaris Kristen mengambil alih simbol kafir itu.",

    {
      "id": "vi-opnir-c01",
      "source_id": "vi-opnir-s1",
      "quote": "In Norse mythology, Víðópnir ( Old Norse pronunciation:",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik ada burung bernama Víðópnir.",
        "en": "Norse mythology has a bird named Víðópnir."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vi-opnir-c02",
      "source_id": "vi-opnir-s1",
      "quote": "According to the eddic poem, Fjölsvinnsmál, Víðópnir or Víðófnir [ˈwiːðˌoːvnez̠] is a rooster that inhabits the crown of the world tree, variously represented as a falcon, sitting between the eyes of the cosmic eagle Hræsvelgr at the top of the tree of life, Mímameiðr (Mimi's Tree), a vast tree taken to be identical with the World Tree, Yggdrasil.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut puisi Edda Fjölsvinnsmál, Víðópnir atau Víðófnir adalah ayam jantan di mahkota pohon dunia, kadang digambarkan sebagai elang alap, yang duduk di antara mata elang kosmis Hræsvelgr di puncak Mímameiðr, pohon yang dianggap sama dengan Yggdrasil.",
        "en": "According to the eddic poem Fjölsvinnsmál, Víðópnir or Víðófnir is a rooster in the crown of the world tree, variously shown as a falcon, sitting between the eyes of the cosmic eagle Hræsvelgr atop Mímameiðr, a tree taken to be Yggdrasil."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vi-opnir-c03",
      "source_id": "vi-opnir-s2",
      "quote": "Er ist von goldener Farbe und leuchtet wie ein Gewitter. Als nahezu unsterblicher Wächter wacht er über die Unversehrtheit dieses Weltenbaums. So gilt er auch als Schrecken des Riesen Surt aus der Feuerwelt Muspellsheim und der Riesin Sinmara aus der Eiswelt Niflheim",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berwarna emas dan bersinar seperti badai petir; sebagai penjaga yang nyaris abadi ia menjaga keutuhan pohon dunia, dan dianggap momok bagi raksasa Surt dari Muspellsheim dan raksasa perempuan Sinmara dari Niflheim.",
        "en": "He is golden and shines like a thunderstorm; as an almost immortal guardian he watches over the world tree's integrity and is a terror to the giant Surt of Muspellsheim and the giantess Sinmara of Niflheim."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vi-opnir-c04",
      "source_id": "vi-opnir-s2",
      "quote": "Widofnir kann man aber nur töten mit der Waffe Läwateinn (an. Lævateinn „Schadenszweig“), einem Zweig, der von Lopt („der Luftige“ = Loki) geschaffen wurde. Über die Waffe wacht Sinmara, die unterhalb der Totengitter wohnt. Sie leiht Läwateinn für den Kampf mit Widofnir aber nur dem, der ihr zuvor eine Schwanzfeder Widofnirs bringt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Widofnir hanya dapat dibunuh dengan senjata Lævateinn, ranting buatan Loki; senjata itu dijaga Sinmara, yang hanya meminjamkannya kepada orang yang lebih dulu membawakan bulu ekor Widofnir.",
        "en": "Widofnir can only be killed with the weapon Lævateinn, a twig made by Loki; Sinmara guards it and lends it only to one who first brings her a tail feather of Widofnir."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vi-opnir-c05",
      "source_id": "vi-opnir-s2",
      "quote": "Karl Joseph Simrock deutete den Namen Widofnir (an. Viðófnir/Viðópnir) im Sinne von Windofnir „Windweber“.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karl Joseph Simrock menafsirkan nama Widofnir (Viðófnir/Viðópnir) sebagai Windofnir, \"penenun angin\".",
        "en": "Karl Joseph Simrock interpreted the name Widofnir (Viðófnir/Viðópnir) as Windofnir, 'wind-weaver'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vi-opnir-c06",
      "source_id": "vi-opnir-s2",
      "quote": "Jacob Grimm stellte ihr Aussagen zum Hahn auf den Maibäumen, zum Wetterhahn auf den Kreuzbäumen der Wenden und zum Wetterhahn auf den Kirchtürmen vergleichend gegenüber. Er hielt es für möglich, dass die christlichen Missionare sich das heidnische Symbol umdeutend aneigneten",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jacob Grimm membandingkannya dengan ayam di tiang Mei dan ayam angin di menara gereja, dan menganggap mungkin para misionaris Kristen mengambil alih simbol kafir itu dengan makna baru.",
        "en": "Jacob Grimm compared it with the cock on maypoles and the weathercock on church towers, and thought Christian missionaries may have appropriated the heathen symbol with new meaning."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vi-opnir-c07",
      "source_id": "vi-opnir-s3",
      "quote": "Dans la mythologie nordique, Veðrfölnir (le dessèche-vent en vieux norrois) (orthographié également , Víðópnir ou Víðófnir) est le faucon qui se tient entre les yeux de Hraesvelg, l'aigle géant qui se tient tout en haut d'Yggdrasil.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu sumber menyamakan Víðópnir dengan Veðrfölnir, elang alap yang berdiri di antara mata Hraesvelg, elang raksasa di puncak Yggdrasil.",
        "en": "One source treats Víðópnir as Veðrfölnir, the falcon standing between the eyes of Hraesvelg, the giant eagle atop Yggdrasil."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Belsnickel adalah sosok pembawa hadiah Natal berbaju bulu dalam cerita rakyat Pfalz dan sekitarnya di Jerman, yang memberi kue kepada anak baik dan memukul anak nakal dengan ranting.",
    "en": "Belsnickel is a fur-clad Christmas gift-bringer of the folklore of the Palatinate and neighbouring areas of Germany, giving treats to good children and switching naughty ones.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "belsnickel-c01",
      "belsnickel-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Belsnickel, juga disebut Belschnickel, Belznickel, atau Pelznickel, adalah sosok pembawa hadiah Natal yang pemarah dan berbaju bulu dalam cerita rakyat wilayah Pfalz di barat daya Jerman di sepanjang Rhein, Saarland, dan Odenwald. Ia masih dipertahankan dalam komunitas Pennsylvania Dutch dan komunitas Jerman di Brasil.",
      "en": "Belsnickel, also called Belschnickel, Belznickel or Pelznickel, is a grumpy, fur-clad Christmas gift-bringer in the folklore of the Palatinate in south-western Germany along the Rhine, the Saarland and the Odenwald. He survives among the Pennsylvania Dutch and in German-Brazilian communities.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "belsnickel-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia berkerabat dengan para pendamping Santo Nikolaus dan mungkin bersumber dari Knecht Ruprecht, tetapi tidak mendampingi Nikolaus; ia datang sendiri dan memadukan sisi menakutkan dan sisi baik yang dalam tradisi lain terbagi antara Nikolaus dan pendampingnya. Ia lelaki berbulu, compang-camping dan kumal, membawa ranting untuk memukul anak nakal sekaligus saku penuh kue, permen, dan kacang untuk anak baik.",
      "en": "He is related to Saint Nicholas's companions and may derive from Knecht Ruprecht, but he does not accompany Nicholas; he comes alone and combines the frightening and kindly sides that other traditions split between Nicholas and his helper. He is a fur-wearing man, ragged and unkempt, carrying a switch for naughty children and pockets of cakes, sweets and nuts for good ones.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "belsnickel-c02",
        "belsnickel-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Para pendatang dari Pfalz membawa tradisi ini ke Pennsylvania, tempat Belsnickel sudah dikenal awal abad ke-19. Ia mendatangi rumah satu atau dua minggu sebelum Natal untuk memeriksa perilaku anak-anak, mengetuk pintu atau jendela dengan tongkatnya, lalu melempar permen ke lantai; anak yang terlalu cepat menyambar bisa terkena rantingnya.",
      "en": "Settlers from the Palatinate brought the custom to Pennsylvania, where Belsnickel was known by the early 1800s. He visited homes one or two weeks before Christmas to check on the children's behaviour, rapping on door or window with his stick and tossing sweets on the floor; children who grabbed too fast might feel his switch.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "belsnickel-c04",
        "belsnickel-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di Franken, sosok serupa bernama Pelzmärtel, gabungan tradisi hari Santo Nikolaus dan hari Santo Martin, dengan nama dari kata pelzen, \"memukul\".       "en": "In Franconia a similar figure is called Pelzmärtel, merging the customs of the feasts of Saint Nicholas and Saint Martin, the name coming from pelzen, 'to beat'.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "belsnickel-c01",
      "source_id": "belsnickel-s1",
      "quote": "Belsnickel (also known as Belschnickel, Belznickle, Belznickel, Pelznikel, Pelznickel, Bell Sniggle) is a crotchety, fur-clad Christmas gift-bringer figure in the folklore of the Palatinate region of southwestern Germany along the Rhine, the Saarland, and the Odenwald area of Baden-Württemberg. The figure is also preserved in Pennsylvania Dutch communities and Brazilian-German communities.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Belsnickel (juga Belschnickel, Belznickel, Pelznickel, dan lain-lain) adalah sosok pembawa hadiah Natal yang pemarah dan berbaju bulu dalam cerita rakyat Pfalz di barat daya Jerman di sepanjang Rhein, Saarland, dan Odenwald di Baden-Württemberg, dan juga dipertahankan dalam komunitas Pennsylvania Dutch serta Jerman-Brasil.",
        "en": "Belsnickel (also Belschnickel, Belznickel, Pelznickel and others) is a crotchety, fur-clad Christmas gift-bringer in the folklore of the Palatinate of south-western Germany along the Rhine, the Saarland and the Odenwald of Baden-Württemberg, also preserved among the Pennsylvania Dutch and German-Brazilians."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "belsnickel-c02",
      "source_id": "belsnickel-s1",
      "quote": "Belsnickel is related to other companions of Saint Nicholas in the folklore of German-speaking Europe. He may have been based on an older German myth, Knecht Ruprecht ... Unlike those figures, Belsnickel does not accompany Saint Nicholas but instead visits alone and combines both the threatening and the benign aspects",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Belsnickel berkerabat dengan pendamping Santo Nikolaus lain dalam cerita rakyat Eropa berbahasa Jerman dan mungkin bersumber dari mitos Knecht Ruprecht; namun ia tidak mendampingi Nikolaus, melainkan datang sendiri dan memadukan sisi mengancam dan sisi baik.",
        "en": "Belsnickel is related to other companions of Saint Nicholas in German-speaking folklore and may be based on the Knecht Ruprecht myth; but he visits alone rather than with Nicholas, combining threatening and benign aspects."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "belsnickel-c03",
      "source_id": "belsnickel-s1",
      "quote": "Belsnickel is a man wearing fur. He is typically very ragged and disheveled. He wears torn, tattered, and dirty clothes, and he carries a switch in his hand with which to beat naughty children, but also pockets full of cakes, candies, and nuts for good children.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Belsnickel adalah lelaki berbaju bulu yang compang-camping dan kumal, membawa ranting untuk memukul anak nakal sekaligus saku penuh kue, permen, dan kacang untuk anak baik.",
        "en": "Belsnickel is a fur-wearing man, ragged and dishevelled, carrying a switch to beat naughty children and pockets full of cakes, sweets and nuts for good ones."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "belsnickel-c04",
      "source_id": "belsnickel-s1",
      "quote": "The Belsnickel character originated in the Palatinate. When people immigrated to Pennsylvania, they brought their German traditions with them. Belsnickel was known in Pennsylvania in the early 1800s.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tokoh Belsnickel berasal dari Pfalz; para imigran ke Pennsylvania membawa tradisi Jerman mereka, dan Belsnickel sudah dikenal di Pennsylvania awal abad ke-19.",
        "en": "The Belsnickel figure originated in the Palatinate; immigrants to Pennsylvania brought their German traditions, and Belsnickel was known there in the early 1800s."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "belsnickel-c05",
      "source_id": "belsnickel-s1",
      "quote": "The traditional Belsnickel showed up at houses 1–2 weeks before Christmas ... He would rap on the door or window with his stick and often the children would have to answer a question for him or sing some type of song. In exchange he would toss candies onto the floor. If the children jumped too quick for the treats, they may end up getting struck with Belsnickel's switch.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Belsnickel tradisional datang ke rumah-rumah satu atau dua minggu sebelum Natal, mengetuk pintu atau jendela dengan tongkat, meminta anak menjawab pertanyaan atau bernyanyi, lalu melempar permen ke lantai; anak yang terlalu cepat menyambar bisa terkena rantingnya.",
        "en": "The traditional Belsnickel came to houses one or two weeks before Christmas, rapped on door or window with his stick, had children answer a question or sing, then tossed sweets on the floor; those who jumped too fast might be struck with his switch."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "belsnickel-c06",
      "source_id": "belsnickel-s2",
      "quote": "Der Pelzmärtel (auch Pelzemärtel, Pelzermärtel, Pelzamärdl, Belzermärdl, Bulzermärtl, Belzemärdl oder Pelzmartin) ist ein in Teilen Frankens (Süddeutschland) verbreiteter Name für den vorweihnachtlichen Gabenbringer. Regional tritt er auch als Pelznickel auf.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pelzmärtel (juga Pelzmartin dan sebagainya) adalah nama pembawa hadiah sebelum Natal yang umum di sebagian Franken (Jerman selatan), dan di beberapa daerah disebut Pelznickel.",
        "en": "Pelzmärtel (also Pelzmartin and others) is a name for the pre-Christmas gift-bringer common in parts of Franconia (southern Germany), regionally also Pelznickel."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "belsnickel-c07",
      "source_id": "belsnickel-s2",
      "quote": "Pelzmärtel leitet sich von Pelz (vom westmitteldeutschen „pelzen“, was so viel wie „prügeln“ bedeutet) und der fränkischen Verkleinerungsform für Martin, „Märtel“ bzw. „Martel“; „Nickel“ ist entsprechend die Verkleinerungsform für Nikolaus.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pelzmärtel berasal dari Pelz (dari kata Jerman Tengah Barat pelzen, \"memukul\") dan Märtel, bentuk kecil nama Martin; Nickel adalah bentuk kecil Nikolaus.",
        "en": "Pelzmärtel comes from Pelz (West Central German pelzen, 'to beat') and Märtel, a diminutive of Martin; Nickel is the diminutive of Nikolaus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "belsnickel-c08",
      "source_id": "belsnickel-s2",
      "quote": "wie Guabiruba, im Staat Santa Catarina, ist er unter dem Namen Pelznickel bekannt. ... Der Pelznickel erscheint an zwei Tagen im Jahr, an Nikolaus und an Heiligabend. Den Rest des Jahres lebe er im Wald. Darum wird er dort auch als Papai Noel do Mato, „Weihnachtsmann des Walds“, bezeichnet.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di kota-kota Brasil seperti Guabiruba, Santa Catarina, ia dikenal sebagai Pelznickel yang muncul dua hari setahun, pada hari Nikolaus dan malam Natal; sepanjang sisa tahun ia konon tinggal di hutan sehingga dijuluki Papai Noel do Mato, \"Sinterklas hutan\".",
        "en": "In Brazilian towns such as Guabiruba, Santa Catarina, he is known as Pelznickel, appearing two days a year, on St Nicholas's day and Christmas Eve; said to live in the forest the rest of the year, he is called Papai Noel do Mato, 'Santa of the forest'."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Elwetritsch adalah makhluk khayalan mirip burung dari barat daya Jerman, terutama Pfalz, yang konon lahir dari persilangan unggas dengan kobold dan peri hutan.",
    "en": "The Elwetritsch is an imaginary bird-like creature of south-western Germany, especially the Palatinate, said to come from crossing poultry with forest kobolds and elves.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Elwetritsch, juga Elwetrittche atau Ilwedritsch dan dalam bahasa Latin semu ilmiah Bestia palatinensis, adalah makhluk khayalan mirip burung yang dikabarkan di barat daya Jerman, terutama di Pfalz. Ia dipandang sebagai padanan lokal makhluk khayalan daerah lain, seperti Wolpertinger dari Bayern atau Rasselbock dari Thüringen. Bersama para emigran, kepercayaan ini menyebar ke Eropa Timur dan Pennsylvania pada abad ke-18 dan ke Brasil pada abad ke-19.",
      "en": "The Elwetritsch, also Elwetrittche or Ilwedritsch and in mock-scientific Latin Bestia palatinensis, is an imaginary bird-like creature reported in south-western Germany, especially the Palatinate. It is seen as the local counterpart of imaginary creatures elsewhere, such as the Bavarian Wolpertinger or the Thuringian Rasselbock. With emigrants the belief spread to Eastern Europe and Pennsylvania in the 18th century and to Brazil in the 19th.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "elwetritsch-c01",
        "elwetritsch-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Elwetritsch digambarkan mirip ayam, tetapi hampir tak bisa memakai sayapnya sehingga harus tinggal di semak belukar atau di bawah tanaman anggur; kadang ia digambarkan bertanduk rusa dan berparuh sangat panjang. Konon ia lahir dari persilangan ayam, bebek, dan angsa dengan kobold dan peri penghuni hutan, sehingga bertelur, tetapi telurnya tumbuh selama masa pengeraman.",
      "en": "The Elwetritsch is described as chicken-like but barely able to use its wings, so it must live in underbrush or under grapevines; it is sometimes shown with deer antlers and a very long beak. It is said to come from crossing chickens, ducks and geese with forest kobolds and elves, and so it lays eggs, though these grow while being brooded.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "elwetritsch-c03",
        "elwetritsch-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di banyak desa Pfalz, wisatawan ditawari izin berburu Elwetritsch sebagai hiburan. Waktu berburu terbaik adalah malam gelap saat bulan baru: penangkap membawa karung, lampu minyak, dan pentung, sementara para penghalau berteriak \"tritsch, tritsch\" dan memukul pohon agar makhluk itu lari ke dalam karung. Penangkap yang tak curiga kadang ditinggalkan diam-diam di luar sampai pulang kedinginan tanpa hasil.",
      "en": "In many Palatinate villages tourists are offered an Elwetritsch hunting licence as a joke. The best time to hunt is a dark new-moon night: the catcher carries a sack, an oil lamp and a club, while beaters shout 'tritsch, tritsch' and strike trees to drive the creatures into the sack. The unsuspecting catcher is sometimes secretly left outside until he comes home frozen and empty-handed.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "elwetritsch-c05",
        "elwetritsch-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Asal namanya diperdebatkan; salah satu tafsiran mengaitkan bagian pertamanya dengan elf atau Elben, roh hutan dalam mitologi Jermanik. Catatan tertulis tertua berasal dari Luckenbach, Texas, tempat pada 1847 para pemburu Jerman konon melihat seekor \"Elfendritschenwolpertinger\".",
      "en": "The origin of the name is disputed; one reading links its first part to elves or Elben, forest spirits of Germanic mythology. The oldest written mention comes from Luckenbach, Texas, where in 1847 German hunters reportedly saw an 'Elfendritschenwolpertinger'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "elwetritsch-c07",
        "elwetritsch-c08"
      ]

        "id": "Elwetritsch (dalam Latin semu ilmiah Bestia palatinensis) adalah makhluk khayalan mirip burung dari barat daya Jerman, terutama Pfalz, dan dianggap padanan lokal Wolpertinger dari Bayern atau Rasselbock dari Thüringen.",

      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Elwetritsch digambarkan mirip ayam, tetapi konon hampir tak bisa memakai sayapnya sehingga harus tinggal di semak belukar atau di bawah tanaman anggur; kadang digambarkan bertanduk rusa dan berparuh sangat panjang.",

    {
      "id": "elwetritsch-c04",
      "source_id": "elwetritsch-s2",
      "quote": "Elwetritschen sollen aus Kreuzungen von Hühnern, Enten und Gänsen mit im Wald lebenden Kobolden und Elfen stammen. Als Geflügelabkömmlinge legen sie selbstverständlich Eier, die allerdings wegen der Waldgeisterherkunft während der Brutzeit wachsen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Elwetritsch konon berasal dari persilangan ayam, bebek, dan angsa dengan kobold dan peri penghuni hutan; sebagai keturunan unggas ia bertelur, tetapi karena darah roh hutannya, telurnya tumbuh selama masa pengeraman.",
        "en": "Elwetritschen are said to come from crossing chickens, ducks and geese with forest kobolds and elves; as poultry offspring they lay eggs, which because of their forest-spirit descent grow during brooding."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "elwetritsch-c05",
      "source_id": "elwetritsch-s2",
      "quote": "In etlichen pfälzischen Gemeinden wird Touristen als launiger Zeitvertreib der Erwerb eines Elwetritschen-Jagdscheins angeboten. ... Die günstigste Jagdzeit sind dunkle Neumond­nächte. Bei einer Variante der Jagd benötigt der Fänger einen Sack, eine Öllampe und einen Knüppel.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di banyak desa Pfalz, wisatawan ditawari izin berburu Elwetritsch sebagai hiburan; waktu berburu terbaik malam gelap saat bulan baru, dan dalam satu cara penangkap memerlukan karung, lampu minyak, dan pentung.",
        "en": "In many Palatinate villages tourists are offered an Elwetritsch hunting licence for fun; the best time is dark new-moon nights, and in one variant the catcher needs a sack, an oil lamp and a club."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "elwetritsch-c06",
      "source_id": "elwetritsch-s2",
      "quote": "Treiber versuchen, durch lautes „Tritsch, tritsch“-Rufen und durch Stockschläge gegen Bäume oder Weinbergspfähle die Elwetritschen aufzuscheuchen, damit sie in den Sack des Fängers flüchten. ... Der häufig ahnungslose Fänger und Jagdscheinaspirant wird gelegentlich heimlich im Freien zurückgelassen, bis er endlich durchgefroren – und ohne Jagdbeute – heimfindet.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para penghalau berteriak \"tritsch, tritsch\" dan memukul pohon atau tiang kebun anggur agar Elwetritsch lari ke karung; penangkap yang sering tak curiga kadang ditinggalkan diam-diam di luar sampai pulang kedinginan tanpa hasil.",
        "en": "Beaters shout 'tritsch, tritsch' and strike trees or vineyard posts to flush the creatures into the sack; the often unsuspecting catcher is sometimes secretly left outdoors until he gets home frozen and empty-handed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "elwetritsch-c07",
      "source_id": "elwetritsch-s2",
      "quote": "Eine Deutung geht davon aus, die Herkunft des zweiten Wortteils sei unklar und strittig, während der erste Bezug nehme auf die Elfen beziehungsweise Elben als weibliche Waldgeister aus der germanischen Mythologie.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu tafsiran menganggap asal bagian kedua nama itu tidak jelas, sedangkan bagian pertama merujuk pada elf atau Elben, roh hutan perempuan dalam mitologi Jermanik.",
        "en": "One reading holds the second part of the name unclear, while the first refers to elves or Elben, female forest spirits of Germanic mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "elwetritsch-c08",
      "source_id": "elwetritsch-s1",
      "quote": "The oldest written mention to date comes from Luckenbach, Texas. In 1847, sources reported that an “Elfendritschenwolpertinger” had been seen by German hunters in the woods around South Grape Creek.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Catatan tertulis tertua berasal dari Luckenbach, Texas: pada 1847 sumber melaporkan para pemburu Jerman melihat seekor \"Elfendritschenwolpertinger\" di hutan sekitar South Grape Creek.",
        "en": "The oldest written mention comes from Luckenbach, Texas: in 1847 sources reported German hunters had seen an 'Elfendritschenwolpertinger' in the woods around South Grape Creek."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "elwetritsch-c09",
      "source_id": "elwetritsch-s2",
      "quote": "Um sich vor Angriffen der Elwetritschen zu schützen, trinken die Jäger vor und während der Jagd reichlich Alkohol, dessen Geruch angeblich die Elwetritschen auf Distanz hält.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Untuk melindungi diri dari serangan Elwetritsch, para pemburu minum banyak alkohol sebelum dan selama perburuan, yang baunya konon menjauhkan makhluk itu.",
        "en": "To protect themselves from Elwetritsch attacks, hunters drink plenty of alcohol before and during the hunt, its smell supposedly keeping the creatures away."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",

    {

      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}

    "id": "Trout berbulu adalah makhluk legenda dalam cerita rakyat Amerika dan Islandia: ikan trout yang menumbuhkan bulu tebal untuk menjaga panas tubuh di air yang sangat dingin.",
    "en": "The fur-bearing trout is a legendary creature of American and Icelandic folklore: a trout that grew a thick coat of fur to keep warm in very cold water.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Trout berbulu adalah makhluk legenda dalam cerita rakyat Amerika dan Islandia. Menurut kisahnya, air danau dan sungai tempat ia hidup begitu dingin sehingga ikan ini menumbuhkan bulu tebal untuk menjaga panas tubuhnya; teori lain menyebut empat kendi atau dua botol tonik rambut tumpah ke Sungai Arkansas. Ia konon ditemukan di Sungai Arkansas, Amerika Utara bagian utara, dan Islandia.",
      "en": "The fur-bearing trout is a legendary creature of American and Icelandic folklore. In the tale, the lakes and rivers it lives in are so cold that the fish grew thick fur to keep warm; another version blames four jugs or two bottles of hair tonic spilled into the Arkansas River. It is supposedly found in the Arkansas River, northern North America and Iceland.",

        "fur-bearing-trout-c01",
        "fur-bearing-trout-c02"

      "id": "Kisah ikan berbulu berasal dari abad ke-17, ketika seorang imigran Skotlandia menulis kepada kerabatnya tentang \"hewan dan ikan berbulu\" yang berlimpah di Dunia Baru.     {

    {

      "claim_ids": [
        "hamingja-c02",

      ]
    }
  ],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hamingja adalah roh pelindung perempuan dalam mitologi Nordik yang menyertai seseorang dan menentukan nasib baik serta kebahagiaannya, lalu diwariskan dalam keluarga.",
    "en": "The hamingja is a female guardian spirit of Norse mythology that accompanies a person, decides their luck and happiness, and passes down within a family.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "hamingja-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Hamingja adalah jenis roh pelindung perempuan dalam mitologi Nordik yang diyakini menyertai seseorang dan menentukan untung serta kebahagiaannya; karena itu kata ini juga berarti kebahagiaan, makna yang dipakai dalam bahasa Islandia modern. Saat orang itu meninggal, hamingja beralih kepada anggota keluarga tercinta sehingga dapat menyertai satu keluarga turun-temurun.",
      "en": "The hamingja is a kind of female guardian spirit in Norse mythology believed to accompany a person and decide their fortune and happiness; for that reason the word also came to mean happiness, its sense in modern Icelandic. When the person died, the hamingja went to a beloved relative and could thus follow a family over generations.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hamingja-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Hamingja bahkan bisa dipinjamkan: Hjalti Skeggiason, sebelum berlayar dalam perjalanan berbahaya, meminta Olaf II dari Norwegia meminjamkan hamingja-nya. Ia biasanya muncul dalam tidur berwujud hewan, dan bisa juga merupakan roh orang yang sedang tidur dalam rupa hewan, seperti Bödvar Bjarki dalam saga Hrólfr Kraki.",
      "en": "A hamingja could even be lent: Hjalti Skeggiason, about to set out on a dangerous voyage, asked Olaf II of Norway to lend him his. It usually appears in sleep in animal form, and can also be the spirit of a sleeping person in animal shape, like Bödvar Bjarki in the saga of Hrólfr Kraki.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hamingja-c01",
        "hamingja-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kata hamingja (Nordik Kuno, \"keberuntungan\") merujuk pada dua konsep: perwujudan untung baik seseorang atau keluarga, dan rupa yang berubah dari para pengubah wujud. Andy Orchard dan Rudolf Simek mencatat kemiripannya dengan fylgja. Dalam kepercayaan Nordik, ia juga dipahami sebagai kekuatan pelindung keluarga yang dapat digambarkan sebagai perempuan raksasa dan mengawasi perilaku keturunannya. Hamingja memberi kekayaan, keberhasilan, dan kekuasaan yang bertambah sepanjang hidup, dan kadang kata ini dipakai untuk kehormatan.",
      "en": "The word hamingja (Old Norse, 'luck') covers two ideas: the personified good fortune of a person or family, and the altered appearance of shape-shifters. Andy Orchard and Rudolf Simek note parallels with the fylgja. In Norse belief it was also understood as a family's guardian force, which could be pictured as a gigantic woman watching over the conduct of her descendants. The hamingja brings wealth, success and power that build up over a lifetime, and the word is sometimes used for honour.",

    {
      "id": "hamingja-c01",
      "source_id": "hamingja-s1",
      "quote": "The hamingja was a type of female guardian spirit in Norse mythology. It was believed that she accompanied a person and decided their luck and happiness. Consequently, the name was also used to indicate happiness, and that is what it means in modern Icelandic. When a person died, the hamingja passed to a beloved family member",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hamingja adalah jenis roh pelindung perempuan dalam mitologi Nordik yang diyakini menyertai seseorang dan menentukan untung serta kebahagiaannya; karena itu namanya juga berarti kebahagiaan, seperti dalam bahasa Islandia modern, dan saat orang itu meninggal, hamingja beralih kepada anggota keluarga tercinta.",
        "en": "The hamingja was a type of female guardian spirit in Norse mythology believed to accompany a person and decide their luck and happiness; hence the word also means happiness, as in modern Icelandic, and when the person died it passed to a beloved family member."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hamingja-c02",
      "source_id": "hamingja-s1",
      "quote": "It was even possible to lend one's own hamingja to a friend, as happened when Hjalti Skeggiason was about to leave on a perilous voyage and asked Olaf II of Norway to lend him his hamingja. It usually appears during sleep in the form of an animal. It can also be the spirit of a sleeping person who appears in the form of an animal, as Bödvar Bjarki in the saga of Hrólfr Kraki.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hamingja bisa dipinjamkan kepada teman, seperti saat Hjalti Skeggiason hendak berlayar berbahaya dan meminta Olaf II dari Norwegia meminjamkan hamingja-nya; ia biasanya muncul saat tidur berwujud hewan, atau bisa berupa roh orang tidur dalam rupa hewan, seperti Bödvar Bjarki dalam saga Hrólfr Kraki.",
        "en": "One's hamingja could be lent to a friend, as when Hjalti Skeggiason, before a perilous voyage, asked Olaf II of Norway to lend him his; it usually appears in sleep as an animal, or can be a sleeping person's spirit in animal form, like Bödvar Bjarki in the saga of Hrólfr Kraki."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hamingja-c03",
      "source_id": "hamingja-s1",
      "quote": "In Norse mythology, hamingja (Old Norse \"luck\") refers to two concepts: the personification of the good fortune or luck of an individual or family, the altered appearance of shape-shifters. Both Andy Orchard and Rudolf Simek note parallels between the concept of the hamingja and the fylgja.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, hamingja (Nordik Kuno, \"keberuntungan\") merujuk pada dua konsep: perwujudan untung baik seseorang atau keluarga, dan rupa yang berubah dari pengubah wujud; Andy Orchard dan Rudolf Simek mencatat kemiripannya dengan fylgja.",
        "en": "In Norse mythology hamingja (Old Norse 'luck') refers to two concepts: the personified good fortune of an individual or family, and the altered appearance of shape-shifters; Andy Orchard and Rudolf Simek note parallels with the fylgja."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hamingja-c04",
      "source_id": "hamingja-s2",
      "quote": "La Hamingja est, dans les croyances nordiques, la force tutélaire d'une famille, que l'on pouvait représenter par une femme gigantesque.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kepercayaan Nordik, Hamingja adalah kekuatan pelindung sebuah keluarga yang dapat digambarkan sebagai perempuan raksasa.",
        "en": "In Norse belief the Hamingja is a family's tutelary force, which could be pictured as a gigantic woman."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hamingja-c05",
      "source_id": "hamingja-s2",
      "quote": "cette force tutélaire, qui veillait au bon comportement de ses descendants.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kekuatan pelindung ini mengawasi perilaku baik keturunannya.",
        "en": "This tutelary force watched over the good conduct of its descendants."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hamingja-c06",
      "source_id": "hamingja-s1",
      "quote": "Luck may be transferred to a descendant of the owner, or to a member of a tribe for a perilous journey. It accords wealth, success and power, and it accrues over a life time. Sometimes hamingja is used to denote honor.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Keberuntungan itu dapat dialihkan kepada keturunan pemiliknya atau kepada anggota suku untuk perjalanan berbahaya; ia memberi kekayaan, keberhasilan, dan kekuasaan, bertambah sepanjang hidup, dan kadang hamingja dipakai untuk menyebut kehormatan.",
        "en": "The luck may be passed to the owner's descendant or to a tribe member for a perilous journey; it grants wealth, success and power, accrues over a lifetime, and hamingja is sometimes used to mean honour."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Icovellauna adalah dewi Kelt yang dipuja di Gaul, terutama di kuil mata air Le Sablon di Metz dan di Trier; namanya diartikan \"mata air yang baik\".",
    "en": "Icovellauna was a Celtic goddess worshipped in Gaul, notably at the spring temple of Le Sablon in Metz and at Trier; her name has been glossed 'good fountain'.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "icovellauna-c01",
      "icovellauna-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Icovellauna adalah dewi Kelt yang dipuja di Gaul. Tempat pemujaannya antara lain kuil segi delapan di Le Sablon, Metz, yang semula dibangun di atas mata air dan menghasilkan lima prasasti persembahan untuknya, serta kompleks kuil Altbachtal di Trier. Kedua tempat itu berada di lembah Sungai Moselle, kini Lorraine di Prancis dan Rheinland-Pfalz di Jerman.",
      "en": "Icovellauna was a Celtic goddess worshipped in Gaul. Her shrines included an octagonal temple at Le Sablon in Metz, first built over a spring, which yielded five inscriptions dedicated to her, and the Altbachtal temple complex at Trier. Both lie in the Moselle valley, today Lorraine in France and Rhineland-Palatinate in Germany.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "icovellauna-c01",
        "icovellauna-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di kuil Metz, tangga melingkar turun ke permukaan air agar pemuja dapat meninggalkan persembahan di mata air atau mengambil airnya. Persembahan yang ditemukan meliputi patung kecil Merkurius Gaul lokal, koin, dan keramik dari abad ke-2 hingga ke-4 M. Di prasasti ia disebut \"dewi yang sangat suci\" dan sering dikaitkan dengan dewi Mogontia.",
      "en": "At the Metz temple a spiral staircase led down to the water so worshippers could leave offerings in the spring or take the waters. Offerings found there include a statuette of a local Gaulish Mercury, coins and ceramics from the 2nd to 4th centuries CE. Inscriptions call her a 'most holy divinity', and she is often associated with the goddess Mogontia.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "icovellauna-c03",
        "icovellauna-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Demarolle mengartikan namanya bonne fontaine, \"mata air yang baik\". Miranda Green, mengikuti Joseph Vendryes, menafsirkan akar ico- sebagai \"air\" dan menyebutnya dewi air yang menguasai nymphaeum di Sablon, sebuah situs mata air panas. Xavier Delamarre menganggap tafsiran itu sangat tidak mungkin dan mengusulkan ico- sebagai nama burung, mungkin pelatuk. Dalam satu uraian ia juga dipandang pancaran dewi-ibu Kelt Herta yang menguasai air suci yang memancar dari perut bumi.",
      "en": "Demarolle glosses her name as bonne fontaine, 'good fountain'. Miranda Green, following Joseph Vendryes, reads the root ico- as 'water' and calls her a water goddess presiding over the nymphaeum at Sablon, a thermal spring site. Xavier Delamarre thinks that very improbable and suggests ico- may name a bird, perhaps the woodpecker. One account also sees her as an emanation of the Celtic mother goddess Herta, ruling the sacred waters that rise from deep in the earth.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "icovellauna-c04",
        "icovellauna-c06",
        "icovellauna-c07"
      ]

        "en": "Xavier Delamarre considers Vendryes' reading very improbable and, on etymological grounds, suggests ico- may name a bird, perhaps the woodpecker."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Mánagarmr, \"Anjing Bulan\", adalah serigala dalam mitologi Nordik yang menurut Edda Prosa akan menelan Bulan dan melahap daging orang mati.",
    "en": "Mánagarmr, the 'Moon-Hound', is a wolf of Norse mythology who, according to the Prose Edda, will swallow the Moon and gorge on the dead.",

      "managarmr-c02"

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam Edda Prosa, Snorri Sturluson menyebut Mánagarmr (\"Anjing Bulan\") sebagai keturunan terkuat seorang raksasa perempuan. Ia akan menelan Bulan dan melahap orang mati. Nama ini diduga nama lain untuk Hati atau Sköll yang diambil Snorri dari cerita rakyat.",
      "en": "In the Prose Edda Snorri Sturluson names Mánagarmr ('Moon-Hound') as the mightiest offspring of a giantess. He will swallow the Moon and gorge on the dead. The name is presumably another name for Hati or Sköll that Snorri took from folklore.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "managarmr-c01",
        "managarmr-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam uraian lain, Managarm disebut putra raksasa Angrboda dari Hutan Besi yang berkeliaran di bumi memakan daging mayat, sementara saudaranya Hati mengejar Bulan dan Skalli mengejar Matahari. Saat Ragnarök, Hati menyudutkan Bulan dan Managarm, yang menjadi terbesar dan terkuat karena memakan daging mayat, menelannya; darah Bulan memercik ke Matahari dan menggelapkannya. Menurut tradisi lain, Hati sendiri yang menelan Bulan sementara Managarm hanya dapat menonton sebagai hukuman.",
      "en": "Another account makes Managarm a son of the giantess Angrboda of the Iron Wood who roams the earth eating the flesh of corpses, while his brother Hati chases the Moon and Skalli the Sun.   "short_description": {
    "id": "Myling adalah arwah anak yang dibunuh dan tidak dikuburkan dengan layak dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia, yang meminta dibawa ke tanah suci pemakaman.",
    "en": "In Scandinavian folklore a myling is the ghost of a murdered child left unburied, who demands to be carried to consecrated ground.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "myling-c01",
      "myling-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia, mylingar adalah penjelmaan arwah anak-anak yang terpaksa berkeliaran di bumi sampai mereka dapat membujuk seseorang untuk menguburkan mereka dengan layak. Menurut kepercayaan rakyat Swedia, myling adalah mayat hidup bayi yang belum dibaptis, dibunuh dan disembunyikan ibunya; kadang bayi itu bahkan dikubur di bawah lantai kayu pondok untuk menutupi kelahiran yang tak diinginkan.",
      "en": "In Scandinavian folklore mylingar are the ghostly forms of children's souls forced to wander the earth until they can persuade someone to bury them properly. In Swedish folk belief a myling is the revenant of an unbaptised newborn murdered and hidden by its mother, sometimes even buried under the wooden floor of the cottage to conceal the unwanted birth.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "myling-c01",
        "myling-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Myling, yang dikenal juga sebagai utburd di Norwegia, útburður di Islandia, serta ihtiriekko dan liekkiö di Finlandia, konon mengejar pengembara yang sendirian di malam hari dan melompat ke punggung mereka, meminta dibawa ke pemakaman. Ia makin berat saat mendekati pemakaman sampai pembawanya bisa terbenam ke tanah, dan bila orang itu gagal mencapai pemakaman, myling membunuhnya dengan murka.",
      "en": "The myling, also called utburd in Norwegian, útburður in Icelandic and ihtiriekko or liekkiö in Finnish, reportedly pursues people walking alone after dark, leaps onto their backs and insists on being taken to the graveyard. It grows heavier as the graveyard nears until its bearer may sink into the soil, and if the person fails to reach the cemetery, the myling kills them in rage.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "myling-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nyanyiannya di malam hari dapat membongkar kejahatan sang ibu. Myling dapat ditolong dengan memberinya nama, misalnya bila ia berseru \"beri aku nama!\", atau dengan menemukan jasadnya dan menguburkannya di tanah suci pemakaman.",
      "en": "Its singing at night can reveal the mother's crime. A myling can be helped by giving it a name, for instance when it cries 'give me a name!', or by finding its body and burying it in hallowed ground.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "myling-c03",
        "myling-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kata myling berasal dari bahasa Swedia myrding atau mörding, dari bahasa Swedia Kuno myrða, \"membunuh\", sehingga berarti \"yang dibunuh\"; kata ini tercatat dalam bahasa Swedia sejak 1647. Keyakinan bahwa myling murka dan menuntut balas membuatnya dianggap salah satu jenis hantu paling mengancam dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia.",
      "en": "The word myling comes from Swedish myrding or mörding, from Old Swedish myrða, 'to murder', and so means 'the murdered one'; it is attested in Swedish since 1647. The belief that mylingar are enraged and vengeful made them one of the most menacing kinds of ghost in Scandinavian folklore.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "myling-c04",
        "myling-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "myling-c01",
      "source_id": "myling-s1",
      "quote": "In Scandinavian folklore, the mylingar were the phantasmal incarnations of the souls of children that had been forced to roam the earth until they could persuade someone (or otherwise cause enough of a ruckus to make their wishes known) to bury them properly.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia, mylingar adalah penjelmaan arwah anak-anak yang terpaksa berkeliaran di bumi sampai bisa membujuk seseorang untuk menguburkan mereka dengan layak.",
        "en": "In Scandinavian folklore mylingar were the phantasmal forms of children's souls forced to roam the earth until they could persuade someone to bury them properly."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "myling-c02",
      "source_id": "myling-s1",
      "quote": "The myling (also known as utburd in Norwegian, útburður in Icelandic and ihtiriekko and liekkiö in Finnish) is said to chase lone wanderers at night and jump on their backs, demanding to be carried to the graveyard ... apparently grow heavier as they near the graveyard ... If one should prove unable to make it into the cemetery, the myling kills its victim in a rage.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Myling (juga utburd di Norwegia, útburður di Islandia, ihtiriekko dan liekkiö di Finlandia) konon mengejar pengembara yang sendirian di malam hari, melompat ke punggungnya dan meminta dibawa ke pemakaman; ia makin berat saat mendekati pemakaman, dan bila korban gagal mencapainya, myling membunuhnya dengan murka.",
        "en": "The myling (also utburd in Norwegian, útburður in Icelandic, ihtiriekko and liekkiö in Finnish) is said to chase lone wanderers at night, jump on their backs and demand to be carried to the graveyard; it grows heavier as the graveyard nears, and if the victim fails to reach it, kills them in rage."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "myling-c03",
      "source_id": "myling-s1",
      "quote": "It can be heard singing in the night, thereby revealing the mother's crime. Ways to help the myling is to give it a name or to find the corpse and bury it in holy soil.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nyanyiannya di malam hari dapat membongkar kejahatan sang ibu; myling dapat ditolong dengan memberinya nama atau menemukan jasadnya dan menguburkannya di tanah suci.",
        "en": "It can be heard singing at night, revealing the mother's crime; it can be helped by naming it or by finding the body and burying it in holy soil."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "myling-c04",
      "source_id": "myling-s1",
      "quote": "The belief that mylings are enraged and seeking revenge is what gave them the reputation as one of the most menacing types of ghosts in Scandinavian folklore.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Keyakinan bahwa myling murka dan menuntut balas membuatnya dikenal sebagai salah satu hantu paling mengancam dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia.",
        "en": "The belief that mylingar are enraged and vengeful earned them a reputation as one of the most menacing ghosts in Scandinavian folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "myling-c05",
      "source_id": "myling-s2",
      "quote": "Ein Myling (schwedisch: myling, myrding) war im schwedischen Volksglauben ein Wiedergänger eines ungetauften, neugeborenen Kindes, das von seiner Mutter ermordet und versteckt wurde. Manchen Überlieferungen zufolge wurde das tote Kind sogar unter dem Holzboden in der Hütte begraben",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat Swedia, myling (juga myrding) adalah mayat hidup bayi yang belum dibaptis, dibunuh dan disembunyikan ibunya; menurut sebagian tradisi bayi itu bahkan dikubur di bawah lantai kayu pondok.",
        "en": "In Swedish folk belief a myling (also myrding) was the revenant of an unbaptised newborn murdered and hidden by its mother; in some traditions the dead child was even buried under the cottage's wooden floor."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "myling-c06",
      "source_id": "myling-s2",
      "quote": "Das ungetaufte Kind konnte man ebenfalls um Hilfe rufen hören mit den Worten: „Gib mir einen Namen!“ und man konnte das Kind retten, indem man antwortete: „Du kannst meinen haben, ich heiße N. N.“",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bayi yang belum dibaptis itu bisa terdengar berseru \"beri aku nama!\", dan orang dapat menyelamatkannya dengan menjawab \"ambillah namaku\" sambil menyebut namanya sendiri.",
        "en": "The unbaptised child could be heard crying 'give me a name!', and one could save it by answering 'you can have mine' and giving one's own name."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "myling-c07",
      "source_id": "myling-s2",
      "quote": "Das Wort stammt vom schwedischen myrding bzw. mörding, abgeleitet von altschwedisch: myrða mit der Bedeutung „morden“. Myling bedeutet also „Ermordetes“. Das Wort ist im schwedischen Sprachgebrauch seit 1647 belegt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata ini berasal dari bahasa Swedia myrding atau mörding, dari Swedia Kuno myrða, \"membunuh\", sehingga myling berarti \"yang dibunuh\"; kata ini tercatat dalam bahasa Swedia sejak 1647.",
        "en": "The word comes from Swedish myrding or mörding, from Old Swedish myrða, 'to murder', so myling means 'the murdered one'; it is attested in Swedish since 1647."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "myling-c08",
      "source_id": "myling-s1",
      "quote": "In the mobile game Year Walk, part of the game is spent finding lost Mylings to put in the care of the Brook Horse. ... In the PC game Phasmophobia where you take the role of a ghost hunter, one of the possible ghosts you may encounter is a Myling.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam gim seluler Year Walk pemain mencari myling yang hilang untuk dititipkan pada Brook Horse, dan dalam gim PC Phasmophobia myling adalah salah satu hantu yang dapat ditemui.",
        "en": "In the mobile game Year Walk players find lost mylings for the Brook Horse to care for, and in the PC game Phasmophobia the myling is one of the ghosts one may meet."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

    "id": "Selma, atau Seljordsormen, adalah ular laut legendaris dalam cerita rakyat Norwegia yang konon hidup di Danau Seljord, Telemark.",
    "en": "Selma, or Seljordsormen, is a legendary sea serpent of Norwegian folklore said to live in Lake Seljord in Telemark.",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Norwegia, Selma (Seljordsormen, \"ular Seljord\") adalah ular laut legendaris yang konon hidup di Danau Seljord sepanjang 13 kilometer di Kotamadya Seljord, Telemark. Makhluk ini sudah diperbincangkan sedikitnya sejak abad ke-18, dan kesaksian tentangnya cenderung memuncak pada musim panas yang panas dan tenang.",
      "en": "Norwegian folklore tells of Selma (Seljordsormen, 'the Seljord serpent'), a legendary serpent believed to dwell in the 13-kilometre Lake Seljord in Seljord Municipality, Telemark. It has been discussed since at least the 18th century, and eyewitness reports tend to peak in hot, quiet summers.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "selma-lake-monster-c01",
        "selma-lake-monster-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Catatan tertulis tertua berasal dari tahun 1750, ketika makhluk itu dikatakan mengitari perahu dayung milik seorang lelaki dari Bø yang menyeberang dari Ulvenes ke Nes. Selma digambarkan panjangnya antara 4 dan 50 meter, hitam legam, dengan kepala mirip kuda atau buaya, kadang dengan sepasang sirip depan dan satu atau dua punuk.",
      "en": "The oldest written account dates from 1750, when it reportedly circled the rowboat of a man from Bø crossing from Ulvenes to Nes. Selma is described as between 4 and 50 metres long, pitch black, with a horse- or crocodile-like head, sometimes with a pair of front flippers and one or two humps.",

    {

      "quote": "In Norwegian folklore, Selma (Norwegian: Seljordsormen , \"the Seljord serpent\") is a legendary sea serpent said to live in the 13-kilometre-long (8-mile) Lake Seljord (Seljordsvatnet ) in Seljord Municipality, Telemark, Norway.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Norwegia, Selma (Seljordsormen, \"ular Seljord\") adalah ular laut legendaris yang konon hidup di Danau Seljord (Seljordsvatnet) sepanjang 13 kilometer di Kotamadya Seljord, Telemark, Norwegia.",

      }

    {
      "id": "selma-lake-monster-c02",

      "quote": "The sea serpent has been the subject of discussion since at least the 18th century. Eyewitness descriptions of encounters have tended to peak during hot, quiet summers.       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ular laut ini diperbincangkan sedikitnya sejak abad ke-18; kesaksian cenderung memuncak saat musim panas yang panas dan tenang, dan catatan tertulis tertua dari 1750 menyebut makhluk itu mengitari perahu dayung milik lelaki dari Bø yang menyeberang dari Ulvenes ke Nes.",
        "en": "The serpent has been discussed since at least the 18th century; eyewitness accounts peak in hot, quiet summers, and the oldest written account, from 1750, says it circled the rowboat of a man from Bø crossing from Ulvenes to Nes."

      "source_id": "selma-lake-monster-s1",
      "quote": "The sea serpent Selma has been depicted in the coat of arms of Seljord since 1989. Designed by sculpturer Trygve Magnus Barstad, the arms show Selma in a gold-color on a red background.",

      "source_id": "selma-lake-monster-s2",

    "id": "Kucing troll adalah makhluk peliharaan penyihir dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia yang mencuri susu sapi dan krim untuk tuannya.",
    "en": "The troll cat is a witch's familiar of Scandinavian folklore that steals cows' milk and cream for its mistress.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia, kucing troll adalah makhluk peliharaan seorang penyihir. Ia mengisap susu sapi lalu memuntahkannya ke ember susu sang penyihir, dan masuk ke rumah-rumah untuk menjilat krim. Selain kucing, ada makhluk serupa seperti kelinci susu, terwelu susu, dan bola troll.",
      "en": "In Scandinavian folklore the troll cat is a witch's familiar. It sucked milk from cows and spat it into the witch's pails, and slipped into homes to lick up cream. Besides cats, similar creatures include the milk rabbit, the milk hare and the troll ball.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "troll-cat-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Penyihir konon membuatnya dari rambut manusia, kuku, serutan kayu, dan semacamnya. Nama Norwegia trollnøste dan trollnøa menunjukkan wujudnya seperti gulungan benang; jenis lain tampak seperti kucing biasa, dan melukainya akan melukai sang penyihir juga. Menembak kucing troll diyakini membuat susu menyembur dari lukanya, dan kucing itu harus dikubur bersama penyihirnya.",
      "en": "Witches were said to make it from human hair, nails, wood shavings and the like. The Norwegian names trollnøste and trollnøa show its shape, like a ball of yarn; another kind looked like an ordinary cat, and harming it harmed the witch too. Shooting a troll cat was believed to make milk spray from the wound, and it had to be buried with its witch.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "troll-cat-c02",
        "troll-cat-c03"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Dalam kepercayaan Swedia, makhluk ini disebut bjära dan bisa berwujud terwelu, kucing, burung, atau gulungan benang; deskripsi tertua cara membuatnya berasal dari pengadilan sihir di Söderala, Hälsingland, tahun 1597.       "en": "In Swedish belief the creature is called bjära and could take the form of a hare, cat, bird or ball of yarn; the oldest description of how to make one comes from a witch trial at Söderala, Hälsingland, in 1597.       "quote": "A troll cat is the familiar of a witch in Scandinavian folklore. Troll cats sucked milk from cows and spat it out in the witches' milk pails, and went into homes to lick up cream. Aside from cats, similar creatures include the milk rabbit, milk hare, and ball-shaped troll ball.",
      "locator": null,

        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia, kucing troll adalah makhluk peliharaan penyihir yang mengisap susu sapi dan memuntahkannya ke ember susu penyihir serta masuk ke rumah untuk menjilat krim; makhluk serupa antara lain kelinci susu, terwelu susu, dan bola troll.",

      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penyihir konon membuatnya dari \"rambut manusia, kuku, serutan kayu, dan semacamnya\"; nama Norwegia trollnøste dan trollnøa menunjukkan wujud gulungan benang, sedangkan jenis lain tampak seperti kucing biasa, dan melukainya akan melukai penyihir juga.",

      "source_id": "troll-cat-s1",

      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menembak kucing troll diyakini membuat susu menyembur dari lukanya; kucing itu harus dikubur bersama penyihirnya, atau penyihir akan keluar dari kuburnya untuk mengambilnya. Makhluk terkait adalah tilberi, pencuri susu pembantu penyihir dalam cerita rakyat Islandia.",
        "en": "Shooting a troll cat was thought to make milk spray from the wound; it had to be buried with the witch, or she would leave her grave to fetch it. A related creature is the tilberi, a milk thief and witches' aide in Icelandic folklore."

      "source_id": "troll-cat-s1",
      "quote": "The existence of troll cats appears to be related to the observation of matter (such as hair) regurgitated by cattle. The slime mold Fuligo septica and the foam made by spittle bugs were seen as troll cat droppings.",

      "source_id": "troll-cat-s2",
      "quote": "Una bjära (mjölkhare, trollhare, pukhare, puken, trollkatten, baran, bärarn) era, en las creencias populares suecas, un ser característico que una bruja usaba para robar leche de las vacas de los vecinos.       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat Swedia, bjära (juga mjölkhare, trollhare, trollkatten, dan lain-lain) adalah makhluk yang dipakai penyihir untuk mencuri susu sapi tetangga; ia bisa berwujud terwelu (Småland), kucing atau burung (Dalarna), atau gulungan benang (Norrland).",
        "en": "In Swedish folk belief a bjära (also mjölkhare, trollhare, trollkatten and others) was a being a witch used to steal neighbours' cows' milk; it could be a hare (Småland), a cat or bird (Dalarna) or a ball of yarn (Norrland)."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Bahkauv adalah monster dalam cerita rakyat Aachen, Jerman, berupa anak sapi cacat bertaring yang mengintai di saluran air dan melompat ke pundak pemabuk di malam hari.",
    "en": "The Bahkauv is a monster of Aachen folklore in Germany, a deformed fanged calf that lurks in waterways and leaps onto drunkards' shoulders at night.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bahkauv-c01",
      "bahkauv-c02",
      "bahkauv-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bahkauv adalah monster mitos yang konon tinggal di Aachen, Jerman, dan dikenal dalam cerita rakyat Aachen dan Rheinland. Namanya berasal dari Bachkalb, \"anak sapi sungai\". Ia biasa digambarkan seperti anak sapi yang memanjang atau cacat dengan taring tajam, dan sering mengintai di dekat mata air, sungai kecil, dan selokan; saluran pembuangan dan mata air panas di bawah kota disebut sebagai tempat tinggalnya.",
      "en": "The Bahkauv is a mythical monster said to live in Aachen, Germany, and known in the folklore of Aachen and the Rhineland. Its name comes from Bachkalb, 'brook calf'. It is usually described as an elongated or deformed calf with sharp fangs that lurks near fountains, streams and sewers; the city's sewers and the hot springs beneath it are named as its home.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bahkauv-c01",
        "bahkauv-c02",
        "bahkauv-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut sage Aachen, ia tinggal di saluran air buangan mata air panas di Büchel, berwujud anak sapi besar berbulu kusut dengan gigi tajam, mata yang bersinar dalam gelap, cakar seperti beruang, ekor bersisik, dan rantai di leher serta kakinya. Pada malam hari ia menyerang orang mabuk dalam perjalanan pulang, melompat ke pundak mereka dan minta digendong sampai rumah; ia makin berat bila pembawanya berdoa dan makin ringan bila ia mengumpat.",
      "en": "In the Aachen legend it lived in the drain of the hot springs at the Büchel, shaped like a large shaggy calf with sharp teeth, eyes glowing in the dark, bear-like paws, a scaly tail and chains on its neck and legs. At night it attacked drunkards on their way home, jumping onto their shoulders to be carried home; it grew heavier when the bearer prayed and lighter when he cursed.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bahkauv-c04",
        "bahkauv-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Meski begitu, Bahkauv tak pernah membunuh siapa pun dan tak pernah mengganggu perempuan atau anak-anak. Satu legenda menyebut Pippin Muda membunuh seekor Bahkauv yang berbaring di dekat mata air. Pada 1902 kota Aachen mendirikan patung Bahkauv di atas sumur tua yang dikaitkan dengannya; patung itu dilebur untuk logamnya pada Perang Dunia II, dan patung kedua dibangun pada 1967.",
      "en": "Even so, the Bahkauv never killed anyone and never troubled women or children. One legend says Pippin the Younger slew a Bahkauv lying by a spring. In 1902 Aachen erected a statue of the Bahkauv over an old well linked to it; the statue was melted down for its metal in World War II, and a second was built in 1967.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bahkauv-c06",
        "bahkauv-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "bahkauv-c01",
      "source_id": "bahkauv-s1",
      "quote": "The Bahkauv is a mythical monster said to reside in Aachen, Germany. The creature has been featured in the folklore of both Aachen and the Rhineland. Often depicted as a deformed calf with fangs, the Bahkauv is commonly associated with drunken men.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bahkauv adalah monster mitos yang konon tinggal di Aachen, Jerman, muncul dalam cerita rakyat Aachen dan Rheinland, sering digambarkan sebagai anak sapi cacat bertaring, dan dikaitkan dengan lelaki mabuk.",
        "en": "The Bahkauv is a mythical monster said to live in Aachen, Germany, featured in Aachen and Rhineland folklore, often shown as a deformed fanged calf and associated with drunken men."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bahkauv-c02",
      "source_id": "bahkauv-s1",
      "quote": "The Bahkauv is typically described as being similar to an elongated or deformed calf with sharp fangs. According to legend, the creature often lurks near fountains, streams, and sewers. The Aachen city sewers and the many thermal springs found under the city have been cited as a dwelling place for the Bahkauv.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bahkauv biasanya digambarkan seperti anak sapi memanjang atau cacat bertaring tajam; menurut legenda ia sering mengintai di dekat mata air, sungai kecil, dan selokan, dan saluran pembuangan serta mata air panas di bawah Aachen disebut sebagai tempat tinggalnya.",
        "en": "The Bahkauv is typically described as an elongated or deformed calf with sharp fangs; legend says it lurks near fountains, streams and sewers, and Aachen's sewers and hot springs are cited as its home."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bahkauv-c03",
      "source_id": "bahkauv-s2",
      "quote": "Das Bahkauv (aus Bachkalb, auch Badekalb, mundartlich auch Bakauf, Baakauf, Bahkauf oder Bakauv) ist ein Fabelwesen aus Aachen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bahkauv (dari Bachkalb, \"anak sapi sungai\", juga Badekalb, dalam dialek Bakauf atau Bakauv) adalah makhluk khayalan dari Aachen.",
        "en": "The Bahkauv (from Bachkalb, 'brook calf', also Badekalb, dialect Bakauf or Bakauv) is a fabulous creature from Aachen."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bahkauv-c04",
      "source_id": "bahkauv-s2",
      "quote": "Nach dieser Sage hauste das Bahkauv in dem Abwasserkanal der Thermalquellen am Büchel, dem so genannten Kolbert. Seine Gestalt glich einem großen Kalb mit einem zottigen Fell. Im Maul hatte es scharfe Zähne, und seine klobigen Augen leuchteten im Dunkeln. Seine Pfoten sahen aus wie Bärentatzen mit scharfen Krallen, und sein Schweif war geschuppt",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut sage, Bahkauv tinggal di saluran buangan mata air panas di Büchel yang disebut Kolbert; wujudnya seperti anak sapi besar berbulu kusut, bergigi tajam, bermata menyala dalam gelap, bercakar seperti beruang, dan berekor bersisik.",
        "en": "By the legend the Bahkauv lived in the drain of the hot springs at the Büchel, the Kolbert; it looked like a large shaggy calf with sharp teeth, eyes glowing in the dark, bear-like clawed paws and a scaly tail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bahkauv-c05",
      "source_id": "bahkauv-s2",
      "quote": "Nachts dagegen fiel es Nachtschwärmer an, besonders solche, die betrunken auf dem Heimweg waren. Es sprang auf sie auf und ließ sich auf ihren Schultern bis zu ihrem Zuhause tragen. ... Dass das Bahkauv ein Teufelsvieh war, erkannte man daran, dass es sich schwerer machte, wenn sein Träger betete, und dass es sich leichter machte, wenn er fluchte.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada malam hari ia menyerang orang yang pulang larut, terutama yang mabuk, melompat ke atas mereka dan minta digendong di pundak sampai rumah; tanda bahwa ia makhluk setan ialah ia makin berat bila pembawanya berdoa dan makin ringan bila ia mengumpat.",
        "en": "At night it attacked revellers, especially drunk ones heading home, jumping on them to be carried home on their shoulders; that it was a devil's beast showed in growing heavier when its bearer prayed and lighter when he cursed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bahkauv-c06",
      "source_id": "bahkauv-s2",
      "quote": "Das Bahkauv hat aber nie jemanden umgebracht und auch Frauen und Kinder nie belästigt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namun Bahkauv tak pernah membunuh siapa pun dan tak pernah mengganggu perempuan maupun anak-anak.",
        "en": "But the Bahkauv never killed anyone and never troubled women or children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bahkauv-c07",
      "source_id": "bahkauv-s1",
      "quote": "One legend holds that Pippin the Younger slew a Bahkauv as it was lying by a spring. In 1902 the city of Aachen erected a statue of the Bahkauv over an old well that had been associated with the monster. This statue was melted down for its metal in World War II, prompting the city to build a second statue in 1967.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu legenda menyebut Pippin Muda membunuh seekor Bahkauv yang berbaring di dekat mata air; pada 1902 Aachen mendirikan patung Bahkauv di atas sumur tua yang dikaitkan dengannya, yang dilebur untuk logamnya pada Perang Dunia II sehingga kota membangun patung kedua pada 1967.",
        "en": "One legend says Pippin the Younger slew a Bahkauv lying by a spring; in 1902 Aachen erected a Bahkauv statue over an old well linked to it, which was melted down in World War II, so the city built a second in 1967."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Bokkenrijders atau penunggang kambing jantan adalah penyihir dalam cerita rakyat Belanda tenggara dan Belgia timur laut yang terbang dengan kambing pemberian Iblis untuk merampok dan membunuh.",
    "en": "The Buckriders (Bokkenrijders) are witches of south-eastern Dutch and north-eastern Belgian folklore who flew on bucks given by the Devil to rob and murder.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bokkenrijders (penunggang kambing jantan) adalah bagian dari cerita rakyat Belanda tenggara dan Belgia timur laut. Mereka dipercaya sebagai penyihir yang terbang di langit menunggangi kambing jantan pemberian Iblis untuk merampok dan membunuh rakyat biasa serta merampas harta gereja, dan setahun sekali menghadap tuan mereka, sang Iblis, di Padang Mook.",
      "en": "The Bokkenrijders (Buckriders) belong to south-eastern Dutch and north-eastern Belgian folklore. They were believed to be witches who flew through the sky on bucks given by the Devil to rob and murder common people and plunder church property, and once a year visited their master, the Devil, on the Mook Heath.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "buckriders-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sepanjang abad ke-18, kawanan pencuri dan penjahat memakai kepercayaan ini untuk menakut-nakuti penduduk Limburg selatan dan melancarkan serangan di wilayah Limburg dan sebagian Jerman masa kini. Orang percaya mereka dapat menempuh jarak jauh dengan cepat lewat udara dan pulang sebelum fajar. Sebagai tanggapan, kota-kota di Limburg membangun parit pertahanan.",
      "en": "Throughout the 18th century gangs of thieves exploited the belief to frighten the people of southern Limburg, raiding across Limburg and parts of present-day Germany. People believed they could fly far and fast and be home before dawn. In response, Limburg towns built defensive moats.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "buckriders-c02",
        "buckriders-c03"

    },
    {
      "id": "Karena dikaitkan dengan ilmu gaib, banyak lelaki yang mungkin tak bersalah dituduh sebagai bokkenrijder, disiksa, lalu dihukum; tuduhan selalu mencakup perampokan sekaligus sihir.       "quote": "The Buckriders (Dutch: Bokkenrijders ; French: Chevaliers du bouc ) are a part of South-Eastern Dutch and North-Eastern Belgian folklore.       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bokkenrijders (Prancis: Chevaliers du bouc) adalah bagian dari cerita rakyat Belanda tenggara dan Belgia timur laut: penyihir yang terbang menunggangi kambing jantan pemberian Iblis untuk merampok dan membunuh rakyat serta merampas harta gereja, dan setahun sekali mereka menghadap Iblis di Padang Mook.",
        "en": "The Buckriders (Dutch Bokkenrijders, French Chevaliers du bouc) belong to south-eastern Dutch and north-eastern Belgian folklore: witches flying on bucks from the Devil to rob and murder people and church property, who once a year visited the Devil on the Mook Heath."

      "source_id": "buckriders-s1",
      "quote": "Formally, the name Bokkenrijders (buckriders) was first publicly used in 1774, during the \"trial of Wellen\", a town in what belonged then to the Southern Netherlands.",

    {
      "id": "buckriders-c09",
      "source_id": "buckriders-s1",
      "quote": "The trials against the buckriders differed from 'ordinary' criminal proceedings because in many cases a so-called 'ungodly oath' was involved (\"I renounce God and swear submission to the Devil\").",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pengadilan bokkenrijders berbeda dari perkara pidana biasa karena sering melibatkan \"sumpah tak bertuhan\" (\"Aku menyangkal Tuhan dan bersumpah tunduk kepada Iblis\").",
        "en": "The buckrider trials differed from ordinary criminal cases because they often involved an 'ungodly oath' ('I renounce God and swear submission to the Devil')."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Eitri atau Sindri adalah kurcaci pandai besi dalam mitologi Nordik, saudara Brokkr, yang menempa babi emas Gullinbursti, cincin Draupnir, dan palu Mjöllnir.",
    "en": "Eitri or Sindri is a dwarf smith of Norse mythology, brother of Brokkr, who forged the golden boar Gullinbursti, the ring Draupnir and the hammer Mjöllnir.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "eitri-c01",

    ]

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam agama Nordik Kuno, Eitri, juga disebut Sindri, adalah kurcaci dan saudara Brokkr. Menurut Skáldskaparmál, setelah Loki menyuruh para Putra Ivaldi membuat rambut Sif, kapal Skidbladnir milik Freyr, dan tombak Gungnir milik Odin, ia mempertaruhkan kepalanya sendiri dengan Brokkr bahwa Eitri tak akan mampu membuat benda yang menandingi mutu karya itu.",
      "en": "Old Norse religion knows Eitri, also named Sindri, as a dwarf whose brother is Brokkr. The Skáldskaparmál tells how Loki, after the Sons of Ivaldi had crafted Gungnir, the spear of Odin, Skidbladnir, the ship of Freyr, and golden hair for Sif, staked his own head with Brokkr on Eitri being unable to equal that craftsmanship.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "eitri-c01",
        "eitri-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Eitri bekerja di tungkunya sementara saudaranya memompa ububan, tetapi seekor lalat, yang kadang dianggap Loki sendiri, menyengat Brokkr untuk menggagalkan pekerjaan itu. Eitri tetap berhasil membuat babi emas Gullinbursti, cincin emas Draupnir, dan palu Mjöllnir sehingga saudaranya memenangkan taruhan, walau gagang palu itu lebih pendek dari semestinya.",
      "en": "Eitri worked at his furnace while his brother pumped the bellows, but a fly, sometimes thought to be Loki himself, stung Brokkr to spoil the work. Even so Eitri produced Gullinbursti, a boar of gold, Draupnir, a ring of gold, and Mjöllnir, a hammer, so that his brother won the wager, though the hammer's handle turned out too short.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hófvarpnir adalah kuda terbang dalam mitologi Nordik yang dapat berlari di udara dan di atas laut, tunggangan dewi Gná, utusan Frigg.",
    "en": "Hófvarpnir is a flying horse of Norse mythology that can ride through the air and over the sea, the mount of the goddess Gná, Frigg's messenger.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "hofvarpnir-c01",
      "hofvarpnir-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Hófvarpnir (bahasa Nordik Kuno, \"yang melempar-lempar kukunya\" atau \"pelempar kuku\") adalah kuda terbang yang dapat menapak di atas laut, ditunggangi dewi Gná yang menjalankan tugas Frigg di dunia-dunia lain. Gná dan Hófvarpnir tercatat dalam Edda Prosa yang ditulis Snorri Sturluson pada abad ke-13.",
      "en": "In Norse mythology Hófvarpnir (Old Norse 'he who throws his hoofs about' or 'hoof-thrower') is a flying, sea-treading horse ridden by the goddess Gná, who runs errands in other worlds for Frigg. Gná and Hófvarpnir are attested in the Prose Edda written by Snorri Sturluson in the 13th century.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hofvarpnir-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam bab 35 Gylfaginning, High menyebut Gná menunggang Hófvarpnir yang mampu melaju di udara dan di atas laut. Ketika beberapa Vanir melihatnya melintas di udara, Gná menjawab dalam bait bahwa ia tidak terbang, melainkan melaju di atas Hofvarpnir, anak Hamskerpir dan Garðrofa; sumber bait ini tidak disebutkan dan tidak tercatat di tempat lain. Para sarjana menyebut Hófvarpnir dan Sleipnir yang berkaki delapan sebagai contoh kuda transenden dalam mitologi Nordik.",
      "en": "In chapter 35 of Gylfaginning, High says Gná rides Hófvarpnir, which can travel through the air and over the sea. When some Vanir saw her pass through the air, Gná answered in verse that she was not flying but faring on Hofvarpnir, sired by Hamskerpir on Garðrofa; the source of these lines is not given and they appear nowhere else. Scholars cite Hófvarpnir and the eight-legged Sleipnir as examples of transcendent horses in Norse mythology.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hofvarpnir-c02",
        "hofvarpnir-c03",
        "hofvarpnir-c04",
        "hofvarpnir-c05",
        "hofvarpnir-c06"
      ]
    }
