      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Gná adalah dewi yang menjalankan tugas Frigg di dunia lain dan menunggang kuda terbang penapak laut Hófvarpnir (Nordik Kuno, \"yang melempar-lempar kukunya\", \"pelempar kuku\"); keduanya tercatat dalam Edda Prosa yang ditulis Snorri Sturluson pada abad ke-13.",
        "en": "In Norse mythology Gná is a goddess running errands in other worlds for Frigg, riding the flying, sea-treading horse Hófvarpnir (Old Norse 'he who throws his hoofs about', 'hoof-thrower'); both appear in the Prose Edda written by Snorri Sturluson in the 13th century."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hofvarpnir-c02",
      "source_id": "hofvarpnir-s1",
      "quote": "In chapter 35 of the Prose Edda book Gylfaginning ... High adds that Gná rides the horse Hófvarpnir, and that this horse has the ability to ride through the air and atop the sea. High continues that \"once some Vanir saw her path as she rode through the air\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam bab 35 Gylfaginning, High menambahkan bahwa Gná menunggang Hófvarpnir, kuda yang mampu melaju di udara dan di atas laut, dan bahwa \"suatu kali beberapa Vanir melihat jalurnya saat ia melaju di udara\".",
        "en": "In chapter 35 of Gylfaginning High adds that Gná rides Hófvarpnir, a horse able to ride through the air and atop the sea, and that 'once some Vanir saw her path as she rode through the air'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hofvarpnir-c03",
      "source_id": "hofvarpnir-s1",
      "quote": "I fly not though I fare and move through the air on Hofvarpnir the one whom Hamskerpir got with Gardrofa.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gná menjawab: \"Aku tidak terbang, tetapi melaju dan bergerak di udara di atas Hofvarpnir, yang diperanakkan Hamskerpir dengan Gardrofa.\"",
        "en": "Gná replies: 'I fly not though I fare and move through the air on Hofvarpnir, whom Hamskerpir got with Gardrofa.'"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hofvarpnir-c04",
      "source_id": "hofvarpnir-s2",
      "quote": "Hofvarpnir ( del nórdico antiguo: tirador de cascos) era en la mitología nórdica, el caballo de Gna, una de las ayudantas de la diosa Frigg. Según lo que se relata en Gylfaginning, Edda prosaica, Hofvarpnir era capaz de desplazarse a través del aire y del agua. Fue engendrado por unos caballos llamados Hamskerpir y Garðrofa.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hofvarpnir (Nordik Kuno, \"pelempar kuku\") adalah kuda Gná, salah satu pembantu dewi Frigg; menurut Gylfaginning ia mampu bergerak di udara dan di air, dan diperanakkan kuda-kuda bernama Hamskerpir dan Garðrofa.",
        "en": "Hofvarpnir (Old Norse 'hoof-thrower') was the horse of Gná, one of the goddess Frigg's helpers; per Gylfaginning it could move through air and water, and was sired by horses named Hamskerpir and Garðrofa."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hofvarpnir-c05",
      "source_id": "hofvarpnir-s1",
      "quote": "Hófvarpnir and the eight-legged steed Sleipnir have been cited examples of transcendent horses in Norse mythology.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hófvarpnir dan kuda berkaki delapan Sleipnir disebut sebagai contoh kuda transenden dalam mitologi Nordik.",
        "en": "Hófvarpnir and the eight-legged steed Sleipnir have been cited as examples of transcendent horses in Norse mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hofvarpnir-c06",
      "source_id": "hofvarpnir-s1",
      "quote": "The source for these stanzas is not provided and they are otherwise unattested.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber bait-bait ini tidak disebutkan dan tidak tercatat di tempat lain.",
        "en": "The source of these stanzas is not given and they are otherwise unattested."
      }
    }

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kabouter adalah makhluk mirip gnom dalam cerita rakyat Belanda dan Flandria: orang mungil berjanggut dan bertopi runcing yang membantu pekerjaan rumah tetapi menghukum siapa pun yang mengintipnya.",
    "en": "The kabouter is a gnome-like creature of the folklore of the Netherlands and Flanders: a tiny bearded person in a pointed hat who helps with chores but punishes anyone who spies on it.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "kabouter-c01",
      "kabouter-c03",
      "kabouter-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kabouter adalah makhluk mirip gnom dalam cerita rakyat Belanda, sekerabat dengan leprechaun Irlandia, tomte atau nisse Skandinavia, hob Inggris, brownie Skotlandia, dan Klabauter atau kobold Jerman. Dalam cerita rakyat Negeri-Negeri Rendah, kabouter adalah orang mungil setinggi sekitar 10–15 cm yang tinggal di dalam atau dekat rumah dan kandang, atau di bukit, hutan, dan padang; banyak kisah menyebut kerajaan kabouter yang luas.",
      "en": "The kabouter is a gnome-like being of Dutch folklore, comparable to the Irish leprechaun, the English hob, the Scottish brownie, the Scandinavian tomte or nisse and the German Klabauter or kobold. Across the Low Countries, folk tradition pictures kabouters as little people only some 10–15 cm high, dwelling in or by houses and stables, or in hills, woods and heaths; many tales speak of vast kabouter kingdoms.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kabouter-c01",
        "kabouter-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kabouter dapat dipandang sebagai roh yang membantu di rumah dengan bekerja di malam hari dan merawat ternak, misalnya memerah susu. Yang jantan berjanggut panjang, dan semuanya memakai topi tinggi runcing, biasanya merah atau hijau. Mereka pemalu dan sering menghukum orang yang mengintip mereka, misalnya dengan membutakan, mengasamkan susu, menghitamkan biji-bijian, atau menakut-nakuti ternak; meninggalkan susu dan roti untuk mereka dapat memperbaiki hubungan.",
      "en": "Kabouters can be seen as spirits who help at home by working at night and tending animals, for example milking them. The males have long beards, and all wear tall pointed hats, usually red or green. They are shy and often punish people who spy on them, by blinding, souring milk, blackening grain or scaring livestock; leaving out milk and bread helps keep good relations.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kabouter-c03",
        "kabouter-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebuah kisah terkenal menceritakan para kabouter menghilang dari Kempen ke tempat tak dikenal setelah seorang pemburu menembak raja mereka, Kyrië. Dalam Legenda Sepatu Kayu, seorang kabouter mengajari orang Belanda membuat tiang pancang dan sepatu kayu. Ilustrator Belanda Rien Poortvliet berperan besar dalam citra kabouter modern lewat bukunya Leven en werken van de Kabouter (1976) dengan teks Wil Huygen.",
      "en": "A well-known tale tells how the kabouters vanished from the Campine to an unknown place after a hunter shot their king Kyrië. In the Legend of the Wooden Shoes a kabouter teaches a Dutchman to make piles and wooden shoes. The Dutch illustrator Rien Poortvliet shaped the modern kabouter image through his 1976 book Leven en werken van de Kabouter, with text by Wil Huygen.",

        "kabouter-c07"
      ]

      "context": "traditional-belief",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Lange Wapper adalah tokoh cerita rakyat Flandria, raksasa legendaris sekaligus penipu yang kisahnya terutama diceritakan di kota Antwerpen dan sekitarnya, meski kisah serupa juga ada di kota-kota Flandria lain. Menurut satu versi asal-usulnya, seorang petani menemukannya sebagai bayi dalam kubis merah raksasa di petak peterseli; anak itu diasuh keluarga di Antwerpen dan tumbuh menjadi pemuda kuat yang suka menolong.",
      "en": "Lange Wapper is a figure of Flemish folklore, a legendary giant and trickster whose tales were told especially in Antwerp and nearby towns, though similar stories exist in other Flemish cities. In one account of his origin a farmer found him as a baby inside a giant red cabbage in a parsley bed; the child was raised by an Antwerp family and grew into a strong youth who helped those in need.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lange-wapper-c01",
        "lange-wapper-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Suatu hari ia menyelamatkan seorang nenek dari tenggelam di Sungai Schelde, dan nenek itu memberinya kemampuan membesarkan atau mengecilkan diri; karena lebih suka tampil tinggi, ia dijuluki Lange Wapper. Ia menyamar sebagai anak untuk mendapat air susu, mengganggu pemabuk, dan mencurangi anak-anak dalam permainan, dan tawanya yang mengembik membuat orang menyebutnya \"setan air\".",
      "en": "After he rescued an old woman who was drowning in the Scheldt, she granted him the power to grow huge or shrink; since he liked best to appear tall, he became known as Lange Wapper. He disguised himself as a child to get breast milk, vexed drunkards and cheated children at games, and his bleating laugh led people to call him 'water devil'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lange-wapper-c02",
        "lange-wapper-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam satu kisah, seorang lelaki mabuk pulang dari penginapan di Antwerpen dan dikejar sosok misterius; setelah ia bersembunyi di ranjang, Lange Wapper setinggi rumah mengetuk jendelanya dan mengancam akan mematahkan lehernya bila ia mabuk lagi. Satu versi menyebut ia takut pada patung Perawan Maria, yang konon menjelaskan banyaknya patung itu di kota.",
      "en": "In one tale a drunk man walking home from an Antwerp inn is followed by a mysterious figure; after he hides in bed, Lange Wapper, as tall as a house, knocks at his window and threatens to break his neck if he gets drunk again. One version says he feared statues of the Virgin, which supposedly explains how many there are in the city.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lange-wapper-c04",
        "lange-wapper-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "lange-wapper-c01",
      "source_id": "lange-wapper-s1",
      "quote": "Lange Wapper is a Flemish folkloric character. He is a legendary giant and trickster whose folk tales were told especially in the city of Antwerp and its neighbouring towns, but similar tales are also prominent in other Flemish cities.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lange Wapper adalah tokoh cerita rakyat Flandria, raksasa legendaris dan penipu yang kisahnya terutama diceritakan di Antwerpen dan sekitarnya, meski kisah serupa juga menonjol di kota-kota Flandria lain.",
        "en": "Lange Wapper is a Flemish folk character, a legendary giant and trickster whose tales were told especially in Antwerp and nearby towns, with similar tales prominent in other Flemish cities."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lange-wapper-c02",
      "source_id": "lange-wapper-s1",
      "quote": "In a parsley bed, a farmer found a red cabbage that was so big it could not fit in a cauldron. ... One day he saved an old woman from drowning in the Scheldt river, and she gave him the ability to make himself big or small. Because he preferred to appear in his tall form, he gained the nickname Lange Wapper.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di petak peterseli seorang petani menemukan kubis merah raksasa berisi bayi; kelak anak itu menyelamatkan seorang nenek dari tenggelam di Sungai Schelde, dan nenek itu memberinya kemampuan membesarkan atau mengecilkan diri; karena suka tampil tinggi ia dijuluki Lange Wapper.",
        "en": "In a parsley bed a farmer found a huge red cabbage holding a baby; later the boy saved an old woman from drowning in the Scheldt, and she gave him the power to make himself big or small; preferring his tall form, he was nicknamed Lange Wapper."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lange-wapper-c03",
      "source_id": "lange-wapper-s1",
      "quote": "He would disguise himself as a child to receive breastmilk to drink, would vex drunkards, and would cheat children in games. His bleating laughter frightened the general public, who began to call him “water devil”.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menyamar sebagai anak untuk mendapat air susu, mengganggu pemabuk, dan mencurangi anak-anak dalam permainan; tawanya yang mengembik menakutkan orang sehingga ia disebut \"setan air\".",
        "en": "He disguised himself as a child to get breast milk, vexed drunkards and cheated children at games; his bleating laugh frightened people, who called him 'water devil'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lange-wapper-c04",
      "source_id": "lange-wapper-s1",
      "quote": "It is Lange Wapper, as big as a house, who warns, \"If you get drunk again, I will break your neck!\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sosok itu ternyata Lange Wapper, sebesar rumah, yang memperingatkan: \"Kalau kau mabuk lagi, akan kupatahkan lehermu!\"",
        "en": "It is Lange Wapper, as big as a house, who warns: 'If you get drunk again, I will break your neck!'"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lange-wapper-c05",
      "source_id": "lange-wapper-s2",
      "quote": "Le Lange Wapper (en français, le Grand Wapper) est un géant de légende qui joue de mauvais tours aux habitants de la ville d'Anvers, en Belgique. Il apparaît la nuit et poursuit les gens qui ont trop bu. Il sait se faire très grand et très petit.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lange Wapper (Prancis: le Grand Wapper) adalah raksasa legenda yang mempermainkan warga Antwerpen, Belgia; ia muncul di malam hari, mengejar orang yang terlalu banyak minum, dan bisa membuat dirinya sangat besar atau sangat kecil.",
        "en": "Lange Wapper (French le Grand Wapper) is a legendary giant who plays tricks on the people of Antwerp, Belgium; he appears at night, pursues those who drank too much, and can make himself very big or very small."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lange-wapper-c06",
      "source_id": "lange-wapper-s2",
      "quote": "Une version dit aussi que Lange Wapper avait peur des statues de la Vierge et que cela expliquerait le grand nombre de celle-ci dans la ville.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu versi menyebut Lange Wapper takut pada patung Perawan Maria, yang konon menjelaskan banyaknya patung itu di kota.",
        "en": "One version says Lange Wapper feared statues of the Virgin, which would explain their great number in the city."

    {
      "id": "lange-wapper-c07",
      "source_id": "lange-wapper-s2",
      "quote": "Il a aussi donné son nom au projet d'un immense pont (Lange Wapperbrug) surplombant une partie de la même ville. Les Anversois ont rejeté le projet par consultation populaire, le 19 octobre 2009 .",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya dipakai untuk proyek jembatan raksasa (Lange Wapperbrug) di atas sebagian kota itu, yang ditolak warga Antwerpen dalam konsultasi publik pada 19 Oktober 2009.",
        "en": "His name was given to a huge bridge project (Lange Wapperbrug) over part of the city, rejected by Antwerp residents in a public consultation on 19 October 2009."
      }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Nachzehrer adalah mayat hidup dalam cerita rakyat Jerman yang dari dalam kuburnya menyedot daya hidup kerabat dan tetangganya, kerap dengan mengunyah kain kafannya.",
    "en": "The Nachzehrer is a revenant of German folklore that, from inside its grave, drains the life of its relatives and neighbours, often by chewing its own shroud.",
    "claim_ids": [

      "nachzehrer-c03",

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Jerman, Nachzehrer (harfiah \"pemakan sesudah\", juga Nachtzehrer, \"pemakan malam\") adalah sejenis Wiedergänger atau mayat hidup yang diyakini dapat menyeret orang hidup menyusulnya ke dalam kematian, entah karena dengki atau karena ingin dekat dengan orang yang dicintainya. Kata ini mulai dipakai pada abad ke-19, tetapi kepercayaannya jauh lebih tua.",
      "en": "In German folklore the Nachzehrer (literally 'after-consumer', also Nachtzehrer, 'night-consumer') is a kind of Wiedergänger or revenant believed able to drag the living after it into death, whether from malice or a wish to be near its loved ones. The word came into use in the 19th century, but the belief is several centuries older.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nachzehrer-c01",
        "nachzehrer-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kepercayaan ini dulu umum terutama di Mark, dan berbeda dari anggapan lama para ahli folklor, sebarannya tidak terbatas pada wilayah timur dan timur laut Jerman, tetapi sampai ke Rheinland. Nachzehrer berkerabat dekat dengan vampir. Tanda yang sangat umum ialah kain kafan dan pakaian yang dimakannya sendiri, dan bahayanya dianggap paling besar bila orang hidup memberi jenazah barang miliknya sebagai bekal kubur.",
      "en": "The term was once common especially in the Mark, and contrary to an older folklorists' view the belief was not confined to Germany's east and north-east but reached the Rhineland. The Nachzehrer is closely related to the vampire. A very common sign was a shroud and clothing it had eaten itself, and the danger was thought greatest if the living had given the dead their own belongings as grave goods.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nachzehrer-c03",
        "nachzehrer-c04",
        "nachzehrer-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut tradisi, berbeda dari vampir yang harus meninggalkan kubur, Nachzehrer berbaring atau duduk di bawah tanah sambil menyedot daya hidup orang yang ditinggalkannya. Untuk mencegahnya, benda logam seperti gunting atau paku diletakkan di dadanya, dan kacang kering atau kerikil dituang ke peti, karena ia harus menghitungnya lebih dulu dan tak bisa melewati angka dua.",
      "en": "According to tradition, unlike a vampire that must leave its grave, the Nachzehrer lies or sits underground draining the life-force of those it left behind. To prevent this, metal objects such as scissors or nails laid on its chest, and dried pulses or pebbles poured into the coffin, since it had to count them first and could never get past two.",

      ]

        "en": "The word Nachzehrer came into use in the 19th century, but belief in the creature it names is several centuries older."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nachzehrer-c03",
      "source_id": "nachzehrer-s1",
      "quote": "This could involve devouring their own funeral shrouds and clothing – thought to be a very common sign of a nachzehrer. The danger was thought to be particularly great if the living had given the deceased some of their own possessions (e.g. as grave goods).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hal ini bisa berupa memakan kain kafan dan pakaiannya sendiri, yang dianggap tanda Nachzehrer yang sangat umum; bahayanya dianggap sangat besar bila orang hidup memberi jenazah sebagian barang miliknya, misalnya sebagai bekal kubur.",
        "en": "This could involve eating its own shroud and clothes, thought a very common sign of a Nachzehrer; the danger was held to be especially great if the living had given the dead some of their own possessions, e.g. as grave goods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nachzehrer-c04",
      "source_id": "nachzehrer-s2",
      "quote": "Nachzehrer ist eine früher vor allem in der Mark übliche Bezeichnung im deutschen Volksglauben für einen Wiedergänger oder Untoten, der den Vampiren nah verwandt ist",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nachzehrer adalah sebutan dalam kepercayaan rakyat Jerman, dulu terutama di Mark, untuk mayat hidup atau Untote yang berkerabat dekat dengan vampir.",
        "en": "Nachzehrer is a German folk-belief term, formerly common especially in the Mark, for a revenant or undead being closely related to vampires."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nachzehrer-c05",
      "source_id": "nachzehrer-s2",
      "quote": "Im Gegensatz zum Vampir, der sein Grab verlassen müsse, liege oder sitze der Nachzehrer unter der Erde und sauge den Lebenden – meistens seinen Hinterbliebenen oder den Bewohnern seines Dorfes – die Lebenskraft ab.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Berbeda dari vampir yang harus meninggalkan kubur, Nachzehrer berbaring atau duduk di bawah tanah dan menyedot daya hidup orang hidup, biasanya keluarga yang ditinggalkannya atau warga desanya.",
        "en": "Unlike the vampire, which must leave its grave, the Nachzehrer lies or sits underground and drains the life-force of the living, usually its survivors or fellow villagers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nachzehrer-c06",
      "source_id": "nachzehrer-s2",
      "quote": "Oft wurden bannende Metallgegenstände (Scheren, Nägel, Messer) auf die Brust des Toten gelegt. Häufig schütteten die Hinterbliebenen auch getrocknete Hülsenfrüchte oder Kieselsteine in den Sarg. ... Da er aber vom Teufel beseelt war, konnte er nie über zwei Erbsen oder Steine hinaus kommen",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Benda logam penangkal (gunting, paku, pisau) sering diletakkan di dada jenazah, dan keluarga menuang kacang kering atau kerikil ke peti; karena dirasuki Iblis, ia tak pernah bisa menghitung lebih dari dua kacang atau batu.",
        "en": "Warding metal objects (scissors, nails, knives) were often laid on the corpse's chest, and survivors poured dried pulses or pebbles into the coffin; being possessed by the Devil, it could never count past two peas or stones."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nachzehrer-c07",
      "source_id": "nachzehrer-s2",
      "quote": "Im Gegensatz zu einer lange verbreiteten Auffassung in der Volkskunde beschränkt sich das Verbreitungsgebiet des Nachzehrers keineswegs auf die teilweise slawisch geprägten Gebiete im Osten und Nordosten Deutschlands, sondern er war auch bis ins Rheinland verbreitet.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Berbeda dari anggapan lama dalam ilmu folklor, sebaran Nachzehrer tidak terbatas pada wilayah timur dan timur laut Jerman yang sebagian bercorak Slavia, tetapi juga sampai ke Rheinland.",
        "en": "Contrary to a long-held folklorists' view, the Nachzehrer was not confined to the partly Slavic east and north-east of Germany but was known as far as the Rhineland."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia bahasa Inggris dan Jerman; belum ada sumber di luar Wikipedia yang dikutip untuk entri ini."
    },

    }
  ]
}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Türst adalah sosok legenda dari komunitas petani Luzern, \"pemburu mengerikan\" yang meniup terompet berburunya di desa-desa pada bulan-bulan berbadai menjelang Epifani, diiringi anjing-anjing berkaki tiga.",
    "en": "Türst is a legendary figure of the farming communities of Lucerne, a 'dreadful huntsman' who blows his hunting horn through villages in the stormy months before Epiphany, followed by three-legged hounds.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "turst-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Türst, juga Dürst, adalah tokoh cerita rakyat dari komunitas petani Luzern yang diyakini berasal dari masa pra-Kristen. Ia digambarkan sebagai \"pemburu mengerikan\" yang harus diwaspadai saat cuaca buruk; pada bulan-bulan berbadai menjelang Epifani ia meniup terompet berburu menembus desa-desa bersama sekawanan anjing berkaki tiga yang menyalak.",
      "en": "Türst, also Dürst, is a folk figure of the farming communities of Lucerne believed to date from pre-Christian times. He is described as a 'dreadful huntsman' to beware of in stormy weather; in the tempestuous months before Epiphany he blows his hunting horn through the villages with a baying pack of three-legged hounds.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "turst-c01",
        "turst-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ciri khasnya adalah ancaman \"drü Schritt rechts, gang uswägs\" (\"tiga langkah ke kanan, minggir\"): pejalan kaki yang tidak menyingkir tepat waktu diubah menjadi salah satu anjingnya. Pintu lumbung harus dibiarkan terbuka agar ia bisa berburu menembus lumbung tanpa halangan, dan menurut kisah lain ia membuat sapi perah ketakutan sampai sakit dan berhenti menghasilkan susu.",
      "en": "His hallmark is the threat 'drü Schritt rechts, gang uswägs' ('three steps right, get out of the way'): a walker who fails to step aside in time is turned into one of his dogs. Barn doors should be left open so he can hunt through unhindered, and other tales say he frightens dairy cows until they sicken and stop giving milk.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "turst-c02",
        "turst-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisahnya berbeda dari daerah ke daerah. Di Horw ia diiringi anjing-anjing kecil yang dipimpin anjing bermata satu, istrinya Sträggele si penyihir buruk rupa, dan Pfaffenkellnerin, hantu bermata menyala. Di Rickenbach ia memakai pakaian berburu hijau dengan kawanan setengah anjing setengah babi, dan di Wolhusen warga mendirikan salib untuk menghindarinya.",
      "en": "His tales vary by region. In Horw he is followed by small dogs led by a one-eyed hound, his wife Sträggele, a hideous witch, and the Pfaffenkellnerin, a ghost with glowing eyes.       "locator": null,

      "statement": {
        "id": "Türst adalah tokoh cerita rakyat dari komunitas petani Luzern yang berasal dari masa pra-Kristen, digambarkan sebagai \"pemburu mengerikan\" yang harus diwaspadai saat cuaca berbadai; ia meniup terompet berburu menembus desa-desa pada bulan-bulan berbadai menjelang Epifani bersama kawanan anjing berkaki tiga.",
        "en": "Türst is a folk figure of Lucerne's farming communities dating to pre-Christian times, a 'dreadful huntsman' to beware of in stormy weather, who blows his horn through villages in the stormy months before Epiphany with a pack of three-legged hounds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "turst-c02",
      "source_id": "turst-s1",
      "quote": "Türst's distinguishing features are his threat \"drü Schritt rechts, gang uswägs\", or, roughly, \"step right; get out of my way\" in which the careless pedestrian is turned into a member of the dog-pack if he should fail to get out of Türst's way in time.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ciri khas Türst adalah ancaman \"drü Schritt rechts, gang uswägs\" (\"ke kanan, minggir\"): pejalan lalai yang tak menyingkir tepat waktu diubah menjadi anggota kawanan anjingnya.",
        "en": "Türst's hallmark is his threat 'drü Schritt rechts, gang uswägs' ('step right; get out of my way'): a careless walker who fails to move aside in time is turned into a member of his dog pack."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "turst-c03",
      "source_id": "turst-s1",
      "quote": "In Horw, Türst is accompanied by small hounds, a leading hound with only one eye, Türst's wife, Sträggele, a hideous witch, and the Pfaffenkellnerin, the ghost of a deceased parson's mistress with glowing eyes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Horw, Türst diiringi anjing-anjing kecil yang dipimpin anjing bermata satu, istrinya Sträggele si penyihir buruk rupa, dan Pfaffenkellnerin, hantu kekasih seorang pastor yang bermata menyala.",
        "en": "In Horw, Türst is followed by small hounds led by a one-eyed hound, his wife Sträggele, a hideous witch, and the Pfaffenkellnerin, the glowing-eyed ghost of a dead parson's mistress."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "turst-c04",
      "source_id": "turst-s1",
      "quote": "He appears in a green hunting costume in Rickenbach, accompanied by a pack of animals which are half dog and half pig. In Wolhusen, to avoid Türst, the citizens erected crosses. If a cross were to fall, the farmers would have bad luck until the cross stood straight again. The local people of Pilatus say that Türst is a ghost and a \"dreadful huntsman\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Rickenbach ia tampil berpakaian berburu hijau dengan kawanan setengah anjing setengah babi; di Wolhusen warga mendirikan salib untuk menghindarinya, dan bila salib roboh petani akan sial sampai salib ditegakkan; warga Pilatus menyebutnya hantu dan \"pemburu mengerikan\".",
        "en": "In Rickenbach he appears in green hunting dress with a half-dog, half-pig pack; in Wolhusen people raised crosses against him, and a fallen cross brought farmers bad luck until set upright; the people of Pilatus call him a ghost and a 'dreadful huntsman'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "turst-c05",
      "source_id": "turst-s2",
      "quote": "Der Türst (auch Dürst genannt) ist eine Sagenfigur aus dem Luzerner Hinterland, die noch aus heidnischen Zeiten stammt. Er wird als „höllischer Jäger“ bezeichnet",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Türst (juga disebut Dürst) adalah tokoh legenda dari pedalaman Luzern yang berasal dari masa kafir dan disebut \"pemburu neraka\".",
        "en": "Türst (also called Dürst) is a legendary figure from the Lucerne hinterland dating from heathen times, called the 'hellish huntsman'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "turst-c06",
      "source_id": "turst-s1",
      "quote": "It is also said that barn doors should stay open at this time, enabling Türst to hunt through the barn unhindered. Sträggele, his wife, appears in some Türst legends. Other legends say that Türst terrifies dairy cows, causing them to sicken and cease their milk production.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konon pintu lumbung harus dibiarkan terbuka pada masa itu agar Türst dapat berburu menembus lumbung tanpa halangan; istrinya Sträggele muncul dalam sebagian legenda, dan legenda lain menyebut ia menakuti sapi perah hingga sakit dan berhenti menghasilkan susu.",
        "en": "Barn doors should be left open at this time so Türst can hunt through unhindered; his wife Sträggele appears in some legends, and others say he terrifies dairy cows until they sicken and stop giving milk."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Valravn adalah gagak gaib dalam cerita rakyat Denmark masa Renaisans, ksatria yang dikutuk menjadi gagak dan hanya bisa bebas dengan meminum darah seorang anak lelaki.",
    "en": "The valravn is a supernatural raven of Danish Renaissance folklore, a knight cursed into raven form whose curse is broken only by drinking a boy's blood.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "valravn-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Valravn adalah gagak gaib dalam cerita rakyat Denmark masa Renaisans. Ia terutama muncul dalam balada rakyat \"Valravnen\" sebagai ksatria yang diubah menjadi gagak, yang kutukannya hanya dapat dipatahkan dengan meminum darah seorang anak lelaki. Versi tertua balada itu tercatat dalam naskah Jens Billes Håndskrift tahun 1555–1559, walau balada ini diduga berasal dari akhir Abad Pertengahan.",
      "en": "The valravn is a supernatural raven of Danish Renaissance folklore. It appears mainly in the folk ballad 'Valravnen' as a knight turned into a raven whose curse can be lifted only by drinking a boy's blood. The earliest version of the ballad is in Jens Billes Håndskrift of 1555–1559, though the ballad probably dates from the late Middle Ages.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "valravn-c01",
        "valravn-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam balada itu, gagak yang terbang di malam hari menawarkan diri membawa seorang gadis yang menangis ke negeri tunangannya dengan imbalan anak sulung mereka. Setelah anak itu lahir, gagak kembali, lalu menembus dada sang bayi dan meminum separuh darah jantungnya; ia pun menjadi ksatria paling rupawan, dan sang bangsawan sebelumnya gagal menebus putranya dengan harta.",
      "en": "In the ballad a night-flying raven offers to carry a weeping maiden to her betrothed's land in return for their firstborn son. When the child is born the raven returns, pierces the baby's chest and drinks half its heart-blood; he becomes the fairest knight, after the nobleman had failed to ransom his son with riches.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "valravn-c03",
        "valravn-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat Denmark akhir abad ke-19, bila seorang raja atau panglima gugur dan tidak segera dikuburkan, gagak-gagak memakannya dan berubah menjadi valravn; yang memakan jantung raja memperoleh seluruh pengetahuannya dan dapat berbuat sangat jahat. Kisah lain menggambarkannya sebagai makhluk setengah serigala setengah gagak. Menurut Jacob Grimm, padanan nama Jerman Hulu Kuno untuk valravn adalah walahraban.",
      "en": "According to late-19th-century Danish folklore, when a king or chieftain fell in battle and was not buried at once, ravens ate him and became valravne; the one that ate the king's heart gained all his knowledge and could do great evil. Other tales describe it as half wolf, half raven. According to Jacob Grimm, the Old High German equivalent of valravn is walahraban.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "valravn-c04",

        "valravn-c07"
      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "valravn-c01",
      "source_id": "valravn-s1",
      "quote": "A valravn is a supernatural raven in Danish renaissance folklore. It principally appears in the folk ballad \"Valravnen\" (Danish: The Valravn ) where it is depicted as a knight who was transformed into a raven, and whose curse can only be broken by consuming the blood of a boy.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Valravn adalah gagak gaib dalam cerita rakyat Denmark masa Renaisans yang terutama muncul dalam balada rakyat \"Valravnen\" sebagai ksatria yang diubah menjadi gagak, yang kutukannya hanya dapat dipatahkan dengan meminum darah anak lelaki.",
        "en": "A valravn is a supernatural raven of Danish Renaissance folklore, appearing mainly in the ballad 'Valravnen' as a knight transformed into a raven whose curse can only be broken by drinking a boy's blood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valravn-c02",
      "source_id": "valravn-s1",
      "quote": "The earliest version of the ballad is known from Jens Billes Håndskrift (Danish: Jens Bille's Handwritten Notes ), dated to 1555-1559, though the ballad is thought to have originated in the late Middle Ages.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi tertua balada ini dikenal dari Jens Billes Håndskrift, bertanggal 1555–1559, meski balada ini diduga berasal dari akhir Abad Pertengahan.",
        "en": "The earliest version of the ballad is known from Jens Billes Håndskrift, dated 1555–1559, though it probably originated in the late Middle Ages."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valravn-c03",
      "source_id": "valravn-s1",
      "quote": "\"The first son you have together, will you give to me: to your betrothed's land I will then take you.\" ... The raven impaled his chest, drank half his heart-blood: then he turned into the loveliest knight, to ever walk the earth.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam balada, gagak meminta anak lelaki pertama sang gadis sebagai imbalan membawanya ke negeri tunangannya; kemudian gagak itu menembus dada sang anak, meminum separuh darah jantungnya, dan menjadi ksatria paling rupawan di bumi.",
        "en": "In the ballad the raven asks for the maiden's first son in return for taking her to her betrothed's land; later it pierces the child's chest, drinks half his heart-blood and becomes the loveliest knight on earth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valravn-c04",
      "source_id": "valravn-s2",
      "quote": "Un valraven ou valravn est, dans le folklore danois, un corbeau surnaturel décrit comme le corbeau des tués. ... Après avoir dévoré le cœur d'un enfant, il prend la forme d'un chevalier ou d'une créature mi-loup mi-corbeau.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Denmark, valraven atau valravn adalah gagak gaib yang disebut \"gagak orang yang gugur\"; setelah memakan jantung seorang anak ia berwujud ksatria atau makhluk setengah serigala setengah gagak.",
        "en": "In Danish folklore the valraven or valravn is a supernatural raven called the 'raven of the slain'; after eating a child's heart it takes the form of a knight or a half-wolf, half-raven creature."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valravn-c05",
      "source_id": "valravn-s2",
      "quote": "Selon le folklore danois de la fin des années 1800, lorsqu'un roi ou un chef de guerre est tué dans une bataille et n'est pas enterré immédiatement, les corbeaux viennent et le dévorent. Cet acte les transforme en valraven. Le valraven qui a mangé le cœur du roi acquiert toute la connaissance de l'homme et peut alors accomplir des actes d'une grande malveillance",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat Denmark akhir abad ke-19, bila raja atau panglima gugur dalam perang dan tidak segera dikuburkan, gagak-gagak memakannya dan berubah menjadi valraven; yang memakan jantung raja memperoleh seluruh pengetahuannya dan dapat berbuat sangat jahat.",
        "en": "In late-1800s Danish folklore, when a king or war chief was killed in battle and not buried at once, ravens devoured him and became valraven; the one that ate the king's heart gained all his knowledge and could do great evil."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valravn-c06",
      "source_id": "valravn-s1",
      "quote": "The raven flies at night, during the day he mustn't: ... Out then walked the nobleman and offered him castles so lovely: and in addition half his land, if only he could keep his son.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Balada itu dibuka dengan larik bahwa gagak terbang di malam hari dan tak boleh terbang di siang hari; kelak sang bangsawan menawarkan istana-istana dan separuh negerinya agar boleh mempertahankan putranya.",
        "en": "The ballad opens with the raven flying at night and forbidden by day; later the nobleman offers it lovely castles and half his land if only he may keep his son."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valravn-c07",
      "source_id": "valravn-s2",
      "quote": "Selon Jacob Grimm, le vilde ravn ou vilde valravn (corbeau sauvage) serait l'équivalent du troll diabolique dans les vieilles chansons danoises. Le nom du Valraven en vieux haut-allemand est walahraban, équivalent du danois valravn.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Jacob Grimm, vilde ravn atau vilde valravn (gagak liar) setara dengan troll jahat dalam lagu-lagu Denmark lama; nama Jerman Hulu Kuno untuk valraven adalah walahraban, padanan valravn dalam bahasa Denmark.",
        "en": "According to Jacob Grimm the vilde ravn or vilde valravn (wild raven) is equivalent to the evil troll of old Danish songs; the Old High German name is walahraban, equivalent to Danish valravn."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valravn-c08",
      "source_id": "valravn-s2",
      "quote": "comme le groupe de musique danoise moderne Valravn, qui s'inspire des chants traditionnels.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Grup musik Denmark modern Valravn terinspirasi dari lagu-lagu tradisional.",
        "en": "The modern Danish band Valravn draws on traditional songs."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Baldanders adalah makhluk mitos sastra Jermanik yang terus berubah wujud, berasal dari puisi Hans Sachs dan terkenal lewat novel Simplicius Simplicissimus karya Grimmelshausen.",
    "en": "Baldanders is a shape-shifting creature of Germanic literary myth, created in a poem by Hans Sachs and made famous by Grimmelshausen's novel Simplicius Simplicissimus.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "baldanders-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Baldanders, \"segera lain\", adalah makhluk mitos sastra Jermanik yang bersifat protean, selalu berubah. Ia tokoh dalam novel Simplicius Simplicissimus karya Grimmelshausen, dalam bagian lanjutannya (1669) atau Buku Keenam, yang diambil dari puisi Hans Sachs bertanggal 31 Juli 1534; Hans Sachs kemungkinan menurunkannya dari Proteus, pengubah wujud dalam mitologi Yunani-Romawi.",
      "en": "Baldanders, 'soon different', is a creature of Germanic literary myth with protean qualities. He is a character in Grimmelshausen's novel Simplicius Simplicissimus, in its Continuatio (1669) or Sixth Book, taken from a poem by Hans Sachs dated 31 July 1534; Sachs probably derived him from Proteus, the shapeshifter of Greco-Roman mythology.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "baldanders-c01",
        "baldanders-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam novel itu, sang tokoh utama menemukan patung batu seorang pahlawan Jermanik kuno di hutan; patung itu mengaku bernama Baldanders lalu berturut-turut menjadi pohon ek, babi betina, sosis, padang semanggi, bunga, permadani sutra, dan benda lain, sebelum kembali menjadi manusia dan mengajari Simplicius berbagai keterampilan, termasuk berbicara dengan benda mati. Urutan benda itu menggambarkan siklus alam tentang hal-hal yang binasa dan lahir kembali.",
      "en": "In the novel the hero finds a stone statue of an ancient Germanic hero in a forest; it says it is Baldanders and changes in turn into an oak, a sow, a sausage, a meadow of clover, a flower, a silk carpet and other things, before becoming a man again and teaching Simplicius various arts, including speaking with inanimate objects. The sequence shows a natural cycle of things that perish and are reborn.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "baldanders-c03",
        "baldanders-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut uraian Sachs dan sumber lain, Baldanders melambangkan perubahan terus-menerus dalam alam dan masyarakat. Jorge Luis Borges memasukkannya dalam El libro de los seres imaginarios, dan kelompok musik Ougenweide menggubah lagu Bald anders berdasarkan ciptaan Hans Sachs.",
      "en": "According to Sachs and other descriptions, Baldanders symbolises constant change in nature and society. Jorge Luis Borges included him in El libro de los seres imaginarios, and the band Ougenweide wrote the song Bald anders based on Hans Sachs's creation.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "baldanders-c05",
        "baldanders-c06"
      ]
    }

      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Baldanders adalah tokoh novel Simplicius Simplicissimus karya Grimmelshausen dalam Continuatio (1669) atau Buku Keenam, diambil dari puisi Hans Sachs Baldanderst bertanggal 31 Juli 1534; Sachs kemungkinan menurunkannya dari Proteus, pengubah wujud mitologi Yunani-Romawi.",
        "en": "Baldanders is a character in Grimmelshausen's Simplicius Simplicissimus, in its Continuatio (1669) or Sixth Book, taken from Hans Sachs's poem Baldanderst dated 31 July 1534; Sachs probably derived it from Proteus, the Greco-Roman shapeshifter."

    {
      "id": "baldanders-c03",
      "source_id": "baldanders-s1",
      "quote": "Baldanders subsequently changes into a succession of forms: an oak tree, a sow, a bratwurst sausage, then a peasant's excrement, a meadow of clovers, cow dung (Kuhfladen ), a flower, mulberry tree, and silk carpet. ... in a natural cycle of things that perish and are reborn.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Baldanders lalu berubah menjadi serangkaian wujud: pohon ek, babi betina, sosis bratwurst, kotoran, padang semanggi, kotoran sapi, bunga, pohon murbei, dan permadani sutra; urutan ini menggambarkan siklus alam tentang hal-hal yang binasa dan lahir kembali.",
        "en": "Baldanders then changes into a series of forms: an oak, a sow, a bratwurst, excrement, a clover meadow, cow dung, a flower, a mulberry tree and a silk carpet; the sequence shows a natural cycle of things perishing and being reborn."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "baldanders-c04",
      "source_id": "baldanders-s2",
      "quote": "Darin stößt der Protagonist in einem Wald auf eine Steinfigur, die einen Gott aus einem alten germanischen Tempel darstellt. Als er die Figur mit dem Finger berührt, sagt sie, sie sei Baldanders („jedesmal ein anderer“) ... Am Ende wird Baldanders wieder zum Menschen, um Simplicius in verschiedenen Fertigkeiten – unter anderem auch darin, mit leblosen Dingen zu sprechen – zu unterrichten.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam novel itu sang tokoh utama menemukan di hutan patung batu dewa dari kuil Jermanik kuno; saat disentuh, patung itu berkata ia Baldanders (\"setiap kali yang lain\"), dan akhirnya kembali menjadi manusia untuk mengajari Simplicius berbagai keterampilan, termasuk berbicara dengan benda mati.",
        "en": "In the novel the protagonist finds in a forest a stone figure of a god from an old Germanic temple; touched, it says it is Baldanders ('each time another'), and finally becomes a man again to teach Simplicius various skills, including talking to inanimate things."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "baldanders-c05",
      "source_id": "baldanders-s1",
      "quote": "According to Sachs’ and collected descriptions, the Baldanders is a creature that is symbolic for the continual change in nature and society",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut uraian Sachs dan uraian lain yang dihimpun, Baldanders melambangkan perubahan terus-menerus dalam alam dan masyarakat.",
        "en": "According to Sachs and collected descriptions, Baldanders symbolises continual change in nature and society."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "baldanders-c06",
      "source_id": "baldanders-s2",
      "quote": "Auch Jorge Luis Borges führt in dem von ihm 1974 herausgegeben Bestiarium El libro de los seres imaginarios einen Baldanders auf. Basierend auf Hans Sachs’ Schöpfung komponierte die Gruppe Ougenweide das Lied Bald anders, in der die Figur Baldanders die Hauptrolle spielt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jorge Luis Borges memasukkan Baldanders dalam bestiarium El libro de los seres imaginarios (1974), dan kelompok Ougenweide menggubah lagu Bald anders berdasarkan ciptaan Hans Sachs.",
        "en": "Jorge Luis Borges listed a Baldanders in his 1974 bestiary El libro de los seres imaginarios, and the group Ougenweide wrote the song Bald anders based on Hans Sachs's creation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "baldanders-c07",
      "source_id": "baldanders-s1",
      "quote": "The name Baldanders is also used for a recurring character in Gene Wolfe's science fantasy series The Book of the New Sun.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Baldanders juga dipakai untuk tokoh berulang dalam seri fantasi ilmiah The Book of the New Sun karya Gene Wolfe.",
        "en": "The name Baldanders is also used for a recurring character in Gene Wolfe's science-fantasy series The Book of the New Sun."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Dáinn (Nordik Kuno, \"yang mati\") atau Dain adalah tokoh dalam mitologi Nordik; kebanyakan kisah menyebutnya kurcaci, sebagian lainnya raja para elf. Ia disebut kurcaci dalam daftar kurcaci Völuspá dan dalam bait Sigvatr Þórðarson, tetapi dalam Hávamál ia elf yang mengukir rune: \"Odin bagi Æsir, dan Dain bagi para elf\".",
      "en": "Dáinn (Old Norse 'dead') or Dain is a figure of Norse mythology; most tales make him a dwarf, some a king of elves.     "id": "Egil adalah pemanah legendaris dalam Völundarkviða dan Thidreks saga, saudara pandai besi Völund, yang terkenal karena memanah apel dari kepala putranya.",
    "en": "Egil is a legendary archer of the Völundarkviða and Thidreks saga, brother of the smith Völund, famed for shooting an apple from his son's head.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "egil-brother-of-volund-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Egil adalah pahlawan legendaris dalam Völundarkviða dan Thidreks saga. Namanya berasal dari bentuk Proto-Jermanik *Agilaz, dan legenda yang sama tercermin pada Ægil di Peti Franks dalam bahasa Inggris Kuno serta Aigil di gesper Pforzen dalam bahasa Alemannik. Bentuk awal legendanya tampaknya tentang pemanah termasyhur yang bersama istrinya Alruna mempertahankan sebuah benteng dari banyak penyerang, adegan yang juga terpahat pada Peti Franks.",
      "en": "Both the Völundarkviða and the Thidreks saga feature Egil as a hero of legend. His name derives from Proto-Germanic *Agilaz, and the same story surfaces in Old English Ægil on the Franks Casket and Alamannic Aigil on the Pforzen buckle. The earliest form of the legend seems to have been about a famous archer who, with his wife Alruna, defended a keep against many attackers, a scene also carved on the Franks Casket.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "egil-brother-of-volund-c01",
        "egil-brother-of-volund-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Völundarkviða, Egil adalah putra seorang raja Finn, dengan kakak Slagfiðr dan adik Völund. Ketiga bersaudara itu menikahi valkyrie yang mereka temui dalam rupa angsa; Egil menikahi Ölrún, putri Kaisar Romawi. Sebagai saudara Wieland si pandai besi, ia juga termasuk lingkaran legenda Dietrich von Bern.",
      "en": "In the Völundarkviða Egil is a son of a Finnish king, with Slagfiðr as his elder brother and Völund as his younger. The three brothers marry valkyries they meet in swan shape; Egil weds Ölrún, daughter of the Roman Emperor. As a brother of Wieland the smith he also belongs to the legend cycle of Dietrich von Bern.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "egil-brother-of-volund-c03",
        "egil-brother-of-volund-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Thidreks saga ia pemanah ulung yang dipaksa Raja Nidung memanah apel dari kepala putranya yang berusia tiga tahun; ia menyiapkan lebih dari satu anak panah, dan anak panah pertama sudah membelah apel itu. Ia juga mengumpulkan bulu burung untuk sayap saudaranya Wieland, lalu ketika disuruh menembak Wieland yang melarikan diri di udara, ia sengaja membidik kantong darah di ketiak Wieland sehingga saudaranya selamat.",
      "en": "In the Thidreks saga he is a master bowman whom King Nidung compels to hit an apple placed on his three-year-old son's head; he readied more than one arrow, and the first split the apple. He also gathered bird feathers for the wings of his brother Wieland, and when ordered to shoot Wieland as he fled through the air, he deliberately hit a bladder of blood under Wieland's arm so his brother escaped.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "egil-brother-of-volund-c05",

    {
      "id": "egil-brother-of-volund-c01",
      "source_id": "egil-brother-of-volund-s1",
      "quote": "Egil is a legendary hero of the Völundarkviða and the Thidreks saga. The name is from Proto-Germanic *Agilaz, and the same legend is reflected in Old English Ægil [ˈæɡil] of the Franks Casket and Alamannic Aigil of the Pforzen buckle.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Egil adalah pahlawan legendaris Völundarkviða dan Thidreks saga; namanya dari Proto-Jermanik *Agilaz, dan legenda yang sama tercermin pada Ægil dalam bahasa Inggris Kuno di Peti Franks dan Aigil dalam bahasa Alemannik di gesper Pforzen.",
        "en": "Egil is a legendary hero of the Völundarkviða and Thidreks saga; his name comes from Proto-Germanic *Agilaz, and the same legend appears in Old English Ægil on the Franks Casket and Alamannic Aigil on the Pforzen buckle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "egil-brother-of-volund-c02",
      "source_id": "egil-brother-of-volund-s1",
      "quote": "The Proto-Germanic form of the legend may only be guessed at, but it appears likely that Egil was a renowned archer who defended a keep together with his wife Alruna, against numerous attackers. ... The Franks Casket shows the scene of Aegil and his wife enclosed in the keep, with Aegil shooting arrows against attacking troops.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bentuk Proto-Jermanik legenda ini hanya bisa ditebak, tetapi tampaknya Egil adalah pemanah termasyhur yang bersama istrinya Alruna mempertahankan benteng dari banyak penyerang; Peti Franks menampilkan Aegil dan istrinya di dalam benteng, Aegil memanahi pasukan penyerang.",
        "en": "The Proto-Germanic legend can only be guessed at, but Egil seems to have been a renowned archer who with his wife Alruna defended a keep against many attackers; the Franks Casket shows Aegil and his wife in the keep, Aegil shooting at attacking troops."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "egil-brother-of-volund-c03",
      "source_id": "egil-brother-of-volund-s1",
      "quote": "In the Völundarkviða, Egil is a son of a Finn king, his elder brother being Slagfiðr, his younger one Völund. The three brothers marry valkyries they encounter in swans' form ... while Egil marries Ölrún, a daughter of the Roman Emperor (Kiár of Valland).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Völundarkviða, Egil adalah putra raja Finn dengan kakak Slagfiðr dan adik Völund; ketiganya menikahi valkyrie yang mereka temui dalam rupa angsa, dan Egil menikahi Ölrún, putri Kaisar Romawi (Kiár dari Valland).",
        "en": "In the Völundarkviða Egil is a Finnish king's son, Slagfiðr his elder and Völund his younger brother; the three marry valkyries met in swan form, Egil marrying Ölrún, daughter of the Roman Emperor (Kiár of Valland)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "egil-brother-of-volund-c04",
      "source_id": "egil-brother-of-volund-s1",
      "quote": "In the Thidreks saga, Egil acts as a masterly archer, once he is forced by king Nidung to shoot an apple from the head of his son. He readies two arrows, but succeeds with the first one.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Thidreks saga, Egil adalah pemanah ulung yang pernah dipaksa Raja Nidung memanah apel dari kepala putranya; ia menyiapkan dua anak panah tetapi berhasil dengan yang pertama.",
        "en": "In the Thidreks saga Egil is a master archer once forced by King Nidung to shoot an apple from his son's head; he readies two arrows but succeeds with the first."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "egil-brother-of-volund-c05",
      "source_id": "egil-brother-of-volund-s2",
      "quote": "Egil hatte seinen Sohn mit sich. Drei Jahre alt. Der König legte einen Apfel auf sein Haupt und bat Egil den Apfel entzwei zu schießen. Egil nahm drei Pfeile aus seinem Köcher",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam terjemahan bebas petikan saga, Egil membawa putranya yang berusia tiga tahun; raja meletakkan apel di kepalanya dan meminta Egil membelahnya, dan Egil mengambil tiga anak panah dari tabungnya.",
        "en": "In a free translation of the saga passage, Egil had his three-year-old son with him; the king put an apple on his head and told Egil to split it, and Egil took three arrows from his quiver."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "egil-brother-of-volund-c06",
      "source_id": "egil-brother-of-volund-s2",
      "quote": "Das zweite Mal, als er für seinen Bruder Federn von Wildvögeln sammelt. Aus diesen Federn macht sich Wieland die Flügel ... Egil zielt aber, wie vorher mit seinem Bruder vereinbart, auf eine Kalbsblase voll Blut, welche Wieland unter seiner Achsel trägt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Egil juga mengumpulkan bulu burung liar untuk saudaranya, yang dipakai Wieland membuat sayap; ketika disuruh menembaknya, Egil sesuai kesepakatan membidik kantong kandung kemih anak sapi berisi darah yang dibawa Wieland di ketiaknya.",
        "en": "Egil also gathered wild birds' feathers for his brother, from which Wieland made wings; when ordered to shoot him, Egil, as agreed, aimed at a calf's bladder full of blood carried under Wieland's arm."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "egil-brother-of-volund-c07",
      "source_id": "egil-brother-of-volund-s2",
      "quote": "Der Bogenschütze Egil (auch Egill o. Eigill) ist ein Bruder von Wieland dem Schmied und damit eingebunden in den Sagenkreis um Dietrich von Bern.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemanah Egil (juga Egill atau Eigill) adalah saudara Wieland si pandai besi dan karena itu termasuk dalam lingkaran legenda Dietrich von Bern.",
        "en": "The archer Egil (also Egill or Eigill) is a brother of Wayland the Smith and thus part of the legend cycle of Dietrich von Bern."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Fossegrim adalah roh air atau troll dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia, pemain biola ulung di air terjun dan sungai yang dapat mengajarkan keahliannya dengan imbalan persembahan.",
    "en": "Fossegrim is a water spirit or troll of Scandinavian folklore, a master fiddler of waterfalls and streams who can teach his skill in return for an offering.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "fossegrim-c01",
      "fossegrim-c02",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Fossegrim, juga disebut grim saja dalam bahasa Norwegia atau Strömkarlen (\"Lelaki Sungai\") dalam bahasa Swedia, adalah roh air atau troll dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia. Ia sering digambarkan sebagai lelaki tampan tanpa busana yang bermain biola di sungai dan air terjun, dikaitkan dengan roh penggilingan kvernknurr dan berkerabat dengan roh air nokken, sehingga di Swedia kadang disebut näcken.",
      "en": "Fossegrim, also simply the grim in Norwegian or Strömkarlen ('the Stream Man') in Swedish, is a water spirit or troll of Scandinavian folklore. He is often shown as a handsome naked man playing the fiddle in streams and waterfalls, linked with the mill spirit kvernknurr and related to the water spirit nokken, so that in Sweden he is sometimes called näcken.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fossegrim-c01",
        "fossegrim-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia pemain biola yang luar biasa: bunyi hutan, angin, dan air bermain di senar biolanya. Lagu strömkarl Swedia konon punya sebelas variasi, dan yang terakhir disimpan untuk roh malam.",
      "en": "He is an exceptionally gifted fiddler: the sounds of forest, wind and water play over his strings. In Sweden the strömkarl's tune reportedly comes in eleven variations, of which the last belongs to the spirits of the night.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fossegrim-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia mau mengajarkan keahliannya dengan imbalan persembahan makanan pada Kamis malam secara rahasia, seperti kambing jantan putih yang dilempar ke air terjun yang mengalir ke utara atau daging domba asap. Bila dagingnya kurang, ia hanya mengajari cara menyetel biola. Pemain biola terkenal seperti Myllarguten dan Ole Bull dikabarkan belajar darinya.",
      "en": "He will teach his art for a secret food offering on a Thursday evening, such as a white he-goat thrown into a north-flowing waterfall or smoked mutton.         "fossegrim-c03",
        "fossegrim-c04"
      ]
    }

      "statement": {
        "id": "Dungeons & Dragons memperkenalkan fossegrim dengan nama \"fossergrim\" sebagai monster dalam Deities & Demigods (1980), dan dalam gim God of War: Ragnarok (2022) grim menjadi kelas musuh mirip katak.",
        "en": "Dungeons & Dragons introduced the fossegrim as the monster 'fossergrim' in Deities & Demigods (1980), and in God of War: Ragnarok (2022) grims are a class of frog-like enemies."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fossegrim-c06",
      "source_id": "fossegrim-s2",
      "quote": "Fossegrim è stato associato allo spirito del mulino (kvernknurr) ed è imparentato con lo spirito dell'acqua (nokken) ed è talvolta chiamato anche näcken in Svezia. È associato ai corsi d'acqua (il nome svedese \"Strömkarlen\" significa \"L'uomo del corso d'acqua\") e in particolare ai corsi d'acqua a cascata (foss in norvegese)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Fossegrim dikaitkan dengan roh penggilingan (kvernknurr), berkerabat dengan roh air (nokken), dan di Swedia kadang disebut näcken; ia terkait aliran air (nama Swedia Strömkarlen berarti \"lelaki aliran air\"), terutama yang berair terjun (foss dalam bahasa Norwegia).",
        "en": "Fossegrim is linked with the mill spirit (kvernknurr), related to the water spirit (nokken) and sometimes called näcken in Sweden; he is tied to watercourses (Swedish Strömkarlen means 'the man of the stream'), especially those with waterfalls (Norwegian foss)."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Heinzelmännchen adalah roh rumah tangga penolong, sejenis kobold, yang dikaitkan dengan kota Köln dan konon mengerjakan semua pekerjaan warga di malam hari.",
    "en": "The Heinzelmännchen are helpful household spirits or kobolds associated with Cologne, said to have done all the citizens' work at night.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "heinzelmannchen-c01",
      "heinzelmannchen-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Heinzelmännchen adalah roh rumah tangga penolong atau kobold yang dikaitkan dengan kota Köln di Jerman, mirip brownie dari Skotlandia. Menurut sage Köln, makhluk-makhluk kecil ini mengerjakan pekerjaan warga di malam hari saat mereka tidur, sehingga penduduk Köln bisa bermalas-malasan di siang hari. Ukurannya yang kecil, topi runcingnya, dan kerajinannya menunjukkan mereka segolongan dengan kobold, Wichtel, dan kurcaci.",
      "en": "The Heinzelmännchen are helpful household spirits or kobolds tied to the city of Cologne in Germany, comparable to the brownies of Scotland. In the Cologne legend these little beings did the townspeople's work at night while they slept, letting the people of Cologne idle away the day. Their small size, pointed caps and diligence mark them as kin of kobolds, Wichtel and dwarfs.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "heinzelmannchen-c01",
        "heinzelmannchen-c02",
        "heinzelmannchen-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kebiasaan itu berakhir ketika istri seorang penjahit begitu ingin melihat mereka sehingga menaburkan kacang polong di lantai bengkel agar mereka terpeleset. Makhluk-makhluk itu murka, menghilang, dan tak pernah kembali, sehingga sejak itu warga Köln harus bekerja sendiri. Kisah ini pertama kali ditulis guru Köln Ernst Weyden pada 1826 dan menjadi populer lewat balada August Kopisch tahun 1836.",
      "en": "This ended when a tailor's wife, eager to catch sight of them, scattered peas on the workshop floor to make them slip. The angered creatures vanished and never came back, so from then on Cologne's citizens had to do their own work. The tale was first written down by the Cologne teacher Ernst Weyden in 1826 and became popular through August Kopisch's 1836 ballad.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "heinzelmannchen-c02",
        "heinzelmannchen-c04",
        "heinzelmannchen-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ahli folklor Marianne Rumpf (1976) mengajukan dua penjelasan asal nama: Heinzelmännlein sebagai sebutan boneka akar mandragora yang dipakai sebagai roh rumah, dan Heinz atau Heinzenkunst sebagai nama alat pembuang air di pertambangan, sehingga para operatornya mungkin disebut Heinzelmänner. Dalam dialek Köln namanya Heizemännche.",
      "en": "The folklorist Marianne Rumpf (1976) offered two explanations of the name: Heinzelmännlein as a name for mandrake dolls used as house spirits, and Heinz or Heinzenkunst as names of a mine-draining device, whose operators may have been called Heinzelmänner. In the Cologne dialect the name is Heizemännche.",

        "heinzelmannchen-c07"
      ]

    {
      "id": "heinzelmannchen-c01",
      "source_id": "heinzelmannchen-s1",
      "quote": "are helpful household spirits or kobolds associated with the city of Cologne in Germany, akin to brownies of Scotland.",
      "locator": null,

      "statement": {
        "id": "Heinzelmännchen adalah roh rumah tangga penolong atau kobold yang dikaitkan dengan kota Köln di Jerman, mirip brownie dari Skotlandia.",
        "en": "The Heinzelmännchen are helpful household spirits or kobolds associated with Cologne in Germany, akin to the brownies of Scotland."
      }

    {
      "id": "heinzelmannchen-c02",
      "source_id": "heinzelmannchen-s1",
      "quote": "The little house gnomes are said to have done all the work of the citizens of Cologne during the night, so that the inhabitants of Cologne could be very lazy during the day. ... this went on until a tailor's wife got so curious to see the gnomes that she scattered peas onto the floor of the workshop to make the gnomes slip and fall.       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gnom-gnom rumah kecil itu konon mengerjakan semua pekerjaan warga Köln di malam hari sehingga penduduk bisa bermalas-malasan di siang hari, sampai istri seorang penjahit yang ingin melihat mereka menaburkan kacang polong di lantai bengkel agar mereka terpeleset; mereka murka, menghilang, dan tak pernah kembali.",
        "en": "The little house gnomes are said to have done all of Cologne's work at night so the citizens could be lazy by day, until a tailor's wife, curious to see them, scattered peas on the workshop floor to make them slip; infuriated, they vanished and never returned."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heinzelmannchen-c03",
      "source_id": "heinzelmannchen-s1",
      "quote": "A two-pronged theory on the origin of the name was proposed by Marianne Rumpf [de] (1976), first from the form \"Heinzelmännlein\" as a colloquial name for mandrake dolls ... The genuine Kölsch (Colognian) dialect form should be Heizemann/Heizemännche",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Marianne Rumpf (1976) mengajukan teori ganda asal nama, pertama dari bentuk Heinzelmännlein, sebutan sehari-hari untuk boneka mandragora; bentuk asli dalam dialek Köln adalah Heizemann atau Heizemännche.",
        "en": "Marianne Rumpf (1976) proposed a two-pronged theory of the name, first from Heinzelmännlein, a colloquial name for mandrake dolls; the genuine Cologne dialect form is Heizemann or Heizemännche."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heinzelmannchen-c04",
      "source_id": "heinzelmannchen-s1",
      "quote": "This legend of the Heinzelmännchen was first written down by the Cologne teacher Ernst Weyden (1805–1869) in 1826.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda Heinzelmännchen pertama kali ditulis oleh guru Köln Ernst Weyden (1805–1869) pada 1826.",
        "en": "The legend was first written down by the Cologne teacher Ernst Weyden (1805–1869) in 1826."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heinzelmannchen-c05",
      "source_id": "heinzelmannchen-s2",
      "quote": "Die Heinzelmännchen waren der Sage nach Kölner Hausgeister. Sie verrichteten nachts, wenn die Bürger schliefen, deren Arbeit. ... Neben ihrer geringen Größe zeigen auch typische Attribute, wie die Zipfelmütze und ihr Fleiß, dass die Heinzelmännchen zur Gruppe der Kobolde, Wichtel und Zwerge gehören.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut sage, Heinzelmännchen adalah roh rumah Köln yang mengerjakan pekerjaan warga di malam hari saat mereka tidur; ukuran kecil, topi runcing, dan kerajinannya menunjukkan mereka segolongan dengan kobold, Wichtel, dan kurcaci.",
        "en": "By legend the Heinzelmännchen were Cologne house spirits who did the citizens' work at night while they slept; their small size, pointed cap and diligence mark them as kin of kobolds, Wichtel and dwarfs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heinzelmannchen-c06",
      "source_id": "heinzelmannchen-s2",
      "quote": "Populär geworden ist sie in der Gedichtfassung (Ballade) von 1836 des Malers und Dichters August Kopisch (1799–1853), mit der er die ursprünglich rheinländische Sage aus dem Siebengebirge nach Köln transportierte",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah ini menjadi populer lewat versi puisi (balada) tahun 1836 karya pelukis dan penyair August Kopisch, yang memindahkan sage asal Siebengebirge di Rheinland ke Köln.",
        "en": "The tale became popular through the 1836 ballad by the painter and poet August Kopisch, who moved the originally Rhineland legend from the Siebengebirge to Cologne."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heinzelmannchen-c07",
      "source_id": "heinzelmannchen-s2",
      "quote": "Zum anderen waren Heinz oder Heinzenkunst Namen von Vorrichtungen im Bergbau zur Wasserabführung. Daher könnten nach Rumpf die Bediener solch hilfreicher Vorrichtungen Heinzelmänner genannt worden sein.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kedua, Heinz atau Heinzenkunst adalah nama alat pembuang air di pertambangan, sehingga menurut Rumpf para operatornya mungkin disebut Heinzelmänner.",
        "en": "Second, Heinz or Heinzenkunst were names of mine-draining devices, so according to Rumpf their operators may have been called Heinzelmänner."

      "source_id": "heinzelmannchen-s2",
      "quote": "Der Weihnachtsmarkt auf dem Heumarkt und dem Platz Alter Markt in Köln nennt sich „Heinzels Wintermärchen“. Auf ihm werden Heinzelmännchenfiguren in verschiedenen Situationen dargestellt.",

    "id": "Hinzelmann adalah kobold dalam mitologi Jerman utara, roh rumah tangga yang sifatnya mendua, yang menurut legenda menghantui Kastel Hudemühlen di Niedersachsen sejak 1584.",
    "en": "Hinzelmann was a kobold of northern German mythology, an ambivalent household spirit who by legend haunted Hudemühlen Castle in Lower Saxony from 1584.",

    {
      "id": "Hinzelmann (aslinya Hintzelmann, juga Katermann atau Katzen-Veit) adalah kobold dalam mitologi Jerman utara, roh rumah tangga bersifat mendua yang mirip Puck (Robin Goodfellow). Para sarjana modern menganggapnya berbeda dari Heinzelmännchen di Köln yang namanya mirip. Jacob Grimm mengaitkan kelompok nama ini dengan kucing, karena Hinz adalah nama kucing dalam Reineke Fuchs.",
      "en": "Hinzelmann (originally Hintzelmann, also Katermann or Katzen-Veit) was a kobold of northern German mythology, an ambivalent household spirit similar to Puck (Robin Goodfellow). Modern scholars hold him distinct from the similarly named Heinzelmännchen of Cologne. Jacob Grimm linked this group of names to the cat, since Hinz was the cat's name in Reineke Fuchs.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hinzelmann-c01",
        "hinzelmann-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut legenda, ia mulai menghantui Kastel Hudemühlen di Niedersachsen pada 1584. Ia tampil tanpa wujud dengan suara anak kecil.",
      "en": "According to the legend he began haunting Hudemühlen Castle in Lower Saxony in 1584. At first there were only banging noises; then he began talking with the servants, saying he was called Lüring, had a wife named Hille Bingels and used to live in the Bohemian Forest mountains. He appeared without a body and with a child's voice.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hinzelmann-c03",
        "hinzelmann-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia rajin mencuci piring, menggosok perkakas dapur, menemukan barang hilang, dan merawat kuda, tetapi bisa memukul dengan tongkat bila nasihatnya diabaikan; para pelayan wajib menyiapkan semangkuk susu manis berisi remah roti putih untuknya. Ia penipu yang keisengannya umumnya tak berbahaya, dan pernah meramalkan kematian seorang tamu yang mengejeknya. Pada 1588 ia pergi dengan sukarela dan menetap di Eystrup.",
      "en": "He diligently washed dishes, scrubbed cookware, found lost things and groomed horses, yet could strike with a stick when his advice was ignored; the servants had to set out a bowl of sweet milk with crumbled white bread for him. He was a trickster whose pranks were mostly harmless, and once foretold the death of a visitor who mocked him. In 1588 he left of his own accord and settled in Eystrup.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hinzelmann-c04",
        "hinzelmann-c05",
        "hinzelmann-c06",
        "hinzelmann-c07"
