  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "hinzelmann-c01",
      "source_id": "hinzelmann-s1",
      "quote": "also known as Katermann or Katzen-Veit ) was a kobold in the mythology of northern Germany. He was described as a household spirit of ambivalent nature, similar to Puck (Robin Goodfellow). The similar-sounding Heinzelmann (Heinzelmännchen) of Cologne is considered a distinct and separate being by modern scholars.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hinzelmann (aslinya Hintzelmann, juga Katermann atau Katzen-Veit) adalah kobold dalam mitologi Jerman utara, roh rumah tangga bersifat mendua yang mirip Puck (Robin Goodfellow); Heinzelmännchen dari Köln yang namanya mirip dianggap makhluk terpisah oleh sarjana modern.",
        "en": "Hinzelmann (originally Hintzelmann, also Katermann or Katzen-Veit) was a kobold of northern German mythology, an ambivalent household spirit like Puck (Robin Goodfellow); the similar-sounding Heinzelmännchen of Cologne is held by modern scholars to be distinct."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hinzelmann-c02",
      "source_id": "hinzelmann-s1",
      "quote": "Grimm explains that Hinz was the name of the cat in Reineke Fuchs (Reynard the Fox), so that Hinz/Hinze became a name that was emblematic for a \"cat\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Grimm menjelaskan bahwa Hinz adalah nama kucing dalam Reineke Fuchs, sehingga Hinz atau Hinze menjadi nama lambang \"kucing\".",
        "en": "Grimm explains that Hinz was the cat's name in Reineke Fuchs, so Hinz or Hinze became emblematic of 'cat'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hinzelmann-c03",
      "source_id": "hinzelmann-s1",
      "quote": "According to this legend, the Hinzelmann (\"Hintzelmann\" in the original source) began haunting the castle Hudemühlen in Lower Saxony beginning in the year 1584. First only its presence was felt from the banging noises. ... began telling his personal details, that he was named Lüring, with a wife named Hille Bingels, and that he used to live in the Bohemian Forest mountain range.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, Hinzelmann (dalam sumber asli Hintzelmann) mulai menghantui Kastel Hudemühlen di Niedersachsen pada 1584; mula-mula hanya terdengar gedoran, lalu ia mengaku bernama Lüring, beristri Hille Bingels, dan dulu tinggal di pegunungan Hutan Bohemia.",
        "en": "By the legend Hinzelmann ('Hintzelmann' in the source) began haunting Hudemühlen Castle in Lower Saxony in 1584; at first only banging was heard, then he said he was named Lüring, had a wife Hille Bingels and once lived in the Bohemian Forest mountains."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hinzelmann-c04",
      "source_id": "hinzelmann-s1",
      "quote": "Hinzelmann would usefully and dedicatedly perform kitchen chores such as dish-washing and scrubbing cookware, recover lost items, and groom horses. It gave advice or pep talks, but could strike with a stick when his words were not paid attention to. ... The cook or the servants were obliged to put out a bowl of sweet milk with crumbled white bread in it",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hinzelmann rajin mengerjakan tugas dapur seperti mencuci piring dan menggosok perkakas, menemukan barang hilang, dan merawat kuda; ia memberi nasihat tetapi bisa memukul dengan tongkat bila diabaikan, dan juru masak atau pelayan wajib menyiapkan semangkuk susu manis berisi remah roti putih.",
        "en": "Hinzelmann dutifully did kitchen chores such as washing dishes and scrubbing cookware, recovered lost items and groomed horses; he gave advice but could strike with a stick if ignored, and the cook or servants had to set out a bowl of sweet milk with crumbled white bread."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hinzelmann-c05",
      "source_id": "hinzelmann-s1",
      "quote": "The Hinzelmann was certainly a trickster, but his pranks were generally harmless. A comparison has been made between the Hinzelmann and Puck (Robin Goodfellow) of English tradition.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hinzelmann jelas seorang penipu, tetapi keisengannya umumnya tak berbahaya; ia dibandingkan dengan Puck (Robin Goodfellow) dari tradisi Inggris.",
        "en": "Hinzelmann was certainly a trickster, but his pranks were generally harmless; he has been compared with Puck (Robin Goodfellow) of English tradition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hinzelmann-c06",
      "source_id": "hinzelmann-s2",
      "quote": "Hinzelmann (auch Lüring) ist die Bezeichnung für einen Kobold als Sagengestalt. Er soll Gutes, wie die Erledigung von Hausarbeit, vollbracht haben. Er konnte aber auch böse werden, wenn man ihn reizte. Dabei trat Hinzelmann ohne Gestalt auf und hatte die Stimme eines Kindes. ... 1588 ging er freiwillig von Hudemühlen fort und ließ sich danach in Eystrup nieder.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hinzelmann (juga Lüring) adalah kobold dalam sage yang konon berbuat baik seperti mengerjakan pekerjaan rumah, tetapi bisa jahat bila diganggu; ia tampil tanpa wujud dengan suara anak kecil, dan pada 1588 pergi dengan sukarela dari Hudemühlen lalu menetap di Eystrup.",
        "en": "Hinzelmann (also Lüring) is a legendary kobold said to have done good, such as housework, but could turn nasty if provoked; he appeared without form and with a child's voice, and in 1588 left Hudemühlen of his own accord and settled in Eystrup."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hinzelmann-c07",
      "source_id": "hinzelmann-s1",
      "quote": "Hinzelmann also predicted the demise of a visitor to the castle named lord Falkenberg, who had taunted the spirit and hassled him by playing tricks. The annoyed Hinzelmann pronounced the lord's death in veiled terms",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hinzelmann juga meramalkan kematian tamu kastel bernama Tuan Falkenberg, yang mengejek dan mengusilinya; Hinzelmann yang kesal mengucapkan kematian tuan itu dengan kata-kata samar.",
        "en": "Hinzelmann also predicted the death of a castle visitor, Lord Falkenberg, who had taunted and tricked him; the annoyed spirit pronounced the lord's death in veiled terms."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hinzelmann-c08",
      "source_id": "hinzelmann-s2",
      "quote": "Der Legende nach wird Hinzelmann bei gleichzeitiger Erfüllung dreier Bedingungen wiederkommen: Wenn die Köchin einen kaputten Wassereimer mit einer kaputten Kelle füllt, wenn der Dackel unter einem Weidenbaume Junge wirft und wenn ein Kind mit nur einem Auge zur Welt kommt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, Hinzelmann akan kembali bila tiga syarat terpenuhi bersamaan: juru masak mengisi ember rusak dengan gayung rusak, anjing dachshund beranak di bawah pohon dedalu, dan seorang anak lahir bermata satu.",
        "en": "By legend Hinzelmann will return when three conditions are met at once: the cook fills a broken bucket with a broken ladle, a dachshund whelps under a willow, and a child is born with only one eye."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Litr (juga Lit; dalam bahasa Nordik Kuno berarti \"warna, rupa\") adalah nama yang disandang seorang kurcaci dan seorang jötunn dalam mitologi Nordik. Namanya diterjemahkan sebagai \"warna\", \"corak\", atau \"rupa\", dan berasal dari bentuk Proto-Jermanik yang direkonstruksi *ulituz.",
      "en": "Litr (also Lit; Old Norse for 'colour, appearance') is the name of a dwarf and of a jötunn in Norse mythology. The name has been rendered 'colour', 'hue' or 'appearance', and comes from a reconstructed Proto-Germanic *ulituz.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "litr-c01",
        "litr-c03"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Dalam Gylfaginning karya Snorri Sturluson, ketika Thor menyucikan api pembakaran Baldr dengan Mjöllnir, seorang kurcaci bernama Litr berlari di depan kakinya; Thor menendangnya ke dalam api dan ia terbakar.       "en": "In Snorri Sturluson's Gylfaginning, as Thor hallowed Baldr's pyre with Mjöllnir, a dwarf named Litr ran before his feet; Thor kicked him into the fire and he burned. Litr is also listed as a dwarf in the Völuspá, and a dwarf of that name appears in Áns saga bogsveigis, forced by its hero to make him a bow.",
      "claim_ids": [

      "en": "In a stanza by Bragi Boddason quoted in the Skáldskaparmál, Thor is called 'Lit's men's fight-challenger'; since Thor is the giants' foe, Litr here is generally taken to be a giant. This led John Lindow to suggest there was probably originally only one Litr, a giant.",

      "quote": "En una estrofa del escaldo Bragi Boddason citada por Snorri en Skáldskaparmál Litr es también mencionado en un kenning para Thor: «El que desafía a luchar a los hombres de Lit» («Litar flotna fangboði»). Dado que Thor es el enemigo de los gigantes, generalmente se asume que, en este kenningar, Litr debe hacer referencia a un gigante.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam bait skald Bragi Boddason yang dikutip Snorri dalam Skáldskaparmál, Litr disebut dalam kenning untuk Thor, \"penantang tarung orang-orang Lit\" (Litar flotna fangboði); karena Thor musuh para raksasa, Litr di sini umumnya dianggap raksasa.",
        "en": "In a stanza by the skald Bragi Boddason quoted by Snorri in the Skáldskaparmál, Litr appears in a kenning for Thor, 'challenger of Lit's men' (Litar flotna fangboði); as Thor is the giants' enemy, Litr here is generally taken as a giant."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "litr-c06",
      "source_id": "litr-s2",
      "quote": "Esto llevó a John Lindow a sugerir que probablemente hubiera solo un Litr original, un gigante",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hal ini membuat John Lindow menduga bahwa semula mungkin hanya ada satu Litr, seorang raksasa.",
        "en": "This led John Lindow to suggest there was probably only one original Litr, a giant."
      }
    }
  ],

    "en": "Mótsognir is the first dwarf and ruler of the dwarfs in Norse mythology, named in the Völuspá as the greatest of them.",

      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Mótsognir (\"yang meminum kekuatan\"), juga Móðsognir dalam naskah Hauksbók (\"yang meminum keberanian\"), adalah penguasa para kurcaci. Völuspá 10 menyebutnya \"penguasa semua kurcaci\". Menurut Gylfaginning 14 karya Snorri, para kurcaci mula-mula berwujud belatung di daging Ymir, lalu atas keputusan para dewa memperoleh akal dan rupa manusia; Mótsognir adalah kurcaci pertama dan Durinn yang kedua.",
      "en": "In Norse mythology Mótsognir ('he who drinks in might'), also Móðsognir in the Hauksbók manuscript ('he who drinks in courage'), is the ruler of the dwarfs. Völuspá 10 calls him 'lord of all the dwarves'. According to Snorri's Gylfaginning 14, the dwarfs first took shape as maggots in Ymir's flesh, then by the gods' decision gained reason and human form; Mótsognir was the first dwarf and Durinn the second.",

        "motsognir-c02"

    {
      "id": "Menurut Völuspá 9, para dewa memutuskan membentuk kurcaci dari darah Brimir dan tulang Bláinn, ungkapan yang biasa ditafsirkan sebagai darah dan daging raksasa purba Ymir. Para sarjana memperdebatkan tafsiran Snorri karena teks baitnya berbeda dari naskah lain.       "quote": "\"the dwarfs had taken shape first and acquired life in the flesh of Ymir and were then maggots, but by decision of the gods they became conscious with intelligence and had the shape of men though they live in the earth and in rocks.       "statement": {

    {
      "id": "motsognir-c03",
      "source_id": "motsognir-s1",
      "quote": "According to Völuspà 9, the dwarfs were created by ginnheilög goð, \"the high holy gods\", who resolved to fashion them \"out of Brimir's blood and Blainn's bones,\" a phrase typically interpreted to mean the blood and flesh of the primordial giant, Ymir.",

      "statement": {

      "id": "motsognir-c04",
      "source_id": "motsognir-s2",
      "quote": "Modsognir, auch Motsognir (altnordisch Módsognir bzw. Mótsognir), ist in der nordischen Mythologie der erste Zwerg und wurde von den Göttern erschaffen. Umstritten ist die Bedeutung des Namens, am ehesten könnte man ihn mit ‚der Müde, der Kraftlose‘ wiedergeben",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Modsognir atau Motsognir adalah kurcaci pertama dalam mitologi Nordik yang diciptakan para dewa; arti namanya diperdebatkan, paling mungkin \"yang lelah, yang tak bertenaga\".",
        "en": "Modsognir or Motsognir is the first dwarf in Norse mythology, created by the gods; the meaning of the name is disputed, most likely 'the weary, powerless one'."

      "source_id": "motsognir-s1",

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Skogsrå adalah makhluk perempuan penghuni hutan dalam cerita rakyat Swedia: tampak seperti perempuan cantik dari depan, tetapi dari belakang berekor dan berpunggung berongga.",
    "en": "The skogsrå is a female forest being of Swedish folklore: a beautiful woman from the front, but with a tail and hollow back when seen from behind.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "skogsra-c01",
      "skogsra-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Skogsrå (\"rå hutan\"), juga disebut skogsfru (\"nyonya hutan\"), skogsjungfru (\"gadis hutan\"), atau skogssnuva, adalah makhluk mitos perempuan penghuni hutan dalam cerita rakyat Swedia, sangat mirip huldre dari Norwegia. Ia tampil sebagai perempuan cantik yang tampak ramah; dari depan ia seperti perempuan biasa, tetapi dari belakang sering berekor dengan punggung berongga dan busuk, satu kaki manusia dan satu kaki kuda, serta kulit seperti kulit kayu.",
      "en": "The skogsrå ('forest rå'), also called skogsfru ('mistress of the forest'), skogsjungfru ('maiden of the forest') or skogssnuva, is a female forest being of Swedish folklore, much like the Norwegian huldre. She shows herself as a lovely woman of apparently kindly mood; face to face she seems an ordinary woman, yet her rear view frequently reveals a tail, a back that is hollow and decayed, one human foot beside a horse's foot, and bark-like skin.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "skogsra-c01",
        "skogsra-c02",
        "skogsra-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Lelaki yang terpikat mengikutinya ke hutan akan disesatkan sementara ia menertawakan korbannya; seorang lelaki yang mengira diantar pulang mendapati dirinya di rawa. Pemburu yang tergoda bisa diberi keberuntungan berburu, tetapi dihukum bila tak setia kepadanya, dan ia juga melimpahkan kasih dan karunia kepada pembuat arang atau penebang kayu.",
      "en": "Men lured into following her into the forest are led astray while she laughs at them; one beguiled man who thought he was being taken home found himself in a swamp. A seduced hunter may be granted good hunting luck but punished if unfaithful to her, and she also shows favour and bounty to charcoal burners and woodcutters.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "skogsra-c03",
        "skogsra-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Banyak kisah menceritakan cara lelaki melepaskan diri darinya, misalnya menembaknya atau menipunya agar membuka rahasia ramuan; tanaman daphne (tibast) dan valerian (vändelrot) dapat menjadi jimat pelindung. Konon ia dapat dibunuh dengan peluru perak atau senapan yang diisinya sendiri.",
      "en": "Many tales tell how men got rid of her, for example by shooting her or tricking her into revealing a herbal secret; the plants daphne (tibast) and valerian (vändelrot) serve as protective talismans. She can reportedly be killed with a silver bullet or a gun she herself has loaded.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "skogsra-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "claim_ids": [
        "skogsra-c05"
      ]
    }

  "modern_depictions": [],

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "skogsra-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skogsr%C3%A5",
      "title": "Skogsrå",
      "author": null,

    },
    {
      "id": "skogsra-s2",
      "url": "https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skogsr%C3%A5et",
      "title": "Skogsrået",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (fr)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "fr",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "skogsra-c01",
      "source_id": "skogsra-s1",
      "quote": "skogsfru (definite: skogsfrun , 'the mistress of the forest'), skogsjungfru (definite: skogsjungfrun , 'the maiden of the forest'), skogssnua , skogssnuva , or skogsnymf (definite: skogssnuvan , skogsnymfen , 'the forest nymph'), is a mythical female creature (or rå ) of the forest in Swedish folklore.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Skogsrå, juga skogsfru (\"nyonya hutan\"), skogsjungfru (\"gadis hutan\"), skogssnua, skogssnuva, atau skogsnymf (\"nimfa hutan\"), adalah makhluk mitos perempuan (rå) penghuni hutan dalam cerita rakyat Swedia.",
        "en": "The skogsrå, also skogsfru ('mistress of the forest'), skogsjungfru ('maiden of the forest'), skogssnua, skogssnuva or skogsnymf ('forest nymph'), is a mythical female forest creature (rå) of Swedish folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "skogsra-c02",
      "source_id": "skogsra-s1",
      "quote": "appears in the form of a beautiful woman with a seemingly friendly temperament. She appears like a woman from the front but seen from behind she often has a tail and a hollow, rotten back, a human foot and a horse foot, and skin like tree bark.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tampil sebagai perempuan cantik yang tampak ramah; dari depan seperti perempuan, tetapi dari belakang sering berekor, berpunggung berongga dan busuk, berkaki satu manusia dan satu kuda, serta berkulit seperti kulit kayu.",
        "en": "She appears as a beautiful, seemingly friendly woman; from the front like a woman, but from behind often with a tail, a hollow rotten back, one human foot and one horse foot, and skin like bark."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "skogsra-c03",
      "source_id": "skogsra-s1",
      "quote": "The men who are enticed into following her into the forest are led astray, and she laughs at the victim. A beguiled man thought he was led back home, when everything disappeared and he found himself in a swamp.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lelaki yang terpikat mengikutinya ke hutan disesatkan dan ia menertawakan korbannya; seorang lelaki yang mengira diantar pulang mendapati semuanya lenyap dan dirinya berada di rawa.",
        "en": "Men enticed into following her into the forest are led astray as she laughs at them; one beguiled man thought he was led home, but everything vanished and he found himself in a swamp."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "skogsra-c04",
      "source_id": "skogsra-s1",
      "quote": "If the seduced man is a hunter, he may be rewarded with good luck in the hunt, but should he be unfaithful to the skogsrå, he will be punished with dire inconveniences. She is also said to bestow her affection and bounty upon the charcoal maker, or woodcutter.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bila lelaki yang tergoda seorang pemburu, ia bisa diganjar keberuntungan berburu, tetapi dihukum berat bila tak setia; ia juga konon melimpahkan kasih dan karunianya kepada pembuat arang atau penebang kayu.",
        "en": "If the seduced man is a hunter he may be rewarded with hunting luck, but punished harshly if unfaithful; she is also said to bestow her favour and bounty on charcoal makers or woodcutters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "skogsra-c05",
      "source_id": "skogsra-s1",
      "quote": "there is usually inserted lines of verse naming the daphne (Swedish: tibast ) and valerian (vändelrot ). These two plants can be used as talisman to protect against her. ... It is said she can be killed by a silver bullet or with a gun loaded by herself.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah-kisah biasanya disisipkan larik yang menyebut daphne (tibast) dan valerian (vändelrot), dua tanaman yang dapat dipakai sebagai jimat pelindung terhadapnya; konon ia dapat dibunuh dengan peluru perak atau senapan yang diisinya sendiri.",
        "en": "Tales usually include verse lines naming daphne (tibast) and valerian (vändelrot), two plants usable as talismans against her; she can reportedly be killed by a silver bullet or a gun she loaded herself."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "skogsra-c06",
      "source_id": "skogsra-s2",
      "quote": "Une skogsrå ( ; skogsrået dans la forme définie en suédois) est une créature féminine surnaturelle de la forêt dans le folklore suédois, très similaire à l'huldre norvégienne. ... La créature est aussi désignée par skogsfrun, skogssnuvan, skogsnymfen ou råndan.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Skogsrå adalah makhluk perempuan gaib penghuni hutan dalam cerita rakyat Swedia, sangat mirip huldre Norwegia, dan juga disebut skogsfrun, skogssnuvan, skogsnymfen, atau råndan.",
        "en": "The skogsrå is a supernatural female forest creature of Swedish folklore, very like the Norwegian huldre, also called skogsfrun, skogssnuvan, skogsnymfen or råndan."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia bahasa Inggris dan Prancis; belum ada sumber di luar Wikipedia yang dikutip untuk entri ini."

    }
  ]
}

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Vardøger adalah roh pendahulu dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia: penampakan langkah, suara, atau rupa seseorang yang tiba lebih dulu sebelum orang itu sendiri datang.",
    "en": "The vardøger is a spirit predecessor of Scandinavian folklore: the steps, voice or likeness of a person arriving ahead of the person themself.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "vard-ger-c01",
      "vard-ger-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Vardøger, juga disebut vardyvle atau vardyger, adalah roh pendahulu dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia. Kisah-kisahnya mirip déjà vu yang terbalik: roh dengan langkah kaki, suara, bau, atau rupa seseorang tiba lebih dulu di suatu tempat, sehingga para saksi mengira telah melihat atau mendengar orang itu sebelum ia benar-benar datang. Dalam cerita rakyat Finlandia, gagasan ini dikenal sebagai etiäinen.",
      "en": "The vardøger, also called vardyvle or vardyger, is a spirit predecessor of Scandinavian folklore. Its stories are like déjà vu in reverse: a spirit bearing someone's footsteps, voice, scent or looks reaches a place first, so witnesses think they have seen or heard the person before he or she actually arrives. In Finnish folklore the idea is known as etiäinen.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vard-ger-c01",
        "vard-ger-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Berbeda tipis dari doppelgänger, vardøger tidak bermakna seram, dan disamakan dengan kembaran gaib atau bentuk bilokasi. Kata Norwegia vardøgr berarti bunyi atau penampakan pertanda seseorang sebelum ia tiba, dan berasal dari bahasa Nordik Kuno varðhygi, dari vǫrð, \"penjaga\", dan hugr, \"pikiran, roh\"; di Swedia gagasan yang sama disebut vård.",
      "en": "Subtly unlike the doppelgänger, the vardøger has no sinister overtone and is likened to a phantom double or a form of bilocation. The Norwegian word vardøgr means a premonitory sound or sight of a person before arrival, from Old Norse varðhygi, from vǫrð, 'guard', and hugr, 'mind, spirit'; in Sweden the same idea is called vård.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vard-ger-c02",
        "vard-ger-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat Norwegia, melihat vardøger seseorang sering hanya berarti orang itu sedang dalam perjalanan. Karena vardøger semacam roh pelindung, orang yang memilikinya biasa dianggap orang baik yang dijaga roh baik; semula ia dianggap sejenis fylgja.",
      "en": "According to Norwegian folklore, seeing someone's vardøger often just means that person is on the way. Because the vardøger is a kind of guardian spirit, those who have one are commonly called good people protected by a kindly spirit; originally it was considered a kind of fylgja.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vard-ger-c04",
        "vard-ger-c05",
        "vard-ger-c06"
      ]
    }

      "quote": "nearly déjà vu in substance, but in reverse, where a spirit with the subject's footsteps, voice, scent, or appearance and overall demeanor precedes them in a location or activity ... This bears a subtle difference from a doppelgänger, with a less sinister connotation. It has been likened to being a phantom double, or form of bilocation.       "locator": null,

      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisahnya mirip déjà vu terbalik: roh dengan langkah, suara, bau, atau rupa seseorang mendahuluinya di suatu tempat; berbeda tipis dari doppelgänger dan kurang seram, ia disamakan dengan kembaran gaib atau bilokasi, dan dalam cerita rakyat Finlandia disebut etiäinen.",
        "en": "Its stories are like déjà vu in reverse: a spirit with a person's footsteps, voice, scent or looks precedes them; subtly different from the doppelgänger and less sinister, it is likened to a phantom double or bilocation, and in Finnish folklore is called etiäinen."

    {

      "source_id": "vard-ger-s1",
      "quote": "Vardøgr is a Norwegian word defined as ‘‘premonitory sound or sight of a person before he arrives’’. ...       "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vardøgr adalah kata Norwegia yang berarti \"bunyi atau penampakan pertanda seseorang sebelum ia tiba\"; asalnya bahasa Nordik Kuno varðhygi, dari vǫrð (\"penjaga\") dan hugr (\"pikiran, kehendak, roh\"); di Swedia gagasan yang sama disebut vård.",
        "en": "Vardøgr is a Norwegian word meaning 'premonitory sound or sight of a person before he arrives', from Old Norse varðhygi, of vǫrð ('guard') and hugr ('mind, will, spirit'); in Sweden the same concept is called vård."

    {
      "id": "vard-ger-c04",
      "source_id": "vard-ger-s2",
      "quote": "Si ves un vardøger, según el folclore noruego, eso significa que te estas cruzando en el camino de una persona. A menudo solo quiere decir que esa persona de la cual has visto su vardøger está en camino.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat Norwegia, melihat vardøger berarti kau berpapasan di jalan seseorang, dan sering hanya berarti orang itu sedang dalam perjalanan.",
        "en": "According to Norwegian folklore, seeing a vardøger means you are crossing someone's path, and often only that the person is on the way."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vard-ger-c05",
      "source_id": "vard-ger-s2",
      "quote": "originalmente, un vardøger era considerado un fylgja, una especie de espíritu guardián de esa persona.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Semula, vardøger dianggap sebuah fylgja, sejenis roh pelindung orang itu.",
        "en": "Originally a vardøger was considered a fylgja, a kind of guardian spirit of that person."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vard-ger-c06",
      "source_id": "vard-ger-s2",
      "quote": "Debido a que el vardøger es un tipo de espíritu guardián, se suele decir de aquellos que tienen un vardøger que son buenas personas, ya que un espíritu benéfico les protege. En resumen, podríamos decir que un vardøger es la contraparte de un doppelgänger.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena vardøger sejenis roh pelindung, orang yang memilikinya biasa disebut orang baik yang dilindungi roh baik; singkatnya vardøger adalah lawan doppelgänger.",
        "en": "Since the vardøger is a kind of guardian spirit, people who have one are usually called good people protected by a kindly spirit; in short, the vardøger is the counterpart of a doppelgänger."
      }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Vörðr adalah roh penjaga dalam mitologi Nordik yang diyakini mengikuti jiwa (hugr) setiap orang sejak lahir sampai mati.",
    "en": "The vörðr is a warden spirit of Norse mythology believed to follow every person's soul (hugr) from birth to death.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "vor-r-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, vǫrðr (\"pemelihara\" atau \"penjaga\") adalah roh penjaga yang diyakini mengikuti jiwa atau hugr setiap orang sejak lahir sampai mati. Dalam bahasa Swedia Kuno kata padanannya varþer, dan dalam bahasa Swedia modern vård; kepercayaan pada roh penjaga ini tetap kuat dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia sampai beberapa abad terakhir.",
      "en": "In Norse mythology the vǫrðr ('caretaker' or 'guardian') is a warden spirit believed to follow every person's soul, or hugr, from birth to death. Old Swedish had the matching word varþer and modern Swedish vård; belief in such guardian spirits stayed strong in Scandinavian folklore until the last few centuries.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vor-r-c01",
        "vor-r-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kadang roh penjaga ini menampakkan diri sebagai cahaya kecil atau sebagai sosok orang yang dijaganya. Merasakan penjaga orang lain bisa menimbulkan sensasi fisik seperti gatal di tangan atau hidung, dan penjaga itu bisa tiba sebelum orangnya sendiri. Penjaga orang yang sudah meninggal dapat menjadi hantu yang menghuni tempat atau orang tertentu, berbeda dari mayat hidup yang lebih sadar seperti draugar.",
      "en": "At times the warden showed itself as a small light or in the shape of its person. Sensing another's warden could cause bodily feelings such as an itching hand or nose, and the warden might arrive before the person did. A dead person's warden could become a revenant haunting particular places or people, distinct from more conscious undead such as the draugar.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vor-r-c02",
        "vor-r-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pohon tua yang tumbuh di pekarangan, sering linden, ash, atau elm, dapat dijuluki \"pohon penjaga\" (Swedia: vårdträd) dan diyakini melindungi pertanian dari nasib buruk; mematahkan daun atau rantingnya dianggap pelanggaran berat. Kata Inggris wraith diturunkan dari vǫrðr. Di bawah pengaruh Kristen, sebagian orang memandang roh ini seperti hati nurani baik dan buruk, sebagian lagi sebagai malaikat pelindung.",
      "en": "An old tree on the farmstead, often a linden, ash or elm, might be called a 'warden tree' (Swedish vårdträd) and was believed to protect the farm from bad luck; breaking a leaf or twig from it was a serious offence. The English word wraith is derived from vǫrðr. Under Christian influence some came to see the spirit as a good and a bad conscience, others as guardian angels.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vor-r-c01",
        "vor-r-c03",
        "vor-r-c06"
      ]
    }

    "id": "Wiedergänger, \"yang berjalan lagi\", adalah sebutan Jerman untuk orang mati yang kembali ke dunia orang hidup, sering dalam wujud jasmani, dan biasanya berniat jahat.",
    "en": "Wiedergänger, 'one who walks again', is the German term for the dead who return to the world of the living, often in bodily form and usually ill-disposed.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam bahasa Jerman, Wiedergänger (juga ditulis Widergänger) adalah istilah untuk mayat hidup dan berbagai fenomena hantu dari beragam budaya, berarti \"yang berjalan lagi\", sekerabat dengan kata Skandinavia gjenganger. Inti mitosnya adalah orang mati yang kembali ke dunia orang hidup, sering dalam wujud jasmani sebagai Untote. Mereka menyeramkan, biasanya berniat jahat, dan kerap menimbulkan masalah bagi orang hidup.",
      "en": "In German, Wiedergänger (also spelled Widergänger) is a term for revenants and various ghost phenomena from many cultures, meaning 're-walker', cognate with Scandinavian gjenganger. The core of the myth is the dead returning to the world of the living, often in bodily form as undead. They are uncanny, usually ill-disposed, and often trouble the living.",

        "en": "The French medievalist Jean-Claude Schmitt, studying Central European images of the 12th–16th centuries, divides the undead by depiction into revenant, Lazarus, soul and ghost types; the revenant resembles a living person."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wiederganger-c06",
      "source_id": "wiederganger-s2",
      "quote": "Gerade in den katholisch geprägten Gebieten verschmolz der Glaube an den aufhockenden Wiedergänger mit dem Seelenglauben",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terutama di wilayah Katolik, kepercayaan pada Wiedergänger penunggang punggung bercampur dengan kepercayaan pada jiwa.",
        "en": "Especially in Catholic areas, belief in the back-riding Wiedergänger merged with belief in souls."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {

    "id": "Askafroa, \"istri pohon ash\", adalah roh penjaga pohon ash dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia yang berbahaya dan harus ditenangkan dengan persembahan pada Rabu Abu.",
    "en": "The Askafroa, 'wife of the ash tree', is a dangerous guardian spirit of the ash tree in Scandinavian folklore who must be appeased with offerings on Ash Wednesday.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Askafroa (bahasa Swedia, \"istri pohon ash\"), dalam bahasa Denmark Askefrue, adalah makhluk legenda dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia yang mirip hamadryad Yunani. Ia penjaga pohon ash dan dianggap makhluk jahat yang banyak menimbulkan kerusakan, sehingga orang perlu memberinya persembahan pada Rabu Abu.",
      "en": "The Askafroa (Swedish 'wife of the ash tree'), Danish Askefrue, is a legendary creature of Scandinavian folklore similar to the Greek hamadryads. She guards the ash tree and was considered a malicious being that did much damage, so people had to make offerings to her on Ash Wednesday.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "askafroa-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia tinggal di pohon ash di wilayah Skåne, Swedia, dan merupakan jiwa pohon itu; bila pohonnya ditebang, ia akan membalas dendam. Sarjana Swedia Hyltén-Cavallius mencatat dalam Wärend och Wirdarne bahwa di Ljunit Hundred para tetua menuang air ke akar pohon ash sebelum matahari terbit pada Rabu Abu sambil berkata: \"Kini aku berkorban, agar kau tidak mencelakai kami\". Mereka juga percaya siapa pun yang mematahkan dahan atau ranting pohon ash akan jatuh sakit.",
      "en": "She lives in ash trees in Skåne in Sweden and is the tree's soul; if the tree is felled she takes revenge. The Swedish scholar Hyltén-Cavallius recorded in Wärend och Wirdarne that in Ljunit Hundred the elders poured water on the ash's roots before sunrise on Ash Wednesday, saying: 'Now I sacrifice, so that you do us no harm'. They also believed anyone breaking branches or twigs from the ash would fall ill.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "askafroa-c02",
        "askafroa-c03",

    {
      "id": "Pohon ash memiliki tempat penting dalam mitologi Nordik: bersama pohon ek, ia pohon paling sakti yang diberi kekuatan khusus, seperti menghentikan darah atau menangkal bisa ular, dan pohon mitologis Yggdrasil pun pohon ash. Askafroa tetap makhluk yang cukup samar dan jarang tampil dalam fiksi modern, walau muncul dalam gim daring Dark Age of Camelot.",
      "en": "The ash tree holds a prominent place in Norse mythology: with the oak it was the most magical tree, credited with powers such as stopping bleeding or countering snake venom, and the mythological tree Yggdrasil was an ash.       "id": "askafroa-c02",
      "source_id": "askafroa-s1",
      "quote": "The Askafroa lives in ash trees in the Scania region of Sweden, and is said to be a very dangerous creature. She is the soul of the tree and if it is cut down, she will exert revenge.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",

      "source_id": "askafroa-s1",
      "quote": "The Swedish scholar Hyltén-Cavallius recorded in his ethnographic work Wärend och Wirdarne a belief of a female creature living in the ash tree, in Ljunit Hundred.       "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sarjana Swedia Hyltén-Cavallius mencatat dalam karya etnografinya Wärend och Wirdarne kepercayaan di Ljunit Hundred tentang makhluk perempuan di pohon ash; para tetua berkorban kepadanya pada pagi Rabu Abu dengan menuang air ke akar pohon sebelum matahari terbit.",
        "en": "The Swedish scholar Hyltén-Cavallius recorded in his ethnographic work Wärend och Wirdarne a belief in Ljunit Hundred in a female being in the ash tree; the elders sacrificed to her on Ash Wednesday morning, pouring water over the roots before sunrise."

  ],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Bäckahästen, \"kuda sungai\", adalah kuda air dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia, terutama Skåne: kuda putih megah yang memikat orang, terutama anak-anak, menungganginya lalu menenggelamkan mereka.",
    "en": "Bäckahästen, the 'brook horse', is a water horse of Scandinavian, especially Scanian, folklore: a majestic white horse that lures people, above all children, onto its back and drowns them.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "b-khesten-c01",
      "b-khesten-c03",
      "b-khesten-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bäckahästen (dari bahasa Swedia bäck, \"sungai kecil\", dan hästen, \"kuda\") dalam kepercayaan rakyat Skåne adalah roh sungai atau Näck dalam wujud kuda. Ia dapat dibandingkan dengan kuda air mitologi Kelt seperti each uisge dari Skotlandia, kelpie, dan ceffyl dŵr dari Wales.",
      "en": "Bäckahästen (from Swedish bäck, 'brook', and hästen, 'the horse') is, in Scanian folk belief, the spirit of a brook or the Näck in horse form. It can be compared with the water horses of Celtic mythology such as the Scottish each uisge, the kelpie and the Welsh ceffyl dŵr.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "b-khesten-c01",
        "b-khesten-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia pemangsa licik yang mengintai di sungai dan danau untuk menyeret penunggang dan kuda lain ke air. Wujudnya kuda jantan gagah yang biasanya putih berkilau, kadang kelabu, hitam, atau kebiruan, dengan mulut penuh taring tajam, dan sering muncul saat berkabut. Siapa yang naik ke punggungnya tak bisa turun lagi, dan punggungnya makin panjang semakin banyak anak yang naik.",
      "en": "It is a treacherous predator lurking in brooks, rivers and lakes to drag riders and other horses into the water. It appears as a fine stallion, usually shining white, sometimes dapple-grey, black or bluish, with a mouth full of sharp fangs, and often shows itself in fog. Whoever climbs onto its back cannot get down, and its back grows longer the more children climb on.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "b-khesten-c02",
        "b-khesten-c03",
        "b-khesten-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Untuk mencegahnya menenggelamkan anak-anak, orang dapat melempar sepotong baja di antara kuda itu dan air, sehingga kekuatannya patah; anak yang menyerukan kata \"salib\" juga membuatnya melempar mereka dan menghilang. Kuda ini juga bisa dipasangi bajak dan bekerja keras sepanjang hari, karena dijinakkan oleh baja pada tali kekangnya.",
      "en": "To stop it drowning children one can throw a piece of steel between the horse and the water, breaking its power; a child calling out the word 'cross' also makes it throw them off and vanish. It can also be put to the plough and work hard all day, tamed by the steel in its harness.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "b-khesten-c03",
        "b-khesten-c04",
        "b-khesten-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "source_id": "b-khesten-s1",
      "quote": "Bäckahästen (schwedisch, von bäck „Bach“ und hästen „das Pferd“) ist im schonischen Volksglauben der Geist eines Baches oder der Näck in Pferdegestalt. Es kann mit den Wasserpferden aus der keltischen Mythologie wie dem schottischen Each Uisge, dem schottisch-irischen Kelpie oder dem walisischen Ceffyl dŵr verglichen werden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bäckahästen (Swedia: bäck, \"sungai kecil\", dan hästen, \"kuda\") dalam kepercayaan rakyat Skåne adalah roh sungai atau Näck dalam wujud kuda, yang dapat dibandingkan dengan kuda air mitologi Kelt seperti each uisge Skotlandia, kelpie, dan ceffyl dŵr Wales.",
        "en": "Bäckahästen (Swedish bäck 'brook' and hästen 'the horse') is in Scanian folk belief a brook spirit or the Näck in horse form, comparable with Celtic water horses such as the Scottish each uisge, the kelpie and the Welsh ceffyl dŵr."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "b-khesten-c02",
      "source_id": "b-khesten-s1",
      "quote": "Das Bäckahäst ist ein hinterhältiges Raubtier, das an Bächen, Flüssen und Seen lauert, wo es Reiter und andere Pferde ins Wasser zieht, um sie zu ertränken. Es zeigt sich als stattlicher Hengst, meist weiß schimmernd, aber sein Fell kann auch apfelgrau, schwarz oder blau changieren. Sein Maul ist voller scharfer Fangzähne.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bäckahäst adalah pemangsa licik yang mengintai di sungai dan danau untuk menyeret penunggang dan kuda lain ke air dan menenggelamkannya; ia tampak sebagai kuda jantan gagah yang biasanya putih berkilau, kadang kelabu belang, hitam, atau kebiruan, dengan mulut penuh taring tajam.",
        "en": "The bäckahäst is a treacherous predator lurking by brooks, rivers and lakes, dragging riders and other horses into the water to drown them; it appears as a fine stallion, usually shimmering white, sometimes dapple-grey, black or blue, its mouth full of sharp fangs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "b-khesten-c03",
      "source_id": "b-khesten-s1",
      "quote": "Die Anzahl der Kinder spielt dabei keine Rolle, da der Pferderücken immer länger wird, je mehr Kinder sich auf das Untier setzen. Wenn man ein Bäckahäst daran hindern will, Kinder zu ertränken, soll man ein Stück Stahl zwischen das Pferd und das Wasser werfen. ... Eine andere Möglichkeit, dem Untier zu entkommen, besteht darin, dass ein Kind das christliche Wort „Kreuz“ ausruft",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jumlah anak tak jadi soal karena punggung kuda makin panjang semakin banyak anak yang naik; untuk mencegahnya menenggelamkan anak, orang melempar sepotong baja di antara kuda dan air, atau seorang anak menyerukan kata Kristen \"salib\".",
        "en": "The number of children does not matter, as the horse's back grows longer the more climb on; to stop it drowning children, throw a piece of steel between horse and water, or a child may cry out the Christian word 'cross'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "b-khesten-c04",
      "source_id": "b-khesten-s2",
      "quote": "Le bäckahäst, bäckahästen ou bækhesten (littéralement « cheval des ruisseaux ») est un cheval fantastique issu du folklore scandinave, très proche du kelpie. Il s'agit d'un majestueux cheval blanc qui apparait près des rivières, en particulier par temps de brouillard. Celui qui monte sur son dos se trouve incapable d'en descendre.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bäckahäst, bäckahästen, atau bækhesten (harfiah \"kuda sungai kecil\") adalah kuda khayalan cerita rakyat Skandinavia yang sangat mirip kelpie: kuda putih megah yang muncul dekat sungai, terutama saat berkabut, dan siapa yang naik ke punggungnya tak bisa turun.",
        "en": "The bäckahäst, bäckahästen or bækhesten ('brook horse') is a fantastic horse of Scandinavian folklore very close to the kelpie: a majestic white horse appearing near rivers, especially in fog, and whoever mounts it cannot get off."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "b-khesten-c05",
      "source_id": "b-khesten-s3",
      "quote": "Bækhesten eller Bäckahästen (fra svensk: bäckhästen) er et mytologisk dyr i nordisk folklore og skånsk folketro, svarende til nøkken, blot i hesteform. Hesten er den skandinaviske udgave af den keltiske Kelpie",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bækhesten atau Bäckahästen adalah hewan mitos dalam cerita rakyat Nordik dan kepercayaan Skåne, padanan nøkken dalam wujud kuda, dan versi Skandinavia dari kelpie Kelt.",
        "en": "Bækhesten or Bäckahästen is a mythical animal of Nordic folklore and Scanian belief, the nøkk in horse form, and the Scandinavian version of the Celtic kelpie."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "b-khesten-c06",
      "source_id": "b-khesten-s1",
      "quote": "Im schwedischen Volksglauben wird weiter berichtet, dass das Bäckahäst einem Bauern erlaubte, es vor den Pflug zu spannen ... Der Hengst war durch den Stahl im Pferdegeschirr während der Tagesarbeit gebändigt worden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut kepercayaan Swedia, bäckahäst pernah membiarkan seorang petani memasangnya pada bajak; kuda jantan itu jinak sepanjang hari kerja karena baja pada tali kekangnya.",
        "en": "In Swedish belief the bäckahäst let a farmer harness it to the plough; the stallion was tamed through the working day by the steel in its harness."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Naga Jermanik, disebut wyrm, ormr, atau wurm (\"ular\"), adalah naga dalam mitologi dan cerita rakyat Jermanik yang sering digambarkan sebagai ular besar berbisa penimbun emas.",
    "en": "The Germanic dragon, called wyrm, ormr or wurm ('serpent'), is the dragon of Germanic mythology and folklore, often portrayed as a large venomous snake hoarding gold.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "germanic-dragon-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Worm, wurm, atau wyrm (bahasa Inggris Kuno wyrm, Nordik Kuno ormr, Jerman Hulu Kuno wurm), yang berarti \"ular\", adalah istilah kuno untuk naga dalam mitologi dan cerita rakyat Jermanik yang lebih luas. Makhluk ini sering digambarkan sebagai ular besar berbisa dan penimbun emas, meski dalam kisah-kisah yang lebih belakangan ia berbagi banyak ciri dengan naga Eropa lain, misalnya bersayap.",
      "en": "The old words worm, wurm and wyrm (Old English wyrm, Old Norse ormr, Old High German wurm) mean 'serpent' and were used for dragons across Germanic myth and folklore. These beasts usually appear as huge poisonous snakes guarding gold, although later stories lend them traits common to other European dragons, wings among them.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "germanic-dragon-c01",
        "germanic-dragon-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Cacing-naga terkenal dalam karya Jermanik abad pertengahan antara lain naga yang membunuh Beowulf, Fáfnir dalam siklus Völsung, Nidhogg, dan ular dunia Jǫrmungandr, dengan subkategori seperti lindworm dan ular laut. Dalam satu uraian, naga Jermanik dibedakan dari gambaran naga biasa: ia tidak bertubuh bersisik berkaki empat dan bersayap kelelawar, tetapi berbentuk ular raksasa tanpa kaki atau sayap.",
      "en": "Famous worms in medieval Germanic works include the dragon that killed Beowulf, Fáfnir of the Völsung Cycle, Nidhogg and the world serpent Jǫrmungandr, with subtypes such as lindworms and sea serpents. In contrast to the usual picture of a scaly, four-legged dragon with bat wings, the Germanic dragon has the form of a gigantic snake without legs or wings.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "germanic-dragon-c02",
        "germanic-dragon-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kaitan naga dengan timbunan harta tersebar luas dalam sastra Jermanik. Dalam siklus Völsung, Fáfnir berubah menjadi naga untuk menjaga dan mengerami timbunan harta, termasuk cincin Andvaranaut, sampai dibunuh pahlawan Sigurd dengan pedang Gram; dalam Beowulf, naga terbangun dari gundukan kuburnya ketika sebuah piala dari timbunan hartanya dicuri, lalu menuntut balas kepada orang Geat.",
      "en": "The link between dragons and treasure hoards is widespread in Germanic literature. In the Völsung Cycle Fáfnir turns into a dragon to guard and brood over a hoard including the ring Andvaranaut, until the hero Sigurd kills him with the sword Gram; in Beowulf the dragon wakes in its burial mound when a cup is stolen from its hoard and seeks vengeance on the Geats.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "germanic-dragon-c01",
      "source_id": "germanic-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "Worm, wurm or wyrm (Old English: wyrm ; Old Norse: ormr ; Old High German: wurm ), meaning serpent, are archaic terms for dragons (Old English: draca ; Old Norse: dreki/*draki ; Old High German: trahho ) in the wider Germanic mythology and folklore, in which they are often portrayed as large venomous snakes and hoarders of gold.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Worm, wurm, atau wyrm (Inggris Kuno wyrm, Nordik Kuno ormr, Jerman Hulu Kuno wurm), berarti \"ular\", adalah istilah kuno untuk naga dalam mitologi dan cerita rakyat Jermanik, yang sering digambarkan sebagai ular besar berbisa dan penimbun emas.",
        "en": "Worm, wurm or wyrm (Old English wyrm, Old Norse ormr, Old High German wurm), meaning serpent, are archaic terms for dragons in Germanic mythology and folklore, often shown as large venomous snakes hoarding gold."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "germanic-dragon-c02",
      "source_id": "germanic-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "Prominent worms attested in medieval Germanic works include the dragon that killed Beowulf, the central dragon in the Völsung Cycle – Fáfnir, Nidhogg (Old Norse: Níðhǫggr ), and the great world serpent, Jǫrmungandr, including subcategories such as lindworms and sea serpents.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cacing-naga terkenal dalam karya Jermanik abad pertengahan antara lain naga pembunuh Beowulf, Fáfnir dalam siklus Völsung, Nidhogg, dan ular dunia Jǫrmungandr, termasuk subkategori seperti lindworm dan ular laut.",
        "en": "Prominent worms in medieval Germanic works include the dragon that killed Beowulf, Fáfnir of the Völsung Cycle, Nidhogg and the world serpent Jǫrmungandr, with subcategories such as lindworms and sea serpents."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "germanic-dragon-c03",
      "source_id": "germanic-dragon-s2",
      "quote": "In Germaanske draak is in mytysk bist ôfkomstich út 'e mytology en folkloare fan 'e Germaanske folken, dat kwa skaaimerken sterk ôfwykt fan hoe't in draak ornaris foarsteld wurdt. Ynstee fan in mei skobben bepânsere lichem mei fjouwer poaten, in lange sturt en twa flearmûseftige wjukken op 'e rêch hat in Germaanske draak de foarm fan in gigantyske slange, sûnder poaten of wjukken.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga Jermanik adalah hewan mitos dari mitologi dan cerita rakyat bangsa Jermanik yang cirinya sangat berbeda dari gambaran naga biasa: bukannya bertubuh bersisik berkaki empat, berekor panjang, dan bersayap kelelawar, ia berbentuk ular raksasa tanpa kaki atau sayap.",
        "en": "The Germanic dragon (Germaanske draak) is a mythical beast of Germanic peoples' mythology and folklore that differs sharply from the usual dragon: instead of a scaly four-legged body, long tail and bat wings, it has the form of a gigantic snake (gigantyske slange) without legs (poaten) or wings (wjukken)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "germanic-dragon-c04",
      "source_id": "germanic-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "The association between dragons and hoards of treasure is widespread in Germanic literature ... In the Völsung Cycle, Fáfnir , upon claiming a hoard of treasure, including the ring Andvaranaut, transforms into a dragon to protect and brood over it.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kaitan naga dengan timbunan harta tersebar luas dalam sastra Jermanik; dalam siklus Völsung, Fáfnir yang menguasai timbunan harta, termasuk cincin Andvaranaut, berubah menjadi naga untuk menjaga dan mengeraminya.",
        "en": "The link between dragons and treasure hoards is widespread in Germanic literature; in the Völsung Cycle Fáfnir, having claimed a hoard including the ring Andvaranaut, becomes a dragon to guard and brood over it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "germanic-dragon-c05",
      "source_id": "germanic-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "Fáfnir's brother, Regin, reforges the sword Gram from broken shards, and gives it to the hero Sigurd, who uses it to kill the dragon ... In Beowulf, the dragon slain by the poem's eponymous hero, is awoken from the burial mound in which it dwells when a cup from its hoard is stolen, leading it to seek vengeance from the Geats.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Regin, saudara Fáfnir, menempa ulang pedang Gram dan memberikannya kepada pahlawan Sigurd yang memakainya untuk membunuh naga itu; dalam Beowulf, naga terbangun dari gundukan kuburnya ketika sebuah piala dari hartanya dicuri dan menuntut balas kepada orang Geat.",
        "en": "Fáfnir's brother Regin reforges the sword Gram for the hero Sigurd, who kills the dragon with it; in Beowulf the dragon wakes in its burial mound when a cup is stolen from its hoard and seeks vengeance on the Geats."
